# LumenAiry Deep Audit -- v5.17.0 (HEAD 71f9715) -- 2026-07-01

**Status: AUDIT ONLY -- no fixes applied.** Every finding below is a work item
for later implementation; the *Fix direction* lines are suggestions, not commitments.

## Methodology

- **Pass 1 (find):** 21 scoped finder agents, one per subsystem/dimension, weighted
  toward the recent delta (v5.16.1 memory estimator/guardrail, v5.17.0 row-band lens
  mode + memory root-cause fixes, the tilted-ASM dtype fix) and the recently graduated
  `elements/eme` + `elements/bor` subpackages; plus cross-cutting memory-leak,
  performance, and test-quality sweeps over the whole package.
- **Pass 2 (verify):** every deduplicated finding went to a refute-first verifier that
  located the cited code, actively tried to refute the claim (guards elsewhere, callers
  preventing the state, documentation making it intended, existing test coverage), ran a
  minimal probe where cheaply checkable, and re-graded severity. Verdicts:
  CONFIRMED / REFUTED. Only CONFIRMED findings appear in the main body; the two
  refuted candidates are in the appendix for the record.
- Documented limitations were excluded by instruction (a documented limitation is not a
  finding unless the documentation is wrong or a promised guard is missing).
- 90 agents total; probes run under Windows CPython 3.14 / numpy 2.4.4 in C:/tmp.

## Statistics

- Raw findings: 122; after dedup: 116; **confirmed: 114**, refuted: 2.
- By severity (re-graded by verifiers): **P1: 8**, **P2: 42**, **P3: 64**
- By category: gap: 42, consistency: 35, memory: 15, physics: 13, perf: 9

## Executive summary

**114 confirmed findings (8 P1, 42 P2, 64 P3), every one adversarially verified** — the
verifiers located the cited code, tried to refute each claim, and ran a reproduction probe
wherever cheaply checkable; the two candidates that did not survive are in the appendix.
Severities were re-graded by the verifiers, not the finders.

**What held up.** The headline v5.17.0 claim — the now-default row-band lens path being
byte-identical to the whole-grid path — **survived scrutiny** (probes reproduced
`np.array_equal` outputs; the findings against the chunked path concern lost *diagnostics*
and estimator/knob consistency, not numerics). The solver eigen-cores (pmm/rcwa/eme/bor
physics kernels), the GATE-4 BOR diffraction correspondence, and the v5.14.x P1 fixes in
RCWA all re-verified intact. As in prior deep audits, the physics cores are sound; the
risk lives at the boundaries.

**The eight P1s** (each probe-confirmed, silent-wrong-answer or hard-failure class):

1. **[P1-01]** BOR flux normalization uses an *absolute dimensional* threshold (`1e-10`) —
   the same structure expressed in meters instead of microns silently returns
   energy ≈ 0.5–0.99 garbage T (probe: energy 0.678…0.500 across modes, no warning).
2. **[P1-02]** `bruggeman()` in `emt.py` has a wrong linear coefficient in its quadratic —
   the returned eps violates the defining Bruggeman self-consistency equation (hand
   re-derivation + probe); the existing test only checks a symmetry the wrong formula
   also satisfies.
3. **[P1-03]** The v5.14.1 RCWA gain/evanescent-incidence P1 guard was never mirrored into
   1-D PMM: a `1e-6` gain superstrate silently returns `sum(T) = -12.5`.
4. **[P1-04]** `PMMStack`/`PMM2DStack` never invalidate `self._internal` on re-solve —
   `internal_field()`/`layer_absorption()` silently serve the *previous* wavelength's
   fields after `set_source(...)` + `solve()`.
5. **[P1-05]** `pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered` also lacks the incidence guard (the hybrid
   raises; the staggered silently returns `sum(T) = -144.6`).
6. **[P1-06]** LG/HG mode-stack cache keys omit the grid *origin* — a shifted same-shape
   grid gets a cache hit and `decompose_lg` returns ~27%-wrong overlaps silently.
7. **[P1-07]** Ray-trace fixed-index glass registration keys on `id(elem)` — CPython id
   recycling retargets previously built surface lists to the wrong refractive index
   (probe: 198/200 sweep lists resolved to the wrong n).
8. **[P1-08]** The GUI wave-optics worker imports the pre-v5.14-reorg module path
   `lumenairy.propagation` — every GUI wave-optics run dies and the dock hangs.

**Cross-cutting themes** (use these to batch the implementation):

- **Guard-mirroring drift is the #1 recurring pattern (~15 findings).** A fix or guard
  added to one sibling was never propagated: RCWA's incidence guard vs 1-D PMM /
  staggered-2-D / the JAX twins (P1-03, P1-05, P2-10, P2-11, P2-14); the fail-loud
  surface-type guard in `trace_jax` vs `trace_jax_with_params` (P2-34); `semi_diameter`
  honored by JAX but not NumPy tracing (P2-35); `PMM2DStack` validates thickness,
  `PMMStack` doesn't (P3-30); the close-guard applied only to one GUI dock (P2-38).
  A systematic sibling-parity sweep when fixing will close these cheaply.
- **Cache lifecycle / key-completeness (~12 findings).** Beyond P1-06/P1-07: unbounded
  `_HOMOG_CACHE` across sweeps (P2-16/17), `_PreparedPMMStack` eig caches (P3-32),
  glass.py caches unenrolled *and* invisible to the enrollment meta-pin due to a
  case-sensitive filter (P3-40 + P2-42), `user_library` re-points the registry without
  invalidating caches (P2-41), wrapper-merit meshgrid keyed on aperture value (P2-25),
  eme/pmm JAX static caches growing per geometry (P3-16, P3-28).
- **Recent-delta consistency (v5.16.1/v5.17.0) — fix before the next release.** The
  chunked default silently drops the freeform-departure warning (P2-04);
  `sag_chunk_rows=0` is re-enabled to AUTO inside the traced amp legs (P2-05); the
  estimator ignores `parallel_amp` on the chunked path (P2-22); `set_low_memory(False)`
  does not restore the aggressive dtype (P2-23); `set_max_ram` is bypassed by the batch
  planners (P2-21); `snapshot_fft_state` misses the new setter-backed globals so spawned
  workers lose them (P3-54); the new kwargs are undocumented (P3-06).
- **Zemax IO trust boundary.** Unknown SURFTYPEs are silently parsed as EVENASPH —
  the exact bug family of the just-fixed off-by-one, one level up (P2-19); the exporter
  drops aspheric coefficients on mirrors (P2-20); the txt loader drops aspherics entirely
  while claiming interchangeability (P3-43).
- **Physics-convention drift (docs vs code vs siblings).** Zernike 'Noll' fabricates a
  sign convention that matches no published one (P2-02); `apply_waveplate` retardance
  sign is opposite the library's own Berreman/RCWA solvers (P2-15); GBD ABCD amplitude
  uses the wrong Collins factor (P2-30); `RCWAStack(symmetry=True)` stores
  recentering-gauge per-order amplitudes (P2-18).
- **Real performance wins found**: `angular_spectrum_propagate_batch` is measured 2.07×
  *slower* than two scalar calls, contradicting its docstring (P2-27); the tilted-ASM
  4-shift idiom misses the 2-shift fold already applied to plain ASM (P2-28, ~19%);
  `retain_internal` rebuilds an O(n²) Redheffer chain (P2-12).

**Suggested implementation order:** (1) the 8 P1s; (2) the recent-delta cluster while
v5.17.0 context is fresh; (3) the guard-mirroring sweep (mechanical, high value); (4) cache
lifecycle cluster + registry enrollment (and fix the meta-pin filter so it stays closed);
(5) conventions/docs/perf at leisure. Several findings note non-discriminating tests
alongside the code defect (P1-02, P3-64, P2-42) — fix the test together with the code so
the regression stays caught.

### Post-audit note (7b82f9c landed mid-audit)

The audit launched at HEAD `71f9715`; commit `7b82f9c` ("V14 live-forward for
`_PYFFTW_DOUBLE_BUFFER` + estimator recalibration, v5.17.1") was committed to this branch
while the finders were running. Reconciliation against the findings:

- **P3-54 still stands** (re-verified at post-7b82f9c HEAD): `7b82f9c` added
  `_PYFFTW_DOUBLE_BUFFER` to `propagation._LIVE_FORWARD_NAMES` (in-process attribute
  reads), but `fft_infra._FFT_STATE_KEYS` / `snapshot_fft_state` — the *spawned-worker*
  mechanism P3-54 targets — still omits `_PYFFTW_DOUBLE_BUFFER`,
  `PYFFTW_FALLBACK_ON_ERROR`, `USE_SCIPY_FFT`, the plan-cache size, and the ASM cache
  bounds.
- The estimator-anchor recalibration in `7b82f9c` changes *constants*, not logic; the
  structural estimator/guardrail findings (P2-21, P2-22, P2-23, P3-45, P3-46) are
  unaffected, though `lumenairy/memory.py` line numbers may have drifted by a few lines.
- All other findings are in files `7b82f9c` did not touch.

## P1 findings (wrong results / corruption / leak on a mainline path)

### [P1-01] Absolute flux threshold 1e-10 in mode normalization makes BORStack.solve silently return wrong R/T for SI-unit (meter-scale) inputs

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/bor/zcascade.py:82`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _layer_modes_staggered normalizes each mode to unit z-flux only when abs(P) > 1e-10 (an ABSOLUTE, dimensional threshold; P scales as Rbig*h ~ Rbig^2/N times the unit-norm eigenvector). If the flux falls below it, the code silently falls back to field-norm normalization, after which |S|^2 is no longer a power fraction, so R, T and energy from BORStack.solve are wrong. For Rbig ~ 1e-5 m (the same structure expressed in meters instead of microns) the propagating-mode flux is ~1e-13, so EVERY mode takes the fallback. Same threshold duplicated in bor_solve.py:38.

**Failure scenario.** BORStack(Rbig=8.0, m=1, N=80, n_sup=1.0, n_sub=1.5), k0=2.0 (micron units): energy = 1.0 for all 9 incident modes. Identical physics in SI meters (Rbig=8e-6, k0=2e6): same 9 propagating modes are found, but energy = [0.68, 0.69, 0.66, 0.72, 0.63, 0.78, 0.57, 0.99, 0.50] — R and T are silently wrong power fractions, no warning or raise.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_zcascade_f1.py) reproduced the claim exactly. Micron units (Rbig=8.0, k0=2.0, m=1, N=80, n_sub=1.5): energy = [1,1,1,1,1,1,1,1,1] over 9 incident modes. Same physics in SI meters (Rbig=8e-6, k0=2e6): same 9 propagating modes, R identical ([0.0407..0.2571], masking the bug), but T = [0.637, 0.653, 0.615, 0.688, 0.568, 0.768, 0.472, 0.989, 0.243] giving energy = [0.678, 0.690, 0.662, 0.717, 0.628, 0.783, 0.570, 0.989, 0.500] — silent, no warning/raise. Mechanism pinned: post-normalization propagating-mode |z-flux| = 1.000 (micron) vs 7.87e-14..3.72e-13 (SI) — every SI mode took the field-norm fallback at zcascade.py:82 because raw |P| ~ Rbig^2/(2N) ~ 4e-13 < 1e-10 (absolute dimensional threshold). Identical threshold duplicated at bor_solve.py:38 (_flux_normalize).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for unit documentation or guards: BORStack/zcascade docstrings and lumenairy/elements/bor/__init__.py never pin a unit system — the API takes raw k0/Rbig and Maxwell is scale-invariant, so meter-scale input is physically valid. No warning path, no raise, no test at non-micron scale (test_zcascade.py and BORStack examples all use O(1) dimensionless/micron values, where |P|~0.03-0.4 clears the threshold). Checked whether the fallback could accidentally preserve power fractions: it cannot — field-norm scaling leaves per-mode fluxes unequal (7.9e-14..3.7e-13, spanning 4.7x), so |S|^2 is not a power fraction; the probe's wrong T confirms it. R coming out identical (superstrate-only amplitude ratios) makes the corruption harder to notice, strengthening rather than refuting the finding.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Make the normalization threshold relative to the mode's own scale, e.g. abs(P) > tol * sum((|Er|^2+|Ephi|^2) * r * h) (in both zcascade.py:82 and bor_solve.py:38), or normalize flux in dimensionless r/Rbig coordinates; optionally warn when the evanescent fallback fires for a mode later classified as propagating.

### [P1-02] bruggeman() uses a wrong linear coefficient in its quadratic, returning eps that violates the Bruggeman self-consistency equation everywhere except accidental points

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/emt.py:200`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Clearing denominators of f(ea-e)/(ea+g e) + (1-f)(eb-e)/(eb+g e) = 0 gives g e^2 + b e - ea*eb = 0 with b = ((1-f) - g f)*ea + (f - g(1-f))*eb. The code (line 200) instead sets b = (g f - 1)*ea + (g(1-f) - 1)*eb, which is not algebraically equal for any g in {1,2} and general f. The returned value therefore does not satisfy the defining Bruggeman equation. The unit test test_mixing_rules_properties only checks phase-swap symmetry at f=0.5, which the wrong b also satisfies (b = (g/2-1)(ea+eb) is symmetric) - a non-discriminating test.

**Failure scenario.** bruggeman(4.0, 4.0, 0.5) returns 2.828 (sphere) / 6.472 (cylinder) instead of the trivially-required 4.0. Dilute limit bruggeman(9.0, 2.0, 0.001) returns 5.218 instead of ~2.0 (should approach the majority phase). Any user homogenizing a 2-phase composite (e.g. porous film, nanocomposite AR layer) via this exported mixing rule gets an effective permittivity off by 10-160%, silently, and feeds it into Berreman/RCWA sweeps.

**Verification evidence.** lumenairy/elements/emt.py line 200. Hand re-derivation: clearing denominators of f(ea-e)/(ea+g e)+(1-f)(eb-e)/(eb+g e)=0 gives g e^2 + b e - ea*eb = 0 with b = ((1-f)-g f)*ea + (f-g(1-f))*eb; for g=2 that is (1-3f)ea+(3f-2)eb, matching the standard 3-D Bruggeman closed form e=[(3f-1)ea+(2-3f)eb+sqrt(...)]/4. Code has b=(g f-1)ea+(g(1-f)-1)eb, which for g=2 collapses to (2f-1)(ea-eb) - vanishes at f=0.5 for ANY ea,eb and is wrong even at f=0/f=1. Probe run against the repo: bruggeman(4,4,0.5,'sphere')=2.82843 (must be 4.0), self-consistency residual 0.1213; 'cylinder'=6.47214, residual -0.2361; bruggeman(9,2,0.001)=5.21785 (dilute limit must be ~2.003; correct root 2.00323 has residual 1e-17); bruggeman(9,2,0.3)=3.78058 residual -0.03581 vs correct 3.34255 residual 0. Test tests/unit/test_v5_14_5_emt_and_berreman_jax.py:94-104 only asserts phase-swap symmetry at f=0.5 - the wrong b=(2f-1)(ea-eb) is symmetric there by construction, so the test is non-discriminating exactly as claimed; the constituent-bounds assertion is applied only to maxwell_garnett, not bruggeman.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Re-derived the quadratic by hand and cross-checked against the textbook 3-D Bruggeman closed form (b=(3f-1)ea+(2-3f)eb at depolarization 1/3) - code disagrees. Searched the repo for any other bruggeman definition, wrapper, or downstream correction (only emt.py defines/exports it). Checked the unit tests for a discriminating case - only the f=0.5 symmetry check exists, which the buggy coefficient passes by construction. Checked whether ea=eb could be special-cased anywhere - it is not; probe returns 2.828 for ea=eb=4. No refutation found; every claimed repro number reproduced exactly.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace line 200 with b = ((1.0-f) - g*f)*ea + (f - g*(1.0-f))*eb and add a discriminating test (self-consistency residual ~0, ea=eb identity, dilute limit approaches majority phase).

### [P1-03] 1-D PMM has no gain/evanescent incidence-medium guard (RCWA's audit-P1 `_require_propagating_incidence` has no PMM mirror): a gain superstrate silently zeroes R and returns negative T

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/_core.py:680`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Every 1-D PMM far-field path normalizes by `kz_inc = float(np.real(_kz_forward(eps_sup, ...)))` (_core.py:680 scalar, :1184 Jones) with only an `abs(kz_inc) < 1e-9` grazing raise. For a gain superstrate (public n = n - ik, the classic sign-convention slip; even Im = -1e-6), `_kz_forward` flips to the Im>=0 root whose REAL part is negative, so kz_inc < 0 negates every efficiency: R is masked to zero (Re(kz_sup) < 0) and T comes back negative. A gain SUBSTRATE symmetrically zeroes T and inflates R (measured sum R = 21.6). A lossy superstrate (Im(n_sup) > 0) yields R+T = 1.0075 undocumented, and under the default stabilize=True the >1 total makes every degree 'non-passive', ending in a RuntimeError that falsely blames a 'high-degree resonance band'. RCWA graded and fixed exactly this as P1 2026-06-10 (rcwa/_core.py:461-489) and its docstring documents the lossy-incidence scope; the PMM 1-D entry points (pmm_efficiency_1d, pmm_jones_1d, all slanted/covariant/segments variants) have neither the guard nor the documentation.

**Failure scenario.** pmm_efficiency_1d(1e-6, 2.0, 1.0, 1.5, 1.0-1e-6j, 0.3e-6, 0.5, 0.6e-6, polarization='te', degree=10, stabilize=False) -> sum R = 0.000000, sum T = -20.63 (lossless reference: R=0.047, T=0.953) with NO raise and NO warning; same silent negation on pmm_jones_1d (sum T = -1.91) and the covariant slanted path (sum T = -2.99). RCWA raises ValueError on the identical input.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/pmm_verify_gain_guard.py, py -P): pmm_efficiency_1d(wl=1e-6, P=2e-6, n_r=2.0, n_g=1.0, n_sub=1.5, n_sup=1.0-1e-6j, depth=0.3e-6, duty=0.5, te, degree=10, stabilize=False) -> sumR=0.000000, sumT=-12.519261, warns=0 (lossless ref: sumR=0.076276, sumT=0.922923). Gain SUBSTRATE (n_sub=1.5-1e-6j): sumR=13.528735, sumT=0.000000. pmm_jones_1d same inputs: sumT=-1.860615, warns=0. pmm_efficiency_1d_slanted (slant 20 deg, covariant): sumT=-13.341342, warns=0. rcwa_efficiency_1d on identical input RAISES 'ValueError: gain incidence medium (Im(n_superstrate) < 0 ...)'. Lossy superstrate Im=0.05: tot=1.0131 (stabilize=False, no warning); with DEFAULT stabilize=True it raises 'RuntimeError: no resonance-free solve in degrees [10, 26); ... high-degree resonance band' — a misdiagnosis, exactly as claimed. Code trace matches: _core.py:680 kz_inc=Re(_kz_forward(eps_sup,...)); _kz_forward (:598-602) flips to Im>=0 root whose Re<0 for Im(eps_sup)<0; only guard is |kz_inc|<1e-9 grazing raise (:681); R zeroed by Re(kz_sup)>1e-12 mask (:693), T negated by negative kz_inc. Jones twin at :621-640/655. Notably the 2-D PMM paths DO guard (twod.py:593, twod_jones.py:442, stack2d.py:465, _jax_stack2d.py:160 all call rcwa._require_propagating_incidence) — the 1-D omission is an internal PMM inconsistency, not just a missing RCWA mirror.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Grepped the whole pmm package for _require_propagating_incidence / gain / imag guards: only 2-D paths import it; oned.py and stack.py have none (stack.py:44 has only a one-sided R+T>1 gain WARNING). Checked oned.py entry points for a conjugation/loss-convention bridge or documented lossy/gain-incidence scope: docstrings pin 'Im(eps)>0 for loss' but say nothing about the incidence medium; no Im check on _C(n_superstrate). Checked whether the stabilize scan or the Wood-anomaly nudge would catch it: neither does (probe shows warns=0 with stabilize=True). Ran the lossy-superstrate case at Im=1e-3 (tot=0.9994, benign) to check the claim wasn't overstated — it only misleads at larger Im, but the Im=0.05 RuntimeError reproduces the claimed false 'resonance band' blame.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Call rcwa's _require_propagating_incidence (already imported by the 2-D PMM paths) at the 1-D PMM entry points / shared kz_inc computation, and document the lossy-incidence scope as rcwa_efficiency_1d does.

### [P1-04] Stale self._internal survives re-solve: internal_field/layer_absorption silently return the PREVIOUS solve's fields after the source or geometry changes

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/stack.py:737`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** solve() writes self._internal only inside the retain_internal branch and NEVER invalidates it at the start of a solve. After solve(retain_internal=True), any later set_source(...)+solve() (without retain) leaves the old _internal (with 'cinc') intact, so internal_field() and layer_absorption() pass their only guard ('cinc' in d) and return fields/absorption for the OLD wavelength/angle while solve() just returned R/T for the NEW one. RCWA avoids this by binding internal data to a returned RCWAResult object; PMM binds it to the mutable stack. PMM2DStack.solve (stack2d.py line 577) has the identical defect.

**Failure scenario.** st.solve(retain_internal=True) at 550 nm; st.internal_field(z) OK. Then st.set_source(750e-9); st.solve(); st.internal_field(z) silently returns the 550 nm field (probe: max|stale - true 750 nm field| = 0.81 while R changed). layer_absorption() likewise returns the 550 nm attribution. Any wavelength/angle scan that inspects fields per point produces wrong data for every point after the first unless the user remembers retain_internal=True on EVERY solve.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_pmm_stack_findings.py, py -P): 1-layer segmented lossy stack, solve(retain_internal=True)@550nm then set_source(750nm)+solve() (no retain): internal_field(0.15um) is byte-identical to the 550nm field (np.allclose True); max|stale - independent true 750nm field| = 1.199 while R_tot changed 0.0581->0.0868. layer_absorption() after the 750nm solve returns the 550nm values [0.1990, 0.2635] vs true 750nm [0.1323, 0.1619] -- silent. Geometry change (add_layer then solve()) leaves stale lmodes vs new _layers: internal_field raises IndexError. Code trace: solve() (stack.py:525) has no invalidation at entry; _internal only written at :737/:824 inside the retain branch; set_source (:471) and add_layer (:190) never clear it; the guard at :975/:1164 only checks 'cinc' in d. PMM2DStack.solve (stack2d.py:577/:639) has the identical structure with no invalidation.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Grepped whole pmm/ package for any '_internal = None' / del / invalidation hook: none exists (attribute is only ever created, never cleared). Checked set_source, add_layer, prepare paths: none touch _internal. Grepped tests/unit (8 files using retain_internal): no test exercises a retain-then-non-retain re-solve, so no coverage masks it. Checked docstrings (:937, :1140): they say 'requires a PRIOR solve(retain_internal=True)', which if anything encourages the failing pattern. RCWA comparison holds: RCWA binds internals to the returned RCWAResult, PMM binds to the mutable stack.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Invalidate (self._internal = None) at solve() entry in both stack.py and stack2d.py so only the just-completed retain_internal=True solve can serve internal_field/layer_absorption.

### [P1-05] pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered has no incidence guards: gain/evanescent superstrate silently returns wildly wrong efficiencies (hybrid raises)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/twod_staggered.py:883`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Unlike pmm_efficiency_2d / pmm_efficiency_2d_cell / pmm_jones_2d, the staggered entry point never calls rcwa._core._require_propagating_incidence (it imports only _grazing_safe_wavelength at line 893). eps_sup = n_superstrate**2 at line 883 flows unguarded into kz_inc = Re(_kz_forward2(eps_sup,...)) (line 993) which normalizes every efficiency. An infinitesimally 'gain' superstrate (Im(n_sup) < 0, e.g. a -1e-9 rounding artifact from a fitted index) flips the forward root so kz_inc < 0 and every T is negated; a metallic/evanescent superstrate divides by kz_inc ~ 0. This is the exact discontinuity-at-Im(n)=0- failure the v5.14.1 RCWA audit graded P1 and fixed suite-wide -- the staggered solver was left out.

**Failure scenario.** pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered(1.2, 1.2, [[4,1],[1,1]], 1.5, n_superstrate=1-1e-6j, 0.3, 1.0) returns sum(R)+sum(T) = -144.98 with min(T) = -36.6, no warning, no raise. With n_superstrate=0.1+3j (evanescent incidence) it returns sum(R)+sum(T) = 35.1 silently. The hybrid pmm_efficiency_2d_cell raises ValueError in both cases.

**Verification evidence.** Reproduced by probe (py -P C:/tmp/verify_stag_probe.py). pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered(1.2um,1.2um,[[4,1],[1,1]],1.5,n_sup,0.3um,1.0um,degree=5,n_orders=3): n_sup=1-1e-6j -> sum(R)=0.0, sum(T)=-144.635, min(T)=-36.72, tot=-144.635, NO warning, NO raise; n_sup=0.1+3j -> tot=+41.19 silently. Identical call to hybrid pmm_efficiency_2d_cell raises ValueError 'gain incidence medium' / 'non-propagating' respectively. Sanity baseline n_sup=1.0 gives tot=0.99978. Code trace: eps_sup=n_superstrate**2 (line 883) flows unguarded to kz_inc=Re(_kz_forward2(eps_sup,kx0,ky0)) (line 993); _kz_forward2 flips the root when Im<0, making kz_inc<0 -> every T negated while the kz_ref>0 mask zeroes all R. Grep confirms _require_propagating_incidence is called by every other efficiency entry point (rcwa oned.py:468/1105/1288, twod.py:644/838/1196/1493, stack.py:1748, pmm twod.py:593, twod_jones.py:442, stack2d.py:465, _jax_stack2d.py:160) but never in twod_staggered.py, which imports only _grazing_safe_wavelength (line 893). No wrapping facade adds the guard (bare re-export in lumenairy/__init__.py:468). Fix-note caveat verified: staggered uses PUBLIC eps (module docstring line 54, 'Im eps > 0 for loss'), so the internal-convention guard needs np.conj(eps_sup).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for an alternative guard in the staggered path (only the Wood-anomaly _gap warning at lines 904-914 exists; it checks Rayleigh-cutoff distance, not gain/evanescent incidence — probe confirmed zero warnings emitted). Checked whether the R-orders mask (line 1002) neutralizes the failure — no, T stays large-negative. Checked test_v5_12_0_pmm2d_staggered.py test_validation — covers only polarization/degree/ndim, not incidence. Checked lumenairy/__init__ and pmm/__init__ for a validating wrapper — bare re-export.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Call _require_propagating_incidence('pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered', np.conj(eps_sup), kx0**2+ky0**2) at the entry point (conjugating the public-convention eps to the guard's internal convention), matching the suite-wide v5.14.1 fix.

### [P1-06] LG/HG mode-stack cache key omits the grid ORIGIN, so same-shape/same-pitch grids at different offsets collide and decompose_lg/decompose_hg silently return wrong overlaps

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/asymptotic_modes.py:311`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _lg_mode_conj_stack's cache_key (asymptotic_modes.py:311-315) is (p_max, ell_max, Ny, Nx, w, cx, cy, dx, dy, dtype_str) -- it captures shape and pitch but NOT the grid origin (e.g. x[0], y[0]). The v4.14.1 P0-NEW-1 fix added dx/dy to close the same-shape/different-pitch collision, but the residual same-shape/same-pitch/shifted-origin collision was left open. The cached stack is evaluated on the FIRST call's X,Y coordinates; a later call on a translated grid (same N, dx, w, cx, cy) gets modes evaluated at the wrong physical positions. _hg_mode_conj_stack (line 364-368) has the identical defect.

**Failure scenario.** Grid A = centred (arange(64)-32)*2e-6, Grid B = A + 8*dx (a shifted ROI, or any non-centred user grid). Decompose an on-axis LG00 on grid A, then decompose the same physical field sampled on grid B: fresh-cache answer for B is a_{0,0}=1.000; cache-hit answer is 0.527 -- silently ~47% wrong modal amplitude, no warning. Any workflow that decomposes fields on two grids sharing (N, pitch) but different centres (mosaic tiles, off-axis ROIs, walk-off tracking) is corrupted for up to 32 cached entries.

**Verification evidence.** Cache keys at asymptotic_modes.py:311-315 (LG) and 364-368 (HG) are (orders, Ny, Nx, w[x/y], cx, cy, dx, dy, dtype) with no origin term; the stack is built from the FIRST call's X,Y values (lines 323-336) and returned verbatim on any key match. Probe (C:/tmp/verify_asym_modes_cache.py, N=64, dx=2e-6, w=20e-6, grid B = grid A shifted +8*dx in x): fresh-cache a00(gridB)=0.9999995; after first decomposing on grid A, cache-hit a00(gridB)=0.7261490 — silently ~27% wrong, no warning. HG variant gives identical numbers (0.9999995 vs 0.7261490). The docstring's claim that entries are 'only reused when the result would be bit-equal' is false.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked all in-library callers: asymptotic_aberration_tensor.py:576-596 passes SX_local=S2X-s2x_img (always centred at 0, origin=-extent derivable from dx,N so no collision possible) and hf.py:468-478 always builds the library-standard (arange(N)-N/2)*dx grid — both immune, so no internal workflow triggers it. Grepped tests (test_v4_14_1_dispatcher_pin_cache_clears.py and suite) for shift/origin/offset coverage: none. Checked docstrings for an 'origin must be centred' contract: absent; docstring instead asserts bit-equal reuse. No guard survives — collision reproduced.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Add the grid origin (e.g. float(X.flat[0]), float(Y.flat[0])) to both the LG and HG cache keys (and update the clear_lg_mode_stack_cache docstring key description).

### [P1-07] id()-keyed fixed-index glass registration serves a stale refractive index to previously built surface lists (probe-confirmed)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/trace.py:1243`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** surfaces_from_elements names the temporary glass for 'spherical_lens'/'aspheric_lens' elements as f'__spherical_{id(elem)}' (line 1243) / f'__aspheric_{id(elem)}' (line 1270) and _register_fixed_index (line 1303) unconditionally overwrites the module-global GLASS_REGISTRY and glass._glass_cache entries. CPython recycles dict ids immediately after GC, so two successive surfaces_from_elements calls with different n_lens produce the SAME glass name, and the second registration silently retargets the first surface list's glass to the new index. Because trace() resolves glass indices at trace time (not build time), any surface list built before the collision is traced with the wrong n. It is also an unbounded global-registry leak when ids do not collide (one permanent GLASS_REGISTRY + _glass_cache entry per distinct id, never evicted, not enrolled in _cache_registry).

**Failure scenario.** listA,_=surfaces_from_elements([{'type':'spherical_lens','n_lens':1.7,'R1':0.05,'R2':-0.05,'d':0.005}],wv); listB,_=same with n_lens=1.9. The elem dicts get the same id (probe: idA==idB True back-to-back), so both lists carry glass name '__spherical_<same id>' and trace(listA,...) now refracts with n=1.9 instead of 1.7 -- silently wrong spot/OPD for any compare-two-designs or n_lens-sweep workflow.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_trace_audit/verify_trace_f1b.py): exact repro nameA==nameB True (both '__spherical_2320781522176'); get_glass_index(listA_glass, 550e-9) returned 1.9 after building listB with n_lens=1.9 (listA built with 1.7). Loop stress: 200-point n_lens sweep keeping all surface lists produced only 2 distinct glass names and 198/200 lists resolving to the WRONG n. Mechanism airtight: trace.py:1243/1270 name by id(elem), _register_fixed_index (trace.py:1303-1314) unconditionally overwrites GLASS_REGISTRY + _glass_cache, and trace() resolves glass indices at trace time (trace.py:125-126), so previously built Surface lists silently retarget. Leak sub-claim also real but self-limiting when ids collide (3 registry entries after 202 builds in the collision-heavy run).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for guards/counters around registration (none: unconditional dict writes, no eviction, no _cache_registry enrollment); checked whether surfaces_from_elements docs declare single-use surface lists (they don't; it is public API returning Surfaces for later trace()); checked get_glass_index for an lru_cache that might mask or alter behavior (none — no lru_cache on it); ran a variant probe with the elem dict built inside a helper function where ids did NOT collide (n stayed 1.7), showing collision is allocation-dependent — but the plain inline-dict sweep/compare pattern (the common workflow) collides essentially always (198/200).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Name the temporary glass with a monotonically increasing counter (itertools.count) or a content hash of (n_lens, wavelength), and/or resolve the fixed index into the Surface at build time instead of registry lookup at trace time.

### [P1-08] WaveOpticsWorker.run imports nonexistent module 'lumenairy.propagation' -> every GUI wave-optics run dies and the dock hangs at 'Running...'

- **Location:** `lumenairy/ui/waveoptics_dock.py:463`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Line 463 executes `import lumenairy.propagation as _prop` to set the FFT backend flags. That module no longer exists (the v5.14 reorg moved it to lumenairy.propagators.propagation; no sys.modules alias or shim remains anywhere in the repo — this is the sole reference). The import sits BEFORE the big try at line 493, so ModuleNotFoundError escapes run(): the custom finished signal is never emitted, _on_finished never fires, btn_run stays disabled and the progress bar stays live forever. The backend-selection feature (pyfftw/scipy) is also dead even if the import were fixed to the right module path.

**Failure scenario.** User clicks Run in the Wave Optics dock (any config) -> worker thread raises ModuleNotFoundError immediately -> no results, Run button permanently disabled, UI stuck at 'Running...' until app restart.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: `py -c "import lumenairy; import lumenairy.propagation as _prop"` -> ModuleNotFoundError (lumenairy resolves to D:\...\Lumenairy\lumenairy\__init__.py); hasattr(lumenairy,'propagation')=False so the PEP-562 `import-as` getattr fallback cannot rescue it; no sys.modules shim anywhere (fft_infra.py:1161 targets 'lumenairy.propagators.propagation'). waveoptics_dock.py:463 sits in run() BEFORE the try at :493; every finished.emit (473/655/685/723/1001) is after it. Live PySide6 probe (C:/tmp/probe_wo_thread.py, QThread subclass with shadowing finished=Signal(object), run() executing the exact failing import): 'thread isFinished: True, slot fired: []' -> _on_finished (waveoptics_dock.py:2641, sole btn_run re-enabler, sets _worker=None) never fires, so btn_run stays disabled and progress bar stays live. Secondary claim also confirmed: even with the corrected path, `_prop.USE_PYFFTW=False` is inert — propagation.py live-forwards USE_PYFFTW/USE_SCIPY_FFT via module __getattr__ (propagation.py:241-287) but a SET creates a shadowing local attr while fft_infra._fft2 reads its own module global (fft_infra.py:1637).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a shim: no lumenairy/propagation.py file, no sys.modules registration of 'lumenairy.propagation' anywhere in the package, no `propagation` attribute on the lumenairy package (grep + runtime hasattr probe). Tested the exact `import a.b as x` statement (not just plain import) to rule out the Python 3.7+ getattr fallback. Checked whether the dock is dead code: main_window.py:276-277 instantiates WaveOpticsDock in the live UI. Checked test coverage: the only two waveoptics tests (test_v4_15_1_agent_d.py, test_v4_15_agent_e.py) cover Q-bfs sag and the MFT-dialog reparent guard, not WaveOpticsWorker.run. Ran a behavioral PySide6 probe to rule out the built-in QThread.finished still delivering to the connected slot despite the shadow — it does not.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Change line 463 to route backend selection through the supported API (e.g. `from ..propagators import fft_infra` and set fft_infra.USE_PYFFTW/USE_SCIPY_FFT, or use reset_fft_backend/setters), and wrap the whole of run() so any exception still emits finished with an error dict.

## P2 findings (wrong results on realistic edge inputs, real perf/memory cost, misleading API)

### [P2-01] DeformableMirror.fit_phase 'streamed' large-DM path materializes ALL influence-function columns anyway (zero memory saving) and runs an O(n_act^4) Python dot-product loop

- **Location:** `lumenairy/analysis/ao.py:228`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The >ceiling/4 branch (lines 223-236), whose docstring promises 'streams per-actuator IF rows... peak memory is n_actuators**2 * N**2 / chunk_size at most', first builds `cols = [self._influence_function_kth(k).ravel() for k in range(n2)]` — a list holding all n2 columns of length N**2, i.e. the FULL n2*N^2*8-byte design matrix the branch exists to avoid — then computes AtA via a Python double loop of n2*(n2+1)/2 individual np.dot calls. There is no chunking anywhere. Additionally the normal-equations solve squares the condition number vs the direct lstsq path.

**Failure scenario.** n_actuators=32, N=1024 (the exact 'goes lazy automatically' example in the class docstring): fit_phase allocates ~8.6 GB in `cols` (same as materializing A) and executes ~525k Python-level dots of 1M-element vectors — minutes of runtime and likely OOM on a 16 GB box. ao_closed_loop calls scratch.fit_phase() EVERY iteration (line 860), so a 10-iteration loop pays this 10x. The docstring memory contract is simply false.

**Verification evidence.** ao.py lines 217-236: streamed branch builds cols = [self._influence_function_kth(k).ravel() for k in range(n2)] (all n2 columns) then a Python double loop of n2*(n2+1)/2 np.dot calls; no chunking exists. Probe (n_act=16, N=384, cache_basis=False, well-posed pitch=N*dx/n_act): tracemalloc peak during fit_phase = 307.3 MB == full 302 MB design matrix; wall 9.06 s vs 0.16 s for equivalent BLAS A.T@A+solve (56x overhead); round-trip err 5.8e-15. Degenerate-geometry probe: cond(A)=5.2e58 -> cond(AtA)=8.1e111, normal-eq answer max|err|=1.4e63 vs lstsq 3.1 (conditioning-squaring real but only bites pathological geometries). ao_closed_loop line 860 calls scratch.fit_phase(measured) every iteration. Docstring memory contract (peak = n2*N^2/chunk_size) is false. Tests (tests/unit/test_ao_dm.py) only use N=32 — streamed branch never covered.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the cached basis rescues memory: for cache-active configs (bytes <= 512MB ceiling), _influence_function_kth returns views into _IF_basis (probe: col.base is dm._IF_basis == True), so in the 134-512MB 'auto' band there IS no extra allocation — but the O(n_act^4) Python loop still runs there (e.g. 32-act/512-grid, the docstring's 'caches eagerly' example, ~minutes per fit_phase, paid every closed-loop iteration), and above 512MB ('auto' lazy, exactly the 32-act/1024-grid docstring example) or with cache_basis=False the full materialization is real (measured 307MB peak). Searched tests for coverage of the streamed branch: none. No caller avoids the branch.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace the branch with genuinely chunked accumulation: build column blocks of size chunk (e.g. 16-64 columns), accumulate AtA/Atb via BLAS block gemms (B.T @ B_full pattern) so peak scratch is N^2*chunk*8 bytes, and reuse _IF_basis views when cached; optionally solve via Cholesky with a documented conditioning caveat.

### [P2-02] normalization='Noll' fabricates a sign flip on m<0 modes: Noll and OSA sine modes have IDENTICAL signs, so 'Noll' coefficients match no published convention

- **Location:** `lumenairy/analysis/zernike.py:414`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _normalization_factors() sets s[j]=-1 for every m<0 mode, and the docstring (lines 347-354, 458-462) asserts 'the sign convention on the sine-like (m<0) modes is opposite to OSA'. This is wrong: Noll 1976 defines Z_odd-j = sqrt(2(n+1)) R_n^m(rho) sin(m*theta) with m>0 (positive sine), and OSA/ANSI Z80.28 defines Z_n^m for m<0 as -N R sin(m*theta) = +N R sin(|m|*theta) — exactly what zernike_polynomial() computes (verified line 134: angular = sin(-m*theta)). The two conventions differ ONLY in single-index ordering, not in polynomial sign. So the -1 rescale returns the NEGATIVE of the true Noll coefficient for every sine mode.

**Failure scenario.** opd = sqrt(6)*rho^2*sin(2*theta) (unit oblique astigmatism, identical polynomial in both conventions). zernike_decompose(..., normalization='Noll') returns c[3] = -1.0 where the true Noll Z5 coefficient is +1.0. Any user cross-checking against a Noll-convention tool (e.g. AO literature, HCIPy, poppy) sees flipped signs on all tilt-Y/astig/coma/trefoil sine modes — e.g. a coma-correction command derived from these coefficients pushes a DM the wrong way.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_zernike_noll_sign.py) on opd=sqrt(6)*rho^2*sin(2theta) (the oblique-astigmatism polynomial, identical in Noll 1976 Table I and ANSI Z80.28, true coefficient +1 in BOTH conventions): OSA c[3]=+1.000000, Noll c[3]=-1.000000; tilt-Y 2*rho*sin(theta): OSA +1.000000, Noll -1.000000 (Noll truth +1). Hand-derivation from primary sources: Noll 1976 Eq.(2) Z_odd-j = sqrt(2(n+1)) R_n^m sin(m*theta), m>0 (positive sine); OSA/ANSI Z_n^m (m<0) = -N R sin(m*theta) = +N R sin(|m|*theta) (positive sine, since sin is odd) — the sine polynomials are IDENTICAL, so the s[j]=-1 factor at zernike.py:414-418 returns the negative of the true Noll coefficient for every m<0 mode. Internal basis verified correct: zernike_polynomial line 134 gives sin(-m*theta)=+sin(|m|*theta); probe: zernike_polynomial(2,-2, rho=0.5, theta=pi/4)=+0.612372=+sqrt(6)*0.25*sin(pi/2).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Searched tests for an external oracle: only tests/unit/test_v5_4_zernike_normalization_weighting.py::test_normalization_noll_changes_sign_on_odd_terms exists, and it is circular — it asserts c_noll[j]==-c_osa[j] against the library's own OSA output, encoding the same wrong premise ('Noll's sine convention is opposite to OSA'), no comparison to Noll 1976 values. (2) Checked whether the internal m<0 basis might itself be OSA-negative-sine so the -1 factor would restore Noll — refuted: the internal basis is +sin(|m|theta), already equal to Noll's polynomial. (3) Considered whether 'Noll' could legitimately mean only a j-permutation — the docstring (lines 350-354) explicitly disclaims ordering ('permute after the fact'), leaving ONLY the sign flip, which matches no published convention. (4) No caller/guard prevents the state; normalization='Noll' is a public documented kwarg reaching _normalization_factors unconditionally.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Make the 'Noll' factor +1 for all modes (i.e. identical coefficients to OSA up to the disclaimed j-ordering), correct the docstrings at lines 347-354 and 458-462, and replace the circular unit test with one that pins coefficients against hand-written Noll 1976 Table I polynomials.

### [P2-03] apply_real_lens_maslov_jax docstring falsely claims algorithmic parity with the NumPy apply_real_lens_maslov, and its det-J sign-flip Maslov counter misses even-multiplicity caustics (point focus Gouy phase)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_jax.py:648`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Docstring lines 648-651 state 'This is the same definition the NumPy apply_real_lens_maslov uses; the only difference is that the radial scan is implemented as a jax.lax.fori_loop'. The NumPy implementation (lenses_maslov.py, read in full) contains NO radial sign-flip scan anywhere: it is a phase-space quadrature / stationary-phase integral whose caustic phase comes from the Hessian signature factor exp(i*pi*sig/4) (line 886) or is captured exactly by quadrature. The JAX function is instead a thin-OPD phase screen plus a -pi/2-per-det(J)-sign-flip correction (lines 808-832). Physically, counting det(J) sign flips misses caustics of even multiplicity: through a point focus both eigenvalues of J flip so det(J) does NOT change sign, count stays 0, but the true Maslov index advances by 2 (the -pi Gouy shift) -- so the JAX 'maslov' phase is wrong past an axial focus, exactly the regime the docstring sells it for. The 32-step fixed radial sampler can also skip closely spaced flips.

**Failure scenario.** A user doing gradient-based design near/through focus picks apply_real_lens_maslov_jax expecting 'the JAX twin' of the validated NumPy Maslov propagator (the Quick decision guide in lenses_maslov.py:104-105 explicitly pairs them). Output phase differs from the NumPy reference by pi past an axial focus (missing Gouy shift) and by the whole diffraction-amplitude model everywhere (screen vs integral); optimization converges to a design tuned to the wrong forward model.

**Verification evidence.** Code-read: lumenairy/elements/lenses_maslov.py contains NO radial det(J)-sign-flip scan; its caustic phase is the stationary-phase Hessian signature phase_sp = np.exp(1j*(np.pi/4.0)*sig) (line 886); the only np.sign uses (lines 837, 984) are Newton det safeguards. So _lens_jax.py:648-651 ('same definition... only difference is a jax.lax.fori_loop') is false. Numeric probe (C:/tmp/probe_maslov_jax_focus.py; N-BK7 singlet f~48mm + flat exit surface 100mm downstream so the exit-vertex plane is ~52mm PAST the axial focus): trace_jax magnification x_out/x_in = -1.065 / -1.061 (both axes negative -> every ray crossed the point focus, det J stays >0), yet maslov_jax vs traced_jax phase difference max = 2.77e-17 rad with Maslov count = {0} over all 3207 valid pixels -- the -pi Gouy shift is entirely missing in exactly the past-focus regime the docstring and the Quick decision guide (lenses_maslov.py:103-105, which pairs maslov/maslov_jax for 'near a caustic') sell it for.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Grepped lenses_maslov.py for sign/maslov_count/fori (no radial scan exists, so no older NumPy variant the docstring could be describing); read the full stationary-phase integrator (lines 820-913) to confirm caustic phase = exp(i*pi/4*sig); checked tests for a numeric maslov-vs-maslov_jax parity pin that would contradict divergence -- test_v5_4_7_walker_v20_cross_backend_parity.py only registers the pair structurally (module:attr existence), and test_audit_misc.py covers only mirror-guard/dy-keyword/finite-output; ran the probe hoping the counter would fire (it did not: count 0, phase diff 2.8e-17).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Correct the docstring to state this is a different, screen-based approximation with a sign-flip-only Maslov counter (and un-pair it from the NumPy Maslov in the Quick decision guide), and/or track the two Jacobian eigenvalue branches (or det sign of each 1-D radial/azimuthal factor) so even-multiplicity point-focus crossings advance the index by 2.

### [P2-04] Row-band (now-default) path silently drops the freeform-type RuntimeWarning for zernike/xy_polynomial/chebyshev surfaces

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_real.py:926`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The _narrow_chunk eligibility gate only excludes freeform_type in ('q_bfs','q_con'). A surface with any OTHER freeform_type (e.g. 'zernike') is chunk-eligible, and the band loop (lines 931-958) computes only the base conic sag -- the freeform departure is dropped, exactly as on the whole-grid path, but WITHOUT the RuntimeWarning the whole-grid path emits at lines 1079-1089 ('freeform departure is NOT included ... use apply_real_lens_traced'). Since v5.17.0 made chunking the DEFAULT for N >= 4096, every large-grid default call on a zernike/xy-polynomial/chebyshev prescription now silently omits the freeform phase with no diagnostic, where every prior release warned. The code comment at line 1027 ('the warning continues to fire for them') is now false on the default path.

**Failure scenario.** N=8192 default apply_real_lens call on a prescription whose surface carries freeform_type='zernike' with real coefficients: the zernike departure is omitted from the OPD and no warning fires; the user gets a numerically wrong lens (missing freeform aberration) with zero indication, whereas the same call pre-5.17 (or with sag_chunk_rows=0) raises the documented RuntimeWarning.

**Verification evidence.** Gate at lumenairy/elements/_lens_real.py:918-930 excludes only freeform_type in ('q_bfs','q_con'); 'zernike' passes and the band loop (935-953) computes only _surface_sag_general(base conic+asphere) with no warning, while the whole-grid path warns at 1079-1089. Probe (C:/tmp/verify_lens_audit/verify_chunk_freeform_warn.py, N=512, zernike surface): whole-grid (sag_chunk_rows=0) -> 1 freeform RuntimeWarning ('the freeform departure is NOT included ... Use apply_real_lens_traced'); chunked (sag_chunk_rows=128) -> 0 warnings; outputs byte-identical (np.array_equal True). _resolve_sag_chunk_rows(None, N) -> None @2048, 256 @4096, 512 @8192, so chunking is the DEFAULT for N>=4096 and the warning silently vanishes on every large-grid default call. The line-1026 comment 'the warning continues to fire for them' is false on this path. No freeform coverage in tests/unit/test_lens_chunked_sag.py.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for an upstream gate disabling chunking on freeform surfaces (none — only _narrow_chunk decides, and it only excludes the two Q types); verified q_bfs/q_con correctly fall through to the whole-grid path where their departure IS computed (so the exclusion tuple is deliberate for Q, just incomplete for non-Q); grepped chunked-sag tests for freeform/warn coverage (zero hits); checked whether the chunked path introduces a NEW numerical error vs whole-grid (it does not — outputs byte-identical; the departure was always dropped on this path, only the diagnostic is lost, which caps severity at P2 rather than P1).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Extend the _narrow_chunk gate to also require surf.get('freeform_type') in (None, 'q_bfs', 'q_con')... i.e. exclude non-Q freeform types so they fall through to the whole-grid warning path (or, alternatively, emit the identical RuntimeWarning once per surface before entering the band loop).

### [P2-05] sag_chunk_rows=0 (the documented force-whole-grid escape hatch) is silently re-enabled to AUTO banding in the traced function's amp legs

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_traced.py:1546`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** apply_real_lens_traced resolves the user's kwarg once at line 1546: _resolve_sag_chunk_rows(0, N) returns None (see _lens_real.py:144). That resolved None is then forwarded verbatim to the two internal apply_real_lens amp legs (lines 1645/1670/1689/1719), where apply_real_lens re-resolves None -> AUTO -> banded for N >= 4096. So sag_chunk_rows=0 disables only the final assembly banding while the per-surface phase screens still run row-banded -- directly contradicting the CHANGELOG/docstring contract ('Pass sag_chunk_rows=0 to force the whole-grid path') and the in-code comment at lines 1543-1544 ('The resolved value also flows to the apply_real_lens amp legs so both stages band consistently'). Output values are byte-identical, but the opt-out knob -- the debugging/benchmarking/repro tool that justified the default flip -- does not do what it says, and estimate_lens_memory(sag_chunk_rows=0) models a whole-grid run the process never executes.

**Failure scenario.** User at N=16384 hits a suspected numexpr/numba issue in the banded phase screens (or wants to reproduce the whole-grid memory/time profile from the estimator or the pre-5.17 baseline) and passes sag_chunk_rows=0 to apply_real_lens_traced: the amp legs still band at 1024 rows, so the whole-grid code path is never exercised and the measured peak disagrees with estimate_lens_memory(..., sag_chunk_rows=0).

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_lt/verify_lens_traced_f1.py): _resolve_sag_chunk_rows(0,16384)->None, _resolve_sag_chunk_rows(None,16384)->1024; instrumenting lumenairy.elements._lens_traced.apply_real_lens and calling apply_real_lens_traced(N=4096, sag_chunk_rows=0, parallel_amp=False) shows the amp leg receives sag_chunk_rows=None, which the inner apply_real_lens (_lens_real.py:819) re-resolves to 256 (banded). Code: _lens_traced.py:1546 overwrites the kwarg with the resolved value; lines 1645/1670/1689/1719 forward it verbatim. So 0 disables banding only for the traced final assembly (_chunk_assembly=False at 1547-1551) while both apply_real_lens amp legs band anyway, contradicting the in-code contract at 1542-1544 ('The resolved value also flows to the apply_real_lens amp legs so both stages band consistently') and _lens_real.py:816-818 ('Pass 0 to force the whole-grid path'). Output values remain byte-identical.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for any second resolution site or raw-kwarg preservation in _lens_traced.py (only line 1546 resolves; the raw value is lost before all four forwarding sites). Checked tests/unit/test_lens_chunked_sag.py: sag_chunk_rows=0 is only exercised through apply_real_lens directly (line 198), never through apply_real_lens_traced, so no test pins the escape hatch end-to-end. Checked whether explicit positive ints or None round-trip correctly (they do — only 0 loses information). Verified no docstring exempts the amp legs.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Forward the caller's raw sag_chunk_rows (or a resolved-from-0 sentinel such as 0 itself) to the apply_real_lens amp legs so 0 survives the round-trip and forces whole-grid in both stages.

### [P2-06] Hardcoded dimensional propagating-mode selector (q.real > 0.1, |q.imag| < 1e-4) silently returns empty R/T for small k0 and drops near-grazing propagating modes

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/bor/bor_stack.py:106`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** prop() classifies propagating modes with three ABSOLUTE thresholds: np.abs(q.imag) < 1e-4, q.real > 0.1, and sqrt(eps).real*k0 - q.real > -1e-9. All are dimensional. If k0*n < ~0.1 (long wavelength relative to the length unit, e.g. THz structures expressed in microns, or any mm/cm-unit workflow), no mode passes q.real > 0.1, so inc/out are empty and solve() returns R=[], T=[], angles=[] with no raise or warning. Even in the intended O(1)-unit regime, a large Rbig produces modes with 0 < q < 0.1 that ARE propagating; power scattered into them is counted in neither R nor T, showing up as spurious absorption in a lossless stack.

**Failure scenario.** BORStack(Rbig=200, m=1, N=80, n_sup=n_sub=1.0).add_layer(10.0, eps=4.0).set_source(k0=0.05).solve() -> inc=[], R=[], T=[] silently (verified by probe). A user sweeping wavelength across this boundary sees results vanish with no diagnostic.

**Verification evidence.** Both failure branches reproduced by probe. (A) BORStack(Rbig=200, m=1, N=80).add_layer(10.0, eps=4.0).set_source(k0=0.05).solve() -> len(inc)=0, len(R)=0, len(T)=0 with no raise/warning, while the superstrate eigenbasis contains 5 genuinely propagating modes (q.real = 0.0265, 0.0359, 0.0424, 0.0462, 0.0492, all |q.imag|<1e-6*k0) — all rejected by q.real > 0.1. (B) In the intended O(1)-unit regime (k0=1.97, Rbig=60, N=200): a physically propagating mode with q.real = 0.07616 is dropped by the same threshold; a full solve with a lossless concentric ring grating (rings=(3.0, 0.5, 2.0, 1.0)) then gives energy min = 0.996701 over incident orders — a 0.33% spurious absorption in a discretization whose cascade otherwise conserves energy to ~1e-13 (max energy = 1.0000000). Site is bor_stack.py:105-107; bor_solve.py:58 duplicates the identical thresholds.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched the bor module, README.md, module docstrings, and the v5.16.0 CHANGELOG entry for any documented unit convention (e.g. 'k0 must be O(1)') or an empty-inc guard — none exist. Checked the only integration test (tests/unit/test_gate4.py): it uses k0=2.0 with Lam~O(1) and asserts sel.size>=3 on its own polarization filter, never exercising either failure branch. Checked whether the third condition (sqrt(eps).real*k0 - q.real > -1e-9) could rescue small-k0 — it cannot, since q.real > 0.1 fails first. Attempted to refute the near-grazing branch at Rbig=60/k0=2.0 exactly (no mode landed in the (0,0.1) window there), but a 19-point k0 scan 1.97-2.06 found dropped propagating modes at 4 of 19 k0 values (q = 0.076-0.085), confirming the window is routinely hit as parameters sweep.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Scale the classifier thresholds by k0 (e.g. |q.imag| < tol_rel*k0 and q.real > tol_rel*k0, keeping the q <= n*k0 cap) in both bor_stack.prop and bor_solve._physical_propagating, and raise or warn when inc comes back empty.

### [P2-07] makedammann2d amplitude-replacement uses np.sign(complex), which discards the far-field phase on NumPy < 2.0 (pyproject allows numpy>=1.20)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/doe.py:859`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Line 859 `farfield2 = farfieldamp * np.sign(farfield)` relies on NumPy 2.0 semantics where sign(z)=z/|z| (the MATLAB/Octave sign() the port needs). On NumPy 1.x (permitted by pyproject.toml 'numpy>=1.20'), np.sign(complex) returns sign(z.real) (else sign(z.imag)), so the IFTA's far-field phase is replaced by ±1 each iteration and the phase-retrieval loop degenerates. No CHANGELOG entry fixes this; the extensive v4.14.2/4.14.3 unit-system audits of this function never touched it.

**Failure scenario.** Any user on numpy 1.20-1.26 (still a supported install per pyproject) designs a Dammann grating: probe with itr=200, 8x8 target -> uniformity collapses 0.9645 -> 0.0206 and only 46/64 target orders survive threshold. The returned nearfield is a silently wrong design (no error, no warning).

**Verification evidence.** doe.py:859 is `farfield2 = farfieldamp * np.sign(farfield)` with complex farfield (fft2 of complex nearfield, line 845). Probe (C:/tmp/probe_dammann_sign.py, run from scratchpad copy because stray C:/tmp/inspect.py shadows stdlib) on numpy 2.4.4 with legacy-1.x sign monkeypatched: numpy2 semantics uniformity=0.9645, eff=0.8952, n_active=64/64; numpy1 semantics uniformity=0.0206, eff=0.7937, n_active=46/64. Exact reproduction of the claimed numbers. pyproject.toml: numpy>=1.20, requires-python>=3.10, so numpy 1.21-1.26 installs are metadata-legal. Legacy-sign simulation matches numpy 1.x documented behavior (sign(x.real)+0j if x.real!=0 else sign(x.imag)+0j); numpy 2.0 changed complex sign to z/|z| (Array API), which is the Octave sign() the port needs.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched entire package for numpy-version guards or a complex-sign helper: none (line 859 is the only complex np.sign use in doe.py). Checked tests: tests/unit/test_audit_sources.py only asserts cell_pixel_size geometry at tiny itr; validation/elements/test_elements.py t_dammann_grating only asserts non-empty phase shape — neither discriminates design quality, and CI runs numpy 2.x regardless. Checked CHANGELOG.md for any complex-sign fix mention: none. Checked whether requires-python (>=3.10) forecloses numpy 1.x: it does not (numpy 1.21.3-1.26 support 3.10-3.12). Verified the monkeypatch actually reaches doe.py's np (same module object) and that legacy semantics match real numpy 1.x docs.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace `np.sign(farfield)` with a version-independent phase projector, e.g. `np.exp(1j * np.angle(farfield))` (or farfield/|farfield| with a zero guard), or alternatively raise the numpy floor to >=2.0.

### [P2-08] surface_sag_zernike_freeform missed the P1-NEW-11 validation sweep: non-positive norm_radius silently produces parity-flipped or zeroed sag

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/freeform.py:283`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** All four sibling freeform sag functions (xy_polynomial line 223, chebyshev line 317, q_bfs line 435, q_con line 556) validate their normalization half-extents after the v4.14.3 P1-NEW-11 audit, with messages explicitly citing the silent-sign-flip / silent-no-op failure mode. surface_sag_zernike_freeform has no guard on norm_radius: line 283 computes rho = sqrt(h_sq)/norm_radius. Negative norm_radius gives rho <= 0 everywhere, which passes zernike_polynomial's `rho <= 1.0` pupil mask (analysis/zernike.py:137) and evaluates the radial polynomial at negative rho, flipping the sign of every odd-n term (coma, trefoil, tilt) -- silently wrong sag. norm_radius = 0 gives rho = inf (plus a runtime divide warning at the origin only), zeroing the whole departure -- a silent no-op.

**Failure scenario.** User typos norm_radius=-0.05 (or passes a signed semi-diameter) for a 50 mm pupil with coma coefficient: the returned sag has coma of the WRONG SIGN; the raytrace/wavefront downstream is plausible-looking but mirrored, exactly the failure class the library's own v4.14.3 audit rated P1 for the rectangular siblings.

**Verification evidence.** freeform.py:255-288 has no norm_radius guard (line 283: rho = sqrt(h_sq)/norm_radius) while all four siblings validate post-P1-NEW-11: xy_polynomial line 223/230, chebyshev line 317/324, q_bfs lines 436+443 (r_max, the direct analog of norm_radius, IS guarded there), q_con lines 557/564. Probe (41x41 grid, +/-40mm, coma j=7, coeff 1e-6): norm_radius=-0.05 returns normally, and inside the 50mm pupil s_neg == -s_pos exactly (allclose True) -- coma sign silently flipped. BONUS defect beyond the claim: negative rho defeats the zernike.py:137 pupil mask (rho<=1.0 true everywhere), so out-of-pupil pixels that are zeroed for norm_radius=+0.05 (max 0.0) carry up to 4.163e-6 m departure for -0.05 (max|s_neg|=4.163e-6 vs max|s_pos|=2.263e-6), corrupting even terms outside the pupil too. norm_radius=0.0 returns all-zero departure (max|sag|=0.0) with only 3 RuntimeWarnings -- silent no-op.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for guards upstream: dispatcher surface_sag_freeform (freeform.py:636-639) passes surface_dict.get('norm_radius', 1.0) through unvalidated; raytrace/trace.py:468 is only a key allowlist, not a value check; grepped all repo callers/tests -- validation/analysis/test_features.py:158,468 use only positive norm_radius, no test covers negative/zero; checked zernike_polynomial's pupil mask as a potential guard -- it is rho<=1.0, which negative rho passes everywhere, so it makes the failure worse, not better; docstring (line 271-272) gives no signed-value semantics that would make this intended.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Add the P1-NEW-11 guard at function entry: raise ValueError unless np.isfinite(norm_radius) and norm_radius > 0, matching the message style of the four sibling sag functions.

### [P2-09] stabilize passive gate is ONE-SIDED (tot <= 1 + tol) and never checks efficiency non-negativity, so the degree-scan consensus silently certifies non-physical (negative-total) solves

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/_core.py:1457`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _stabilize_scalar marks a solve 'passive' via `tot <= 1.0 + passive_tol` (_core.py:1457; Jones twin `tot <= 2.0 + 2.0*passive_tol` at :1501). Any grossly negative or near-zero total passes this gate, and no check anywhere requires per-order efficiencies >= 0. Since consecutive degrees of a systematically-wrong solve agree per-order within _PER_ORDER_TOL, the very first two scanned degrees form a 'converged cluster' and the garbage is returned with ZERO warnings — the consensus mechanism actively launders the wrong answer. The gate was designed assuming the only off-curve modes are super-unity resonances and under-convergence; a sign-flipped flux normalization (finding 1) or any future negative-efficiency defect sails through. Cheap fix: also require tot >= -passive_tol and min(R,T) >= -passive_tol in the passive test.

**Failure scenario.** pmm_efficiency_1d(..., n_superstrate=1.0-1e-6j, degree=10) with the DEFAULT stabilize=True returns sum T = -20.63 with warns == 0: tot = -20.63 <= 1.001 -> passive=True at degrees 10 and 11, both produce identical wrong per-order values -> cluster of 2 -> returned as the 'converged consensus'. The safety net that exists precisely to reject non-physical solves endorses one.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: pmm_efficiency_1d(..., n_superstrate=1.0-1e-6j, degree=10, stabilize=True DEFAULT) -> sumR=0.000000, sumT=-12.508788, warns=0 — no warning, no raise; Jones path stabilize=True -> sumT=-1.860615, warns=0. Code: _core.py:1457 `scanned.append((d, orders, R, T, tot <= 1.0 + passive_tol, tot))` — tot=-12.5 passes; Jones twin :1501 `tot <= 2.0 + 2.0*passive_tol` identical hole. No `>= -tol` or per-order non-negativity check exists anywhere in _stabilize_scalar (:1439-1482), _stabilize_jones (:1486-1529), or _energy_clean_pick (:1416-1430); the deterministic identical per-order error at consecutive degrees forms the _MIN_PLATEAU=2 cluster and is returned as the 'converged consensus'.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for any downstream non-negativity/energy tripwire that would catch the laundered result: stack.py:44 _warn_if_gain fires only on R+T>1 (one-sided, and only on the PMMStack path, not pmm_efficiency_1d); the far-field Re(kz)>1e-12 masks only zero individual orders, they don't bound totals below. Checked whether _energy_clean_pick's lossless-target logic would reject: it only re-picks WITHIN an already-accepted cluster (min|tot-1| never gates admission). Considered whether this is only reachable via finding 1: currently yes — the only known producer of negative efficiencies is the unguarded gain-incidence path — so the fix is partly defense-in-depth; but the failure is live TODAY under default settings, which keeps it at P2 rather than P3.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Tighten the passive test to `(-passive_tol <= tot <= 1 + passive_tol) and min(R.min(), T.min()) >= -passive_tol` (Jones twin analogously with the 2.0 target).

### [P2-10] _pmm_stack_solve_jax has no grazing/evanescent-incidence guard: divides by kz_inc -> NaN R/T where the NumPy stack raises

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/_jax_stack.py:320`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The NumPy PMMStack far-field goes through _assemble_jones_farfield (pmm/_core.py:636) which raises ValueError when |kz_inc| < 1e-9. The JAX twin computes kz_inc = jnp.real(_kzf(eps_sup, kx0n)) at line 294 and divides by it in flux_inc at lines 320/322 and in Re/Te at 323-326 with no guard and no host-side concrete-angle check (unlike the sibling _jax_stack2d, which runs _require_propagating_incidence when the angle is concrete, lines 140-165). At exact grazing kz_inc = 0 -> NaN; in the window 0 < kz_inc < 1e-9 (where NumPy raises) the twin returns huge finite non-physical efficiencies.

**Failure scenario.** PMMStack(..., n_superstrate=1.0) with one traced segment eps, set_source(1.55e-6, angle=pi/2).solve(): NumPy raises 'pmm: grazing/evanescent incidence'; JAX returns R/T full of NaN with no error, and jax.grad of any downstream loss is NaN. Angle sweeps that touch 90 deg (or complex-superstrate configs) silently poison an optimization.

**Verification evidence.** Reproduced with C:/tmp/lumjax_audit/audit_jax_probe2.py: NumPy PMMStack at angle=pi/2 raises ValueError 'pmm: grazing/evanescent incidence (kz_inc ~ 0)...' (guard at _core.py:636); JAX twin (traced eps, concrete angle) returns with NO raise, 'R row0 max = nan, sumRT = nan'. New probe C:/tmp/lumjax_audit/audit_jax_probe5_window.py at angle=pi/2-5e-10 (kz_inc=5.0e-10, inside NumPy's <1e-9 raise window): NumPy raises, JAX returns max R = nan, max T = nan. Code trace: _jax_stack.py:294 computes kz_inc, lines 320-326 divide flux_inc=kz_inc (and (kx0n/kz_inc)^2) with no guard; safe_r/safe_t (295-296) protect only the diffracted-order kz, never kz_inc. The dispatcher (stack.py:574-604) adds no grazing check before calling the twin. The sibling _jax_stack2d.py:134-165 DOES run _require_propagating_incidence + _grazing_safe_wavelength when inputs are concrete, so the guard pattern exists in-repo and is simply missing here. No test covers PMM-JAX grazing (grep 'grazing|kz_inc' in tests/ hits only raytrace/HFPI/RCWA). One sub-claim correction: in the 0<kz_inc<1e-9 window the twin returned NaN, not 'huge finite' efficiencies — silent NaN either way.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a guard in the dispatcher (stack.py:574-604 raises only for retain_internal/stabilize/slant/OOP — no angle check); searched the whole pmm package for _require_propagating_incidence (present in twod/twod_jones/stack2d/_jax_stack2d but NOT in _jax_stack.py or the 1-D stack JAX dispatch); grepped tests/ for a pin documenting NaN-at-grazing as intended (none); re-ran the probe to reproduce both the exact-grazing NaN and the near-grazing window.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Mirror _jax_stack2d's host-side concrete-input guard in _pmm_stack_solve_jax (raise via the _core.py grazing message when angle/n_sup/wl are concrete and |kz_inc| < 1e-9), plus a documented safe-kz_inc fallback for the fully-traced case.

### [P2-11] _scalar_jax_tail (pmm_efficiency_2d / _cell JAX twins) skips the propagating-incidence raise and Wood-anomaly wavelength nudge that both the NumPy 2-D core and the sibling _jax_stack2d apply for concrete inputs

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/_jax_twod.py:337`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The NumPy 2-D core runs _require_propagating_incidence and _grazing_safe_wavelength (twod.py:593-596), and the multilayer JAX twin _jax_stack2d reproduces both host-side when wavelength/theta/phi are concrete (its lines 134-165). The single-layer twins _pmm_efficiency_2d_jax / _pmm_efficiency_2d_cell_jax run neither, even though in the common inverse-design case (traced eps, concrete wavelength/angles) the values are available. R at line 337 divides jnp.real(kz_ref_f / kz_inc) with kz_inc unguarded (only the TM einc_sq at 320-322 has a where-guard), and the un-nudged wavelength sits exactly on Rayleigh cutoffs the NumPy path deliberately steps off.

**Failure scenario.** (1) theta=pi/2 with traced eps_pillar: NumPy raises 'non-propagating incidence'; JAX twin returns sum R+T = inf silently. (2) wavelength exactly == period (m=+-1 cutoff): forward parity vs NumPy degrades from the documented ~1e-15 to ~5.6e-5 because only the NumPy side nudges the wavelength; the twin also solves the numerically singular un-nudged problem, so gradients there are unreliable.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace: twod.py:783-796 dispatches to _pmm_efficiency_2d_jax BEFORE reaching _pmm2d_solve_core whose guards sit at twod.py:593-596 (_require_propagating_incidence + _grazing_safe_wavelength); no guard exists anywhere in _jax_twod.py (kz_inc used unguarded at line 337), while _jax_stack2d.py:134-165 runs exactly this guard block host-side when wavelength/theta/phi/n_sup are concrete. Probe (C:/tmp/audit_verify_jax2d_guards.py, degree=3, n_orders=2, TM): theta=pi/2 -> NumPy raises ValueError 'incidence half-space is non-propagating'; JAX twin returns sum R+T = inf with no raise. wavelength==period (Rayleigh m=+-1 cutoff): NumPy-vs-JAX max|dR| = 1.327e-05, max|dT| = 1.285e-04, vs 2.706e-15 / 1.416e-15 at wl=0.8 off-anomaly — parity collapses by ~10 orders exactly on the anomaly because only the NumPy side nudges the wavelength.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the public entry pmm_efficiency_2d runs the guards before the JAX dispatch (it does not — only node-count/order-count/polarization checks precede line 783); checked whether _scalar_jax_tail's internal where-guards cover kz_inc in R/T (only the TM einc_sq denominator at 320-322 is guarded; the kz_ref_f/kz_inc division at 337/339 is not); checked whether the module docstring documents skipping the guards as intended (it lists stabilize/uniform-shortcut/CPU-eig/x64 caveats but says nothing about grazing or Wood anomalies, whereas the sibling _jax_stack2d explicitly documents AND implements the concrete-input guards — so this is an inconsistency, not a documented scope choice); ran both probes and reproduced both failure modes exactly.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** At the top of _pmm_efficiency_2d_jax / _pmm_efficiency_2d_cell_jax, when wavelength/theta/phi/n_superstrate are concrete (the _concrete() helper from _jax_stack2d), run _require_propagating_incidence and replace wavelength with _grazing_safe_wavelength's nudged value, mirroring _jax_stack2d.py:134-165.

### [P2-12] retain_internal S_below_bot built by rebuilding the full downstream chain per layer: O(nlay^2) Redheffer stars where a linear reverse recurrence suffices

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/stack.py:709`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Lines 709-715: for each i (descending), the inner loop re-stars ifc[i+1] with every propagation+interface below it, giving ~nlay^2 Redheffer stars, each involving dense (2*n_modes)^2 solves. Redheffer star is associative, so S_below_bot[i] = star(star(ifc[i+1], prop_{i+1}), S_below_bot[i+1]) is exact and O(nlay) -- precisely the linear recurrence the S_above loop directly above (lines 702-707) already uses. PMM2DStack.solve repeats the same quadratic pattern (stack2d.py lines 569-576).

**Failure scenario.** A 32-slice tapered grating solved with retain_internal=True (the layer_absorption workflow the taper builders point at): ~528 stars instead of ~62, each a dense multi-hundred-DOF matrix solve on the shared union grid -- roughly 8x the partial-cascade cost, growing quadratically with n_slices; at n_slices=64 it dominates the whole solve.

**Verification evidence.** stack.py:709-715 inner loop re-stars ifc[i+1] with every downstream prop+interface per i. Probe (counting _redheffer_star calls during solve): retain_internal star counts = 26/86/302/1118 for nlay=4/8/16/32 vs base cascade 8/16/32/64; linear recurrence would need ~20/44/92/188 (nlay=32: 1118 vs ~188, ~6x total / ~17x in the partials stage, growing as nlay^2). Exactness of the proposed fix verified two ways: random S-matrix associativity error 1.8e-13, and star(star(ifc[i+1],prop_{i+1}), S_below_bot[i+1]) reproduces the stored quadratic S_below_bot to 3.3e-16 on a real 6-layer stack. Refutation attempt actually strengthened the finding: RCWAStack._internal_partials (rcwa/stack.py:1976-1988) already implements the linear reverse recurrence (S_below_bot[i]=star(ifc[i+1], S_below[i+1]) with a collapsed propagation left-star), so the in-repo intended pattern is linear; PMM2DStack.solve (stack2d.py:569-576) duplicates the same quadratic loop.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a numerical-stability reason to prefer the per-layer rebuild (none: the RCWA sibling uses the recurrence and even documents the decay convention PMM's comment cites); checked whether retain_internal is a rarely-reachable path (it is the documented layer_absorption/internal_field workflow that the tapered-grating builders feed, with n_slices routinely 8-32); verified associativity is not violated in practice (3.3e-16 match on the actual stack, so the recurrence is a drop-in, not an approximation); timed toy degree=4 runs (0.27s at nlay=32) — small at toy size, but each star is a dense (2*n_modes)^2 solve on the shared union grid whose size itself grows with n_slices, so the quadratic count compounds at production degree.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace the inner loop with the RCWAStack._internal_partials linear reverse recurrence S_below_bot[i] = star(star(ifc[i+1], prop_{i+1}), S_below_bot[i+1]) (seed S_below_bot[nlay-1]=ifc[nlay]) in both PMMStack.solve and PMM2DStack.solve.

### [P2-13] layer_absorption(by_material=True) drops a layer whose only loss is Im(ezz): the layer's (correct, nonzero) flux absorption never appears in the per-material dict

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/stack.py:1217`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The per-material split classifies a segment as lossless when abs(Im(exx))<1e-15 AND abs(Im(eyy))<1e-15, ignoring Im(ezz) (and off-diagonal Im(exy)). The weight density is Im(exx)|Ex|^2+Im(eyy)|Ey|^2 only. The docstring's renormalization argument ('the E_z channel is carried by the flux normalization') only holds when at least one in-plane-lossy segment exists in the layer; when a layer's loss is purely via ezz (uniaxial absorber with the lossy axis along z), keyed stays empty, the loop 'continue's, and A[i] is silently absent from the returned dict -- sum(dict values) != sum(A) with no warning.

**Failure scenario.** Uniform layer eps=diag(2, 2, 2+1j), oblique TM incidence (angle=0.6): flux-based A = 0.2789 for pol 0, but by_material dict is {} -- 28% of the incident power is absorbed yet attributed to nothing. Mixed case: a layer with one in-plane-lossy and one ezz-only-lossy material attributes ALL of the layer's absorption to the in-plane one.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: PMMStack(0.8e-6, n_substrate=1.5, degree=10).add_layer(0.3e-6, eps=diag(2,2,2+1j)), set_source(0.55e-6, angle=0.6), solve(retain_internal=True): flux A = [2.7886e-01, 1.60e-15] (sum 0.27886) but by_material dict == {} -- 27.9% of incident power unattributed, no warning. Mixed probe (segments: diag(2+0.05j,2+0.05j,2) + diag(2,2,2+1j)): A.sum()=0.25245, dict = {(2+0.05j): [0.19304, 0.05941]}, sum(dict)==sum(A) -- ALL of the layer's absorption, including the ezz segment's dominant share, attributed to the in-plane-lossy material. Code: stack.py:1217 gates losslessness on Im(exx)/Im(eyy) only; weights at :1224-1225 use only Im(exx),Im(eyy); empty keyed -> 'continue' at :1226 drops the layer from the dict entirely.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked the docstring defense (:1156-1161): the 'E_z channel carried by the flux normalization' argument covers only ISOTROPIC lossy segments (Im(exx)=Im(ezz)) or layers with at least one in-plane-lossy segment -- it does not cover ezz-only loss, and no warning or residual key exists. Grepped tests for by_material/ezz coverage of this case: none. Verified the flux-based A itself is correct (nonzero, closes vs 1-R-T), so the defect is confined to the attribution split, as claimed.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Include Im(ezz)|Ez|^2 in the per-segment weight density (Ez reconstructable per element as in internal_field), or at minimum classify Im(ezz)>1e-15 segments as lossy and emit an explicit unattributed-residual key/warning when keyed is empty.

### [P2-14] pmm_efficiency_2d JAX dispatch returns before _validate_pillar_bounds -- inverted/degenerate bounds silently give an energy-conserving but wrong answer

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/twod.py:783`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The JAX branch (lines 783-796) dispatches to _pmm_efficiency_2d_jax and returns BEFORE the _validate_pillar_bounds call at line 798. The bounds are concrete floats even on the JAX path (they are static geometry), so the guard could run, but never does. _jax_twod._static_prep then feeds the raw [x0, x1] into _build_axis, which for x1 < x0 builds a negative-width strip with negative quadrature weights. _validate_pillar_bounds was added by the v5.14 robustness audit precisely because this failure mode is energy-conserving-but-wrong (the lossless trap); the JAX inverse-design mainline reintroduces it. The same bypass exists for out-of-range bounds (lo <= 0 or hi >= period).

**Failure scenario.** pmm_efficiency_2d(1e-6, 1e-6, jnp.asarray(12.0+0j), 2.25, x_bounds=(0.6e-6, 0.3e-6), y_bounds=(0.2e-6, 0.7e-6), ...) -> no raise, sum R+T = 1.000057 (passes any energy check), but max |dR| = 0.91 vs the correct (0.3, 0.6) geometry. The identical NumPy call raises ValueError. A gradient-based design loop seeded with swapped bounds converges on garbage with no signal.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace: twod.py JAX dispatch (lines 783-796) returns at 796, before _validate_pillar_bounds at line 798; _jax_twod.py _pmm_efficiency_2d_jax -> _static_prep feeds raw float(x_bounds) into _build_axis, which builds strips from [0, x0, x1, period] with no monotonicity/range check. Independent probe (C:/tmp/lumverify/verify_pmm2d_jax_bounds.py, degree=5, n_orders=2, x64): NumPy inverted bounds (0.6P,0.3P) -> ValueError 'x_bounds must satisfy 0 < lo < hi < period'; identical call with jnp.asarray(12.0+0j) eps_pillar -> NO raise, finite sum R+T = 1.013641, max|dT| = 0.3756 vs the correct (0.3P,0.6P) geometry; out-of-range (0.0, 0.6P) on the JAX path also does NOT raise.
