Good-bounds numpy-vs-jax parity max|dR| = 9.7e-13 confirms the twin is otherwise correct, so the wrong answer is purely the bypassed guard. Only signal is one-time NumPy RuntimeWarnings (divide by zero) from the cached _static_prep, absent on cache hits.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched _jax_twod.py for any bounds validation (none: _static_prep and _pmm_efficiency_2d_jax take raw floats); inspected _build_axis for an implicit monotonicity guard (none — it zips bnds pairwise, accepting negative-width strips); checked whether NaNs from the degenerate strip would surface as a crash (no — result is finite and plausible-looking); checked the test suite: test_pillar_bounds_validation (test_v5_14_0_pmm_audit_fixes.py:77) uses plain-float eps so it only covers the NumPy branch, and no *jax* test touches x_bounds; confirmed bounds ARE always concrete on the JAX path (docstring/scope: only materials/depth/wavelength/angles are traced), so no design reason prevents pre-dispatch validation.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Move the _validate_pillar_bounds(...) call above the is_jax_array dispatch in pmm_efficiency_2d (bounds are always concrete static geometry), and add a JAX-path regression test mirroring test_pillar_bounds_validation.

### [P2-15] apply_waveplate retardance sign is opposite to the library's own rigorous Berreman/RCWA retarder Jones - circular handedness (S3) flips between the Jones-element family and the solver family for the same physical waveplate

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/polarization.py:690`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** apply_waveplate applies exp(-1j*retardance) to the slow axis, and its docstring asserts this IS the exp(-i omega t) convention ('the slow axis arrives later'). But under exp(-i omega t) the forward field phasor is exp(+i k n z) (the convention berreman.py declares as 'PUBLIC throughout' and that rcwa_jones_1d matches), so the slow axis (larger n) accumulates POSITIVE relative phase. A rigorous Berreman solve of a uniaxial quarter-wave slab (eps=diag(no^2,ne^2,no^2), d=lambda/(4(ne-no)), index-matched half-spaces) gives transmission-Jones slow/fast relative phase +pi/2, and the 45-deg-rotated tensor on x-pol input gives Ey/Ex=-i, S3=-1 - while apply_waveplate(QWP,45deg) on x-pol gives Ey/Ex=+i, S3=+1. CONVENTIONS.md sec 7 pins the waveplate sign only as 'DECOUPLED from the propagator exp(+i k OPL)' (v5.4.6, pre-Berreman); the flip against the v5.14.4 Berreman/RCWA/EMT retarder path is undocumented, and the docstring's physical justification is backwards relative to the library's own field convention.

**Failure scenario.** A user models an LC retarder rigorously with BerremanStack (or BerremanStack.add_effective_grating for a form-birefringent metasurface QWP), extracts the transmission Jones, and inserts it into a JonesField pipeline next to apply_waveplate/create_circular_polarized elements: the two element families disagree on circular handedness, so downstream S3-dependent results (PBS port assignment after a QWP, vortex/geometric-phase sign, DOCP maps) come out mirrored depending on which family modeled the retarder.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (py, repo root): berreman_jones_1d([(diag(no^2,ne^2,no^2), lam/(4(ne-no)))], n_sub=n_sup=no=1.5, ne=1.6, angle=0) -> Jt = diag(-1j, 1) exactly = diag(e^{ik0*no*d}, e^{ik0*ne*d}) (7.5pi -> -i, 8pi -> +1), i.e. slow-rel-fast phase = +0.500000*pi; apply_waveplate uses e = exp(-1j*retardance) (polarization.py:690) giving slow-rel-fast = -pi/2 under the SAME declared exp(-i omega t) convention (berreman.py:27 'Conventions are PUBLIC throughout (exp(-i w t))'). Handedness probe: Berreman slab with fast axis at +45 deg on x-pol -> Ey/Ex = -1j, S3/S0 = -1.000000 (library formula S3=-2Im(Ex conj Ey), polarization.py:1029); apply_waveplate(pi/2, angle=+pi/4) on x-pol -> Ey/Ex = +1j, S3/S0 = +1.000000. The Berreman fast@+45 Jones matched the library element built with fast@-45 element-for-element (90-deg axis relabel = flipped QWP handedness). Docstring lines 658-680 asserts exp(-i*phi) IS the exp(-i omega t) slow-axis-later behavior — numerically refuted by the Berreman slab, which under exp(-i omega t) gives the slow axis POSITIVE relative phase.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Looked for a documented bridge: CONVENTIONS.md sec 7 Waveplate row (v5.4.6) only pins the sign as 'DECOUPLED from the propagator exp(+i k OPL)' and 'mutually consistent' with the S3 formula for closed Jones pipelines — it predates Berreman (v5.14.4) and never addresses solver-family Jones. (2) Checked whether the S3 formula compensates: it is the single library-wide Stokes formula and the two families give OPPOSITE S3 through it for the same device, so no. (3) Checked orientation bookkeeping: verified with the unrotated slab (orientation-unambiguous, exact diag phases) and confirmed the 45-deg tensor rotation Rz(45) puts the slow ne-axis at 135 deg (fast at +45), so the handedness comparison is apples-to-apples. (4) Searched tests: no test links apply_waveplate/handedness/S3 to berreman (grep of tests/ empty); test_v5_14_5_emt_and_berreman_jax.py cross-pins Berreman/EMT against rcwa_jones_1d, confirming the SOLVER family is internally consistent — so the flip is squarely element-family vs solver-family, exactly as claimed. (5) Considered 'intentional pinned choice' as a refutation: the pin only guarantees closed-element-pipeline intensity correctness; the cross-family handedness contradiction and the backwards docstring justification are real and undocumented.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Document the element-family retarder sign as EE-convention (exp(+i omega t))-equivalent and explicitly opposite to the Berreman/RCWA transmission Jones in CONVENTIONS.md sec 7 and the apply_waveplate docstring (correcting the backwards exp(-i omega t) justification), and provide/point to a conjugation adapter for inserting solver-derived Jones into JonesField pipelines (a hard sign flip of apply_waveplate would break regression-pinned handedness across create_circular_polarized and vector_diffraction and needs a coordinated major-version change).

### [P2-16] _HOMOG_CACHE is a plain unbounded dict keyed on wavelength/angle: RCWAStack.solve_vs_wavelength retains 2 large eigenmode entries per sweep point with no eviction

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/rcwa/_core.py:2169`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _HOMOG_CACHE (rcwa/_core.py:2169) has no LRU bound or byte cap, violating the library's own v4.12.2 convention (every other repeat-keyed cache was converted to a bounded OrderedDict precisely so 'long-running design sweeps do not leak'). Its key (built at stack.py:1772-1777) embeds wl, theta, phi, so every point of the library's own sweep driver solve_vs_wavelength (stack.py:1383, which does no cache management) inserts two never-reused entries, each holding W and V dense (2M,2M) complex128 eigenmode matrices plus kz. It is enrolled in the registry, but nothing drains it except an explicit user call to clear_asm_caches.

**Failure scenario.** 2-D stack with nox=noy=8 (M=289, 2M=578: W+V ~10.7 MB per entry, 2 entries per wavelength) swept over 500 wavelengths -> ~10.7 GB permanently retained after the sweep returns; a subsequent angle sweep doubles it. Long-running dispersion-characterization pipelines accumulate until OOM.

**Verification evidence.** Duplicate of finding 1 (same root defect, same site _core.py:2169/2188). All specific claims verified in-code: (a) the v4.12.2 bounded-cache convention is real — jax_trace.py:1056-1061 ('v4.12.2: converted to an LRU-bounded OrderedDict so long-running optimizers ... do not leak', maxsize 32), phase_retrieval.py:602 ('v4.12.2: converted to LRU-bounded OrderedDicts (were unbounded ...)'), fft_infra.py:1067-1091 (_H_CACHE_SIZE=8 plus 2 GB/entry and 8 GB total byte caps, comment 'All three are bounded LRU dicts so peak memory stays controlled'), zernike/through_focus/wrapper_merits all use move_to_end+popitem — _HOMOG_CACHE is the outlier plain dict; (b) key embeds wl/theta/phi (stack.py:1772-1777) so a second sweep at a different theta exactly doubles the entry count (new theta → all-new keys); (c) enrollment in the registry (_core.py:2200-2203) means the only drain is an explicit clear_asm_caches()/lumenairy_context(clear_caches_on_exit=True) — clear_all_registered_caches (_cache_registry.py:120-154) is never called automatically. Per-entry math at nox=noy=8: W+V = 2*(578)^2*16 B = 10.69 MB, 2 entries/wl → ~10.7 GB after 500 wavelengths, retained for process lifetime.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Same refutation sweep as finding 1: looked for LRU bounds, per-sweep clears in solve_vs_wavelength/solve_vs_angle, automatic registry-triggered draining, weakref semantics, and any test pinning cache size — none exist. Also considered whether repeated identical sweeps reuse keys (they do — the wl nudge in _grazing_safe_wavelength is deterministic — so re-running the SAME sweep does not grow the cache further; growth requires new wl/theta/phi/geometry values, which is exactly what sweeps produce). This finding's P2 grade is the right one: memory-only, no wrong physics, user-clearable escape hatch exists, but the library's own sweep driver violates the library's own documented anti-leak convention with multi-GB retention at realistic order counts.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Adopt the _H_CACHE pattern from fft_infra.py (bounded OrderedDict with move_to_end/popitem under _HOMOG_LOCK, small count cap since only sup/sub of the current geometry need to be hot); findings 1 and 2 should be merged into a single fix.

### [P2-17] _HOMOG_CACHE grows without bound across wavelength/angle sweeps (2 dense 2Nx2N entries per wavelength, no eviction)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/rcwa/_core.py:2188`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P1, re-graded P2)*

**Claim.** _cached_homogeneous_eigenmodes inserts into module-level _HOMOG_CACHE with no size cap or eviction. The key built in RCWAStack._solve_once (stack.py:1772-1777) includes the (nudged) wavelength, theta and phi, so every sweep point creates two NEW entries (sup+sub), each holding a dense 2Nx2N identity W plus a dense 2Nx2N complex V (~2*(2N)^2*16 B). solve_vs_wavelength (the shipped GAP5 dispersive-sweep API, added since the v5.14.1 audit) never clears it, so the cache retains every wavelength's region modes for the life of the process. Caching a literal eye(2N) per entry doubles the waste.

**Failure scenario.** RCWAStack with nox=noy=8 (N=289, 2N=578) swept over 500 wavelengths: 2 entries/wl x ~10.7 MB/entry = ~21 MB per wavelength -> ~10.7 GB retained after the sweep; repeated sweeps (or theta scans, which also key uniquely) keep growing until OOM. Measured: 25-wavelength sweep at nox=noy=4 left 50 entries / 42.1 MB retained.

**Verification evidence.** Code-trace is airtight. _core.py:2169 declares `_HOMOG_CACHE: dict = {}` (plain dict); _cached_homogeneous_eigenmodes (2181-2189) does get/setitem only — no popitem/move_to_end/size check anywhere in the repo (grep). The key built in stack.py:1772-1777 is `("sup"|"sub", n) + (nox, noy, wl, theta, phi, period_x, period_y, n_superstrate, bname)` — wl/theta/phi are in it, so every sweep point mints 2 fresh entries. solve_vs_wavelength (stack.py:1383-1461) loops self.solve() per wavelength with zero cache management (only restores layers/source in `finally`). Each entry stores W = xp.eye(2N) dense (_core.py:1254) plus dense V = Q@diag(1/lam) (_core.py:1257). Size arithmetic verified: nox=noy=4 → N=81, 2N=162 → W+V+kz = 2*162^2*16 + 81*16 = 841,104 B/entry; 25 wl × 2 = 50 entries = 42.06 MB — matching the finding's claimed probe output ('50 entries, 42.1 MB, 2.0 entries/wl') exactly. Extrapolation nox=noy=8: 2N=578 → 10.69 MB/entry → 500 wl = 10.7 GB, as claimed. Only drain is the registry clearer 'rcwa_homogeneous_modes' (_core.py:2195-2205) reached via an explicit user clear_asm_caches() call; no test enforces boundedness (grep tests/: no _HOMOG_CACHE hits). My independent runtime probe (C:/tmp/verify_homog_cache.py, 25-wl 2-D sweep at n_orders=4x4) was launched but the 50 2-D solves had not completed within the verification window; the verdict rests on the deterministic code trace plus the exact analytic match to the claimed measurement.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for eviction/guards: no popitem/OrderedDict/maxsize on _HOMOG_CACHE anywhere; checked _cache_registry.py — it only registers clear functions, no auto-eviction; checked solve_vs_wavelength and solve_vs_angle callers for per-sweep clears — none; checked whether the JAX path bypasses the cache (it does not; bname is merely part of the key); checked docs — the cache comment at _core.py:2165-2168 says 'cleared via the library cache registry', i.e. manual-clear is the only documented mechanism, so unbounded growth during a sweep is not an intended design with a guard; checked tests — none pin cache size. Could not refute.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Convert _HOMOG_CACHE to the library's v4.12.2 bounded-LRU OrderedDict pattern (move_to_end on hit, popitem(last=False) over a small maxsize, under the existing _HOMOG_LOCK), and stop storing W — it is always eye(2N), so cache (V, kz) and rebuild W at the call site, halving entry size.

### [P2-18] RCWAStack.solve(symmetry=True) stores recentering-gauge per-order amplitudes, silently corrupting per_order_amplitudes / to_multiorder_field / to_jones_field phases

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/rcwa/stack.py:1880`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The even-parity cascade recentres a non-origin-centred cell by the diagonal gauge d_m = exp(-0.5j*phi.order) (_core._recentering_phase) and returns (r, t) WITHOUT undoing the gauge; _solve_once stores these directly into rx/ry/tx/ty rows (line 1880: 'r, t = sym_rt[_col]'). Per-order |r|^2 efficiencies and the zeroth-order Jones are gauge-invariant (documented), but RCWAResult.per_order_amplitudes, to_multiorder_field and to_jones_field(order=m) are NOT gated on symmetry and consume the raw complex amplitudes: cross-order relative phases carry a linear ramp, i.e. the reconstructed multi-order field is that of a LATERALLY SHIFTED structure. Only retain_internal is blocked on the symmetry path; the modal bridge is not.

**Failure scenario.** A pillar centred at period/2 (the standard centred-feature cell), 2-layer stack, normal incidence, nox=noy=4: solve(symmetry=True) vs solve(symmetry=False) gives efficiencies identical to 3.5e-14, but per_order_amplitudes phases differ by a ramp up to +/-0.39 rad and to_multiorder_field Ex differs by up to 0.075 in complex value and 0.059 in |Ex| (a genuinely different interference pattern). Any downstream free-space propagation of that JonesField (the library's own pipeline) images the wrong lateral feature position.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace: _core.py:1080-1110 recentres layer P/Q by the diagonal gauge d=_recentering_phase and the _symmetric_cascade_rt docstring (lines 1069-1071) explicitly states results are returned in the recentred gauge with no undo (justified only for |r|^2/|t|^2); stack.py:1880 stores these r,t directly into rx/ry/tx/ty and the modal dict (stack.py:1910) is built unconditionally, feeding per_order_amplitudes (line 218), to_multiorder_field (394) and to_jones_field(order!=0) (307) with no symmetry gating. Probe (C:/tmp/lum_audit_probes/verify_f1_symgauge.py + verify_f1_shift.py; 32-sample pixel cell, square pillar centred at sample 10, 2 layers, nox=noy=4, normal incidence): symmetry=True vs False gives max|dR|=3.3e-14, max|dT|=7.4e-14, zeroth-order Jones diff 2.5e-13 (gauge-invariant as documented), but per-order transmission amplitude phases differ up to 3.1416 rad while |amp| diff is 7.6e-14; the discrepancy equals the conjugated recentering gauge EXACTLY (|ExT - conj(d_inv)*ExF| = 8.5e-14 / 4.3e-14 for the two pol rows, phi_read=-0.75*pi). to_multiorder_field differs by up to 1.71 in complex Ex and 0.97 in |Ex| on a field of max amplitude 1.64, and matches the full-solve field np.roll'ed by -6 px (of 32) in BOTH axes to 5.2e-13 -- i.e. exactly the field of a laterally shifted structure (shift = x0 - P/2 from the angle() wrap). to_jones_field((1,0)) complex Ex differs by 0.167 at level 0.151 (pure phase).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a gauge-undo or symmetry gating in _solve_once and RCWAResult: only retain_internal is blocked on the symmetry path; the modal dict at stack.py:1910 is stored unconditionally. Checked documentation: solve() docstring and CHANGELOG claim failed preconditions fall back 'bit-identically' but never warn that the SUCCESS path stores gauged amplitudes; _core.py docstrings only claim efficiency gauge-invariance. Searched tests: test_v5_6_1_rcwa_symmetry.py and test_v5_14_2_backlog_batch.py compare only efficiencies under symmetry=True; per_order_amplitudes/to_multiorder_field tests (test_rcwa.py, test_v5_6_rcwa_convergence.py, test_v5_11_0) never use symmetry=True. Checked whether the gauge is trivial for centred features: it is trivial only when the centre sits at sample 0 or wraps to phi=0 (e.g. exactly P/2 ON the sample grid); half-pixel-centred and generic off-origin centres (the common cases the fast path exists for) get a nontrivial ramp. No refutation found.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Have _symmetric_cascade_rt also return the gauge d2 and un-gauge r,t (multiply by the appropriate d/d* factor) before storing in the modal dict, or gate/warn in per_order_amplitudes / to_multiorder_field / to_jones_field(order!=0) when the result came from the symmetry path.

### [P2-19] Unknown Zemax SURFTYPEs (TOROIDAL, ODDASPHE, BICONICX, DGRATING, ...) silently parsed as EVENASPH, turning their PARM values into huge bogus aspheric coefficients

- **Location:** `lumenairy/io/prescriptions_zemax.py:384`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The loader's freeform branch only special-cases QBFS/QCON; every other TYPE falls into the else-branch (lines 384-411) that interprets PARM n as the even-asphere r^(2n) coefficient and rescales it by 1/unit_scale^(power-1). For sibling surface types the PARM table means something entirely different (TOROIDAL PARM 1 = radius of rotation in mm; ODDASPHE PARM n = coefficient of r^n; DGRATING PARM = grating lines/um), so the prescription silently acquires enormous fake aspheric sag with no warning or raise.

**Failure scenario.** A .zmx with 'TYPE TOROIDAL' and 'PARM 1 100.0' (100 mm rotation radius) loads with aspheric_coeffs={2: 1e5} [1/m], adding 0.625 m (!) of fake sag at r=2.5 mm — probe-confirmed. The only warning emitted is the unrelated unknown-glass warning; apply_real_lens then produces a completely wrong wavefront.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_zemax_f1.py): 3-surface .zmx with 'TYPE TOROIDAL' + 'PARM 1 100.0' loads via load_zemax_zmx with surfaces element aspheric_coeffs == {2: 100000.0} [1/m] and computed fake sag 0.625 m at r=2.5 mm; 'warnings emitted:' list was EMPTY. Code trace: TYPE stored verbatim (line 163), only QBFS/QCON branch at line 346; all other types with nonempty PARM fall into the EVENASPH interpretation at lines 405-411 (power=2*parm_num, /unit_scale**(power-1) rescale => *1e5 for PARM 1).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched entire module and package for a type whitelist, unknown-type warning, or caller-side guard: only warnings in the file are unknown-glass (lines 530, 987); grep for TOROID/ODDASPHE/DGRATING found no handling anywhere in lumenairy/io. Checked tests (test_v4_15_1_agent_a.py) — they cover QBFS/QCON only, no unknown-type case. Docstring (lines 37-38) documents supported types but that does not prevent the silent-corruption path; the docstring itself never says unsupported types degrade safely.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Whitelist STANDARD/EVENASPH/QBFS/QCON in the coefficient-extraction branch and warnings.warn (or raise) on any other TYPE that carries a nonempty PARM table, skipping the coefficients.

### [P2-20] Zemax exporter silently drops aspheric_coeffs on mirror elements (and all Q-type freeform keys) — load->export->load is not identity for aspheric mirrors

- **Location:** `lumenairy/io/prescriptions_zemax.py:1377`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** load_zemax_zmx stores 'aspheric_coeffs' on mirror elements (line 427) and Q-type keys via q_extra on both mirrors and refractive surfaces (lines 432, 453), but _export_zemax_zmx_full's mirror branch (lines 1377-1385) emits only TYPE STANDARD/CURV/CONI/GLAS MIRROR/DISZ/DIAM — no EVENASPH type switch and no PARM lines — and neither exporter has any QBFS/QCON emission path at all. An even-aspheric mirror (e.g., aspherized OAP) or any Forbes Q-type surface silently degrades to base conic on export, with no warning.

**Failure scenario.** rx = load_zemax_zmx('aspheric_mirror_design.zmx') where a GLAS MIRROR surface has TYPE EVENASPH + PARM 2..4; export_zemax_zmx(rx, out) writes the mirror as a pure conic; reloading out gives elements[k]['aspheric_coeffs'] is None. The cross-verification workflow the exporter exists for (compare LumenAiry vs OpticStudio) then compares against the WRONG surface, and the discrepancy is attributed to the wave code.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_zemax_f2.py): prescription with mirror element aspheric_coeffs={4:1e6} exported via export_zemax_zmx -> file shows the mirror as 'TYPE STANDARD / CONI -1.000000 / GLAS MIRROR' with ZERO PARM lines and zero warnings; reload gives aspheric_coeffs = None. Probe f2b: Q-bfs refractive surface (freeform_type='q_bfs', q_bfs_coeffs=[1e-6,2e-6]) exported -> 'QBFS in exported file? False | PARM in file? False'; reload gives freeform_type=None, q_bfs_coeffs=None. Code trace: mirror branch lines 1377-1385 has no EVENASPH switch; grep for freeform_type/q_bfs_coeffs/q_con_coeffs hits only loader lines 40-498, never either writer.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked both writer paths (_export_zemax_zmx_full lines 1377-1412 and the lens-only writer lines 1555-1603) — only refractive branches emit EVENASPH/PARM; no Q-type emission anywhere. Checked exporter docstrings for a documented limitation excusing mirrors/Q-types: the 3.7.0 note explicitly advertises round-trip preservation of fold geometry, and the refractive-asphere path comment says 'so the coefficients survive a Zemax round-trip', making the mirror asymmetry clearly unintended. No test covers aspheric-mirror or Q-type export round-trip.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Add the same TYPE EVENASPH + PARM emission to the mirror branch of _export_zemax_zmx_full, and add QBFS/QCON emission (TYPE + PARM 0 norm radius + PARM n coefficients) to both writers, or at minimum warnings.warn when dropping these keys.

### [P2-21] set_max_ram() budget override is silently bypassed by pick_batch_size/should_split and by the runtime parallel_amp RAM guard

- **Location:** `lumenairy/memory.py:315`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** set_max_ram's docstring (memory.py:82-86) promises 'All memory-aware code paths in the library (e.g. ASM propagation, batch-size selection) consult get_ram_budget'. But pick_batch_size (line 314-315) and should_split (line 350-351) default `available` to available_memory_bytes(), which reads psutil directly and never consults _MAX_RAM_OVERRIDE. The same contract violation exists at a call site: the apply_real_lens_traced parallel_amp auto-guard (elements/_lens_traced.py:1618-1621) reads psutil.virtual_memory().available directly, so a pinned budget cannot force the doubled parallel working set off. Only asm.py:331 and check_sim_memory honor get_ram_budget.

**Failure scenario.** User on a cgroup-limited HPC node (psutil reports the 256 GB host, cgroup allows 30 GB) follows the documented remedy set_max_ram(30). pick_batch_size/should_split still size against ~200 GB 'available' -> single giant batch -> cgroup OOM-kill. Same box: apply_real_lens_traced sees free=200 GB >= parallel_amp_min_free_gb=48 and keeps the ~2x parallel amp working set despite the 30 GB pin -> OOM mid-lens.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (this box, 102.6 GB actually available): set_max_ram(1); get_ram_budget()=1073741824; should_split(int(0.9*1024**3)) returned False (a 1 GB budget at safety=0.5 demands True); pick_batch_size(100, int(0.2*1024**3)) returned 100 (budget implies 2). Code: memory.py:314-315 and 350-351 default available=available_memory_bytes(); memory.py:141-149 reads psutil.virtual_memory().available and never checks _MAX_RAM_OVERRIDE. Runtime guard: _lens_traced.py:1617-1621 uses _psutil.virtual_memory().available directly to decide the ~2x parallel amp working set; grep confirms nothing under lumenairy/elements/ imports get_ram_budget. set_max_ram docstring (memory.py:82-86) promises 'batch-size selection' consults get_ram_budget.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched all in-library callers: asm.py:330-331 and check_sim_memory (memory.py:760-761) DO honor get_ram_budget; no internal batching path actually calls pick_batch_size (only print_memory_report calls should_split, passing psutil available explicitly), so the utility-function half only bites user code relying on the documented default; pick_batch_size/should_split's own docstrings document the psutil default (internally consistent but contradicting set_max_ram's promise). Checked _lens_traced.py 1590-1626 for any get_ram_budget/override consultation - none; the only ways to disable parallel amp are the kwarg, set_lens_parallel_amp(False), or set_low_memory(True), not the pinned budget. No test covers set_max_ram interaction with these paths (existing tests only cover negative/zero rejection).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Make pick_batch_size/should_split default available to get_ram_budget() and route the _lens_traced.py parallel_amp free-RAM guard through min(get_ram_budget(), psutil available).

### [P2-22] Row-band (chunked) lens estimate ignores parallel_amp (and slant_correction) although the runtime chunked path still doubles the working set

- **Location:** `lumenairy/memory.py:583`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The chunked branch of estimate_lens_memory (lines 583-602) computes total from _LENS_CHUNKED_COMPLEX_ARRAYS + newton + one band, referencing neither parallel_amp nor slant_correction; the whole-grid branch doubles f64_core and complex_part for parallel_amp (lines 621-623). But at runtime the chunked mode does NOT disable parallel amp: apply_real_lens_traced (_lens_traced.py:1615-1683) runs the amp and amp(pw) apply_real_lens legs concurrently, passing sag_chunk_rows into BOTH, so two full sets of resident complex fields + in-glass ASM scratch coexist. The 18.4 GB calibration anchor was measured with parallel_amp=False (test_memory_guardrail.py header, line 7). Since v5.17.0 chunking is the AUTO default at N>=4096 and parallel_amp defaults True, the DEFAULT large-N configuration is estimated as if sequential.

**Failure scenario.** check_sim_memory(32768, 'complex64', ray_subsample=16) with defaults (parallel_amp=True, chunked auto) reports ~103 GB and 'fits' on a ~110 GB budget; the actual run keeps the parallel legs (free >= 48 GB guard) and coexists two chunked apply_real_lens working sets -> peak tens of GB above the estimate -> the OOM the guardrail exists to prevent. Conversely the 'set parallel_amp=False (byte-identical)' claw-back rung (line 800) re-estimates to the IDENTICAL number in chunked mode, so it can never appear as a fitting recommendation even when it genuinely claws back memory.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: estimate_lens_memory(16384,'complex64',ray_subsample=16) is 15.20 GB identically for parallel_amp=True, parallel_amp=False, and slant_correction=True (chunked is the auto-default at N>=4096 via _resolve_sag_chunk_rows, _lens_real.py:136-144); forced whole-grid (sag_chunk_rows=0) gives 36.97 vs 21.94 GB (ratio 1.685). Code: chunked branch memory.py:584-603 computes total from _LENS_CHUNKED_COMPLEX_ARRAYS + newton + one band and never reads parallel_amp/slant_correction; whole-grid branch doubles at memory.py:622-624. Runtime: apply_real_lens_traced's parallel decision (_lens_traced.py:1613-1626) has no coupling to sag_chunk_rows and both concurrent legs receive sag_chunk_rows (_lens_traced.py:1645, 1670) - chunked mode runs parallel by default. Calibration anchors explicitly measured with parallel_amp=False (memory.py:469-477; test_memory_guardrail.py:7). Claw-back probe: check_sim_memory(32768,'complex64',ray_subsample=16, available=20e9) -> fits=False, peak 79.0 GB, ZERO recommendations; the 'set parallel_amp=False (byte-identical)' rung (memory.py:800-801) can never appear in chunked mode because its re-estimate is byte-identical to the failing peak.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Grepped _lens_traced.py for any chunked->sequential coupling (sag_chunk_rows near the _use_parallel_amp decision) - none; the only parallel gates are preserve_input_phase, the kwarg/global, and the psutil free-GB guard. Checked whether the chunked calibration might already include parallel doubling - no: the CALIBRATION note (memory.py:469-471) and the test-file header both pin parallel_amp=False for the anchors. Checked fast_analytic_phase - it skips the second leg only when that opt-in flag is set; the default parallel path runs both apply_real_lens calls concurrently. Did NOT measure actual peak RSS of a parallel-vs-sequential chunked run (needs a real lens prescription and a multi-minute large-N run), so the exact GB shortfall at N=32768 is unquantified - but the estimator inconsistency and the dead claw-back rung are airtight from code + probes. Minor: the finding's quoted 22.48 GB repro figure does not match current constants (15.20 GB for the same args); the qualitative claim reproduces exactly.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Either model the parallel doubling of the leg-local working set (and slant band transients) in the chunked estimate, or force sequential amp in chunked mode so estimate and runtime agree - which also revives the parallel_amp=False claw-back rung.

### [P2-23] set_low_memory(False) never reverts the aggressive complex64 default dtype -- silent precision downgrade persists after 'restoring defaults'

- **Location:** `lumenairy/memory.py:916`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** set_low_memory's docstring says the change 'round-trips via set_low_memory(False)', and the aggressive branch (lines 899-913) sets the process-wide default field dtype to complex64. But the else branch (lines 914-919) restores only the four byte-safe knobs (plan cache, parallel_amp, double buffer, auto-promote); set_default_complex_dtype is never restored, and the returned `prior` dict (which does contain 'complex_dtype') is not consumed by the False path.

**Failure scenario.** set_low_memory(True, aggressive=True) for one big run, then set_low_memory(False) believing defaults are back. Every subsequent propagation in the process runs complex64 (~80 dB dynamic range) with no warning -- deep-null / stray-light results silently wrong-precision while the four visible knobs read as shipped defaults.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: get_default_complex_dtype() == complex128; set_low_memory(True, aggressive=True) -> complex64; set_low_memory(False) -> STILL complex64. Code: aggressive branch memory.py:900-907 captures prior['complex_dtype'] and sets complex64; the enabled=False branch memory.py:915-920 restores only plan cache (8), parallel_amp (True), double buffer (True), auto-promote (True) - set_default_complex_dtype is never called and the returned prior dict is not consumed. Docstring memory.py:858-859: 'Returns the prior values so the change round-trips via set_low_memory(False)'.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the docstring scopes the round-trip to the safe set only - it does not. Searched tests: test_memory_guardrail.py:135-149 (test_set_low_memory_roundtrip_and_byte_safe_defaults) only exercises set_low_memory(True) without aggressive, so no test covers the aggressive dtype restore. Checked for any other reset path restoring the dtype - none in memory.py. Mitigant: the aggressive branch does emit a RuntimeWarning at enable time and returns prior, so a careful caller could restore manually, but the documented False path silently leaves complex64 as the process-wide default.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** In the enabled=False branch, reset the default complex dtype to complex128 (or accept/replay the prior dict), and add an aggressive round-trip test.

### [P2-24] User-supplied bounds are silently dropped for scipy methods that DO support bounds (Powell, Nelder-Mead, TNC, COBYQA)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/optimize/driver.py:1189`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** In the generic minimize branch, `'bounds': bounds if method in ('L-BFGS-B', 'SLSQP', 'trust-constr') else None` discards the parameterization's bounds for every other local method. scipy >= 1.7 honours bounds for Powell, Nelder-Mead, TNC, COBYLA/COBYQA (verified: scipy 1.17.1 Powell with bounds=[(0,1)] on (x-2)^2 returns x=1.0). Unlike the method='lm' branch, which warns loudly about its bounds behaviour (line 1058), this drop is completely silent — no warning, no error.

**Failure scenario.** design_optimize(param_with_bounds, ..., method='Powell') on a lens where a thickness has bounds (1e-3, 8e-3): the optimizer freely walks the thickness negative or a radius through zero, evaluates unphysical prescriptions (which the merit sentinels partially mask), and can converge to an out-of-bounds 'optimum' that violates the user's stated constraints without any diagnostic.

**Verification evidence.** driver.py:1187-1193: `'bounds': bounds if method in ('L-BFGS-B', 'SLSQP', 'trust-constr') else None` in the generic minimize branch. Probe (scipy 1.17.1, repo env): raw `so.minimize((x-2)^2, [0.5], method='Powell', bounds=[(0,1)])` returns x=1.000000 (same for Nelder-Mead) — scipy DOES honour the bounds. Through `design_optimize(RawParameterization(x0=[0.5], bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]), [CallableMerit], method='Powell')`: x_opt=2.000000, max x evaluated=3.500000, 15 merit evaluations outside [0,1], zero warnings emitted (Nelder-Mead: x_opt=2.000000, 31 evals outside, max 2.875). Contrast: the method='lm' branch warns loudly at driver.py:1058-1069 about its bounds behaviour, and 'de'/'dual_annealing' raise when bounds are missing. No docstring documents the restriction (parameterizations.py:213 just says 'Per-parameter bounds for bounded scipy solvers'), and grep of tests/ finds zero Powell/Nelder-Mead coverage, so no test enshrines the drop as intended.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for an up-front method-compat check (the v4.16 constraint check at driver.py:446-472 covers constraints only, not bounds); looked for a warning analogous to the lm-branch one (none); checked design_optimize docstrings in core.py/driver.py for a documented bounds-method restriction (none); grepped tests for Powell/Nelder-Mead coverage (none); ran the actual optimizer end-to-end to see whether some other layer (parameterization, merit sentinels) keeps iterates in-box — it does not (final optimum 2.0 lies outside the user's [0,1] box).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Pass `bounds` through for every minimize method (scipy>=1.7 accepts bounds for Powell/Nelder-Mead/TNC/COBYLA/COBYQA and raises a clear error for methods that truly cannot), or at minimum warn like the lm branch when non-None bounds are being discarded.

### [P2-25] Wrapper-merit meshgrid cache keys on the aperture VALUE: aperture_diameter as a free variable causes 100% cache misses plus up to 32 grid-sized payloads retained, with 6 aperture-independent N x N arrays duplicated per key

- **Location:** `lumenairy/optimize/wrapper_merits.py:158`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The cache key is (Ny, Nx, dx, aperture_key, dtype) where aperture_key embeds the scalar aperture value (line 116) or the array content hash. Every entry stores SEVEN N x N arrays (X, Y, Y_factor, X_factor, r_squared float64 = 40 B/px; E_ones complex128 = 16 B/px; mask 1 B/px, ~57 B/px total), of which six (all except mask) depend only on (N, dx, dtype) yet are duplicated per aperture key. DesignParameterization explicitly supports ('aperture_diameter',) as a free-var path, and every FD perturbation of that variable produces a distinct float -> a distinct key -> a rebuild (defeating the documented 'one meshgrid build per run' contract in the module header, lines 74-83) AND fills the 32-slot LRU with near-identical payloads that persist at module level after the run.

**Failure scenario.** design_optimize with MultiFieldMerit + free_vars including ('aperture_diameter',) at N=2048: each entry ~239 MB; the FD loop generates >32 distinct aperture values per gradient, so the LRU saturates at 32 x 239 MB ~ 7.6 GB retained module-level until clear_asm_caches() is called, while the cache hit rate is ~0% (every eval pays the full meshgrid + mask build the cache was created to avoid). At N=4096 a single entry is ~956 MB.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (py -P C:/tmp/probe_wrapper_merit_cache.py, repo root): 40 FD-scale-distinct aperture floats -> _WRAPPER_MERIT_MESHGRID_BUILDS == 40 (zero hits), cache len pinned at 32; an exact-repeat second pass produced 40 MORE builds (sequential-scan LRU thrash: >32 keys cycled in order evict each entry before reuse), i.e. literal 0% hit rate. Payload measured at 57.0 B/px exactly (X/Y/Y_factor/X_factor/r_squared float64 + E_ones complex128 + bool mask) -> 239.1 MB/entry @N=2048, 7.65 GB for 32 entries, 956.3 MB @N=4096 — all matching the claim. Two entries differing only in aperture: all six non-mask arrays content_equal=True, shared_object=False (fully duplicated), and at FD scale (5e-3 vs 5.0001e-3, N=256, dx=1e-5) even the masks were pixel-identical. Key path: wrapper_merits.py:116 ('scalar', float(aperture)) -> :158 key; :170-232 all seven arrays built+stored per key; :85-86 LRU=32. Free-var path ('aperture_diameter',) is explicitly documented (parameterizations.py:118) and classified (:72); build() writes the raw FD float into the prescription which the three merits pass into the cache (wrapper_merits.py:407-412, 616-621, 860-876). No per-run clear exists — only the manual clear_asm_caches() registry hook (:236-269), so the 32 payloads persist at module level after the run.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for (1) aperture quantization anywhere on the key path — none, raw float(aperture) is keyed; (2) a per-run or per-design_optimize cache clear — none, only manual clear_asm_caches(); (3) doc making per-aperture keys intended — the counter doc (:80-83) is worded per-(N,dx,aperture)-signature so technically holds, but the perf comment at :613-614 ('drops ... to 1 per optimisation run') is defeated; (4) callers preventing the state — DesignParameterization explicitly supports ('aperture_diameter',) and feeds unrounded FD floats through. One mechanism overstatement found: '~0% hit rate' holds only when aperture changes on nearly every eval (sole free var / line-searched); with many free vars, only aperture-perturbed FD legs + each iteration's new base value miss, so the 32-payload saturation accrues over >=32 iterations rather than within one gradient. Memory conclusion unchanged.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Split the cache: key the six aperture-independent arrays on (N, dx, dtype) only, and cache the boolean mask separately keyed on the pixel-quantized aperture radius (e.g. int(round((ap/2/dx)**2 in px^2))) so FD-scale perturbations that produce identical masks share one entry.

### [P2-26] Real-valued E_in is fed uncast into _fft2; pyFFTW rejects it and the shape is permanently blacklisted for ALL dtypes, with a misleading 'memory pressure' warning

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/asm.py:392`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** angular_spectrum_propagate explicitly supports non-complex input (falls back to DEFAULT_COMPLEX_DTYPE for H) but never casts E_in before the FFT at line 392. _fft2 dispatches on x.dtype: for float64/float32 input >=FFTW_MIN_SIZE it builds an in-place real->real FFTW_FORWARD plan, which pyfftw rejects with ValueError ('Invalid direction: The direction is not valid for the scheme'). The except handler calls _handle_pyfftw_failure, which (a) emits a RuntimeWarning blaming 'aligned contiguous buffer allocation failed under memory pressure' (wrong root cause) and (b) adds the bare shape tuple to _PYFFTW_BAD_SHAPES (fft_infra.py:1561), which is keyed WITHOUT dtype. Every subsequent _fft2/_ifft2 at that shape -- including complex128 calls from any propagator/lens in the process -- silently skips pyFFTW forever and runs on scipy. The batch sibling casts non-complex input at asm.py:567, so the scalar path has drifted from it.

**Failure scenario.** User propagates a real float64 aperture mask (a documented supported input) at 2048x2048, then runs a long optimisation loop with complex128 fields at the same shape: one spurious warning, then every FFT in the loop is on scipy instead of pyFFTW (~2-10x slower per the module's own benchmarks) with no further indication and no way to notice except reset_fft_backend().

**Verification evidence.** Probe (py 3.14.6, numpy 2.4.4, pyfftw 0.15.1, FFTW_MIN_SIZE=256): E=np.ones((256,256)) float64 -> angular_spectrum_propagate raises inside pyFFTW with 'ValueError: Invalid direction: The direction is not valid for the scheme', warning text blames 'aligned contiguous buffer allocation failed under memory pressure'; _PYFFTW_BAD_SHAPES=={(256,256)} afterwards. A following complex128 call at the same shape leaves _PYFFTW_PLAN_CACHE EMPTY (0 plans -> permanently on scipy); control run (complex-first after clearing the blacklist) builds both ('fwd',(256,256),'<c16',24) and ('inv',...) plans. Code trace: asm.py:272-275 sets target_cdtype for non-complex input but E_in is never cast before _fft2 at asm.py:392; batch sibling DOES cast at asm.py:567; _fft2 gate (fft_infra.py:1637-1639) dispatches on x.dtype with no iscomplexobj check; _handle_pyfftw_failure keys the blacklist on bare shape (fft_infra.py:1552,1560-1561).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a cast anywhere upstream (none in the scalar path; only H gets astype). Checked whether real input is unsupported: the public docstring says 'ndarray (complex)' but the in-code comment at asm.py:271-275 explicitly documents non-complex input as a supported fallback, and the batch sibling casts, so intent is support. Searched tests for coverage: real-input tests exist (test_audit_propagation.py:416, test_perf_v4_12_0_asymptotic.py:1030) but exercise propagate_hf_chebyshev_quadrature or grids below FFTW_MIN_SIZE=256, so the pyFFTW path is never hit by the suite. Checked whether the blacklist is dtype-keyed elsewhere (backend/fft.py:196 uses the same bare-shape set). No guard found; probe reproduces exactly.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Cast E_in to target_cdtype before the FFT in the scalar path (mirroring asm.py:567), and additionally require np.iscomplexobj(x) (or include dtype kind in the bad-shape key) in the _fft2/_ifft2 pyFFTW gate so a dtype rejection cannot poison the shape for complex callers.

### [P2-27] angular_spectrum_propagate_batch pays a full wasted FFT+IFFT pair on a garbage proxy field every call; measured 2.07x SLOWER than two scalar calls, contradicting its '30-60% faster' docstring

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/asm.py:574`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** To fetch H, the batch path allocates an uninitialised (Ny,Nx) proxy (xp.empty, line 574) and runs the FULL scalar angular_spectrum_propagate on it with return_transfer_function=True (lines 575-578), discarding E_out. That costs one full-size forward FFT + inverse FFT + 4 fftshifts + an extra fftshift(H).copy() (asm.py:402-404) per batch call -- even on H-cache hits. Additionally the batch propagation itself still uses the 4-shift centered-H idiom (lines 590-596) although the scalar path folded to 2 shifts in v5.5.3. Net: for the primary documented use case (JonesField Ex/Ey, B=2) the batch entry point does 6 full-grid 2-D transforms where two scalar calls do 4.

**Failure scenario.** JonesField propagation (B=2) at N=1024, warm caches, pyFFTW backend: batch = 200.5 ms vs 2x scalar = 96.9 ms (ratio 2.07; proxy H-fetch alone measured 73.2 ms/call). Any code that trusted the docstring and switched to the batch API got a ~2x slowdown, worse cold (first call also pays 3-D plan build + MEASURE promotion of the (B,Ny,Nx) key).

**Verification evidence.** Probe at N=1024, B=2, complex128, warm caches: monkeypatch-counted dispatcher calls during ONE warm batch call = {_fft2:1, _ifft2:1, _fft2_nd:1, _ifft2_nd:1} — the scalar FFT+IFFT pair on the xp.empty proxy (asm.py:574-578) runs even on H-cache HIT. Timing (10 warm reps): batch = 360.6 ms/call vs 2x scalar = 55.1 ms (ratio 6.55x SLOWER on this machine, even worse than the claimed 2.07x); proxy H-fetch alone = 38.5 ms/call of pure waste. Outputs agree to 1.35e-15 so it is purely perf. The batch also still uses the 4-shift centered-H idiom (asm.py:590-596) that the scalar path folded to 2 shifts in v5.5.3 (asm.py:316-318,385-392). The regression hits the in-library JonesField propagation path (lumenairy/elements/polarization.py:226), not just external callers.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether an H-cache hit short-circuits the proxy propagation — it does not: return_transfer_function only adds the fftshift+copy, the full FFT/IFFT at asm.py:391-395 always runs. Checked whether the ND path amortizes the cost (docstring claim of fused 3-D plan) — counted calls show the extra 2-D pair is unconditional, and warm timing shows the total is several times slower, not 30-60% faster. Verified numerical equivalence (1.35e-15) to rule out the timing comparing different work. Ratio magnitude is machine/plan-state dependent (ND pyFFTW plan quality), but the direction (slower, never faster warm at B=2) is airtight from the transform count: 6 full-grid 2-D transform-equivalents vs 4.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Build H directly through the freq-grid/_h_cache path (factor a build-H helper out of the scalar function) instead of propagating a garbage proxy, and fold the batch spectrum-domain shifts using the same natural-layout-H trick as the v5.5.3 scalar path.

### [P2-28] angular_spectrum_propagate_tilted still uses the 4-shift centered-H idiom; the exact 2-shift natural-H fold (already applied to plain ASM in v5.5.3) saves ~19% of the cache-warm call

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/asm.py:814`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** L814-816 runs E_fft = fftshift(_fft2(ifftshift(E_demod))); E_prop = fftshift(_ifft2(ifftshift(E_fft * H))) with the tilted H cached in CENTERED layout (built L768-811, stored L811). The identical algebraic fold documented at L386-390 for plain ASM (store H = ifftshift(H) in the cache, drop the two spectrum-domain shifts) applies verbatim here and is exact for any N: probe shows max|diff| = 0.0. Each avoided shift is a full read+write pass over the complex grid.

**Failure scenario.** Tilted-beam propagation (mainline for the TX free-space designs) at 4096^2 complex128, H-cache warm: measured 4-shift FFT stage 685 ms vs folded 406 ms -- 278 ms/call, 19% of the entire tilted call (1445 ms). At 16384^2 the two extra passes move ~16 GB of memory traffic per call.

**Verification evidence.** asm.py L811 stores tilted H CENTERED and L814-816 run the 4-shift idiom, while plain ASM (L376, L386-392) already uses the natural-H 2-shift fold with a comment stating it is 'algebraically EXACT for any N'. Probe (C:/tmp/probe_tilted_shift_verify.py, run with py -P; C:/tmp/inspect.py shadows stdlib otherwise) using the library's _fft2/_ifft2 and the ACTUAL cached tilted H (recovered via the exact ASM_TILTED cache key): folded pipeline is bit-identical to the library output (max|diff| = 0.0) at N=512 complex128, N=512 complex64 (carrier-mod-2pi path), and N=129 odd. Timing at 4096^2 complex128 cache-warm, best of 5: 4-shift = 634.7 ms vs 2-shift = 414.5 ms -> 220.2 ms saved (34.7% of FFT stage); full warm tilted call = 1569.2 ms -> 14.0% of the whole call (claim said 19%; same order, load-dependent).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Searched for any other reader of 'ASM_TILTED' cache entries that could depend on centered layout — the tag appears only inside this function, and the tilted API has no return_transfer_function parameter, so no external contract pins the cached H layout (plain ASM handles that case by re-centering at L402). (2) Tried to break bit-exactness at odd N where fftshift != ifftshift, and on the complex64 carrier path — both bit-identical, since ifftshift(fftshift(X)) = X exactly and permutations commute with elementwise products. (3) Checked docs/changelogs/v4.md L3161 for a documented reason to keep the centered layout — the note only covers cache disjointness/speedup, not layout.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Mirror v5.5.3: store the tilted H natural-layout via _h_cache_store(h_key, np.fft.ifftshift(H)) and replace L814-816 with E_prop = np.fft.fftshift(_ifft2(_fft2(np.fft.ifftshift(E_demod)) * H)).

### [P2-29] fresnel/fraunhofer complex64 path computes the quadratic-phase carrier argument entirely in float32 -- no f64 mod-2pi folding -- violating the documented precision contract and drifting from the ASM kernel and the MFT twins

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/fresnel.py:146`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** For complex64 inputs the coordinate grids are built at float32 (fdtype, lines 136-143), and under NEP-50 the weak Python-complex scalar 1j*k/(2*z) times a float32 array yields complex64, so the phase argument k/(2z)*(X1^2+Y1^2) at line 146 (and the output-plane prefactor at lines 162-164, and fraunhofer's at lines 288-289) is accumulated wholly in float32. set_default_complex_dtype's docstring (fft_infra.py:182-186) promises 'explicit kernel-phase mod-2pi folding everywhere accuracy depends on the absolute kernel argument'; the ASM kernel honours it (asm.py:352-356), the v5.17.1 tilted-ASM fix honours it (asm.py:741-748), and the MFT twin computes the identical carrier in float64 then casts (mft.py:536), but this path does not.

**Failure scenario.** complex64 Fresnel propagation of a 2048-grid, dx=2 um, z=5 mm field: max quad-phase argument 4.0e3 rad -> measured max carrier error 8.5e-4 (rms 1.0e-4) vs 4.0e-8 achievable with the documented f64-fold-then-cast; the error grows linearly with the argument (larger grids / shorter z / edge-of-grid energy), injecting phase speckle well above the c64 noise floor the docs promise. End-to-end on a centred Gaussian the effect is masked (3e-6 rel), but aperture-edge fields see the full carrier error.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (numpy 2.4.4, N=2048, dx=2um, z=5mm, wl=1.55um): argument dtype of (1j*k/(2*z))*(X1**2+Y1**2) with float32 grids is complex64, i.e. float32 accumulation exactly as claimed (fresnel.py L133/136-143/146; same pattern L162-164 and fraunhofer L276-289). Max quad-phase argument 3.4e3 rad; current path max carrier error 7.723e-4 (rms 8.855e-5) vs 4.148e-8 (rms 2.0e-8) with the asm.py:354-356 fold-then-cast recipe -- ~4 orders of magnitude. Error grows with argument: 1.79e-4 at 25% of max arg, 4.50e-4 at 50%, 7.72e-4 at 100%. End-to-end c64 vs c128 fresnel_propagate: annular edge-aperture field max|dE|/max|E| = 2.243e-4 (carrier error dominates, well above the c64 FFT floor) vs 7.2e-6 for a centred Gaussian -- confirming the masking claim. Contract sites verified: fft_infra.py:180-186 promises 'explicit kernel-phase mod-2pi folding everywhere accuracy depends on the absolute kernel argument'; asm.py:348-359 and asm.py:741-748 honour it; mft.py:518-536 builds the identical Fresnel input-plane carrier in float64 then casts. fresnel.py does neither.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Read all of fresnel_propagate and fraunhofer_propagate looking for a fold or f64 carrier construction elsewhere -- none exists. Checked whether the c64 path is undocumented/unsupported: no, v4.10 comment (L126-128) deliberately added dtype-honouring, and fft_infra's set_default_complex_dtype docstring explicitly promises the fold for single-precision propagation. Searched tests for complex64 Fresnel accuracy coverage: only dtype-preservation-style tests and an ASM-helper c64 test (test_audit_propagation.py:1901) exist; nothing pins c64 Fresnel against an f64 reference. Checked the MFT twin to see if the f64 carrier there was coincidence: mft.py builds ALL chirp coordinates as np.float64 by design, so the fresnel.py float32 grids are genuine drift. Tried to argue the error is at the c64 noise floor anyway: refuted by measurement (7.7e-4 vs 4.1e-8 achievable, and 2.2e-4 end-to-end vs ~7e-6 FFT-floor-dominated case).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Build X1/Y1 (and X2/Y2, and fraunhofer's X2/Y2) in float64 when target is complex64 and apply the asm.py:354-356 mod-2pi fold-then-cast recipe (or the mft.py f64-carrier-then-cast pattern) to the input-plane phase and output prefactor.

### [P2-30] apply_abcd_to_beamlets uses amplitude factor Q_new/Q_old instead of the Collins factor 1/(A + B*Q_in) -- output amplitude and piston wrong by a factor (C*q_in + D)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/gbd.py:613`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The Gaussian-beamlet on-axis amplitude through an ABCD system transforms as 1/(A + B/q_in) (Siegman ch. 20 / Collins integral; this is also what the sequential per-leg factors in propagate_beamlets_freespace compose to, since free space C=0,D=1 gives q/(q+t) and a thin lens B=0 gives 1). Line 613 instead applies qratio = Q_new/Q_old = q_in/q_out = (C*q_in + D)/(A + B/q_in), i.e. an extra multiplicative factor (C*q_in + D). For any system with C != 0 (every focusing system) this is a complex factor with |C*q_in+D| != 1, so the single-ABCD path disagrees with the sequential-leg path for the identical optical system in both magnitude and phase. The v4.11.1 axial_opl piston fix (added specifically so GBD superposes coherently with ASM/Fresnel cross-checks) is defeated by this spurious factor.

**Failure scenario.** propagate_gbd_through_prescription on a t1=20mm -> f=50mm lens -> t2=30mm system: single-ABCD amplitude ratio has |amp_abcd/amp_sequential| = 0.600 (= |C*q0+D|), i.e. output field 40% low in amplitude, 64% low in intensity, plus a wrong piston phase (abcd/seq = 0.161+0.578j). Absolute-photometry results and any coherent superposition of the ABCD-path output with a sequential-GBD/ASM reference arm are wrong.

**Verification evidence.** gbd.py:613 `qratio = Q_new/Q_old` with Q=1/q (line 199: Q=-1j/z_R) equals (C*q_in+D)/(A+B*Q_in), i.e. Collins factor times spurious (C*q_in+D). Probe (t1=20mm, f=50mm, t2=30mm, w0=5um, lam=1um): Q identical between paths (rel diff 2.3e-16) but amp_abcd/amp_seq = 0.6000-0.00157j = C*q0+D exactly (ratio/(C*q0+D) = 1.0+1.5e-11j); amp_seq/[exp(ikL)/(A+B*Q0)] = 1.0000000000000004, proving the sequential path IS the correct Collins result. Free-space-only ABCD (C=0): ratio = 1+0j, confirming C!=0 specificity. Worst case t1=f (front focal plane input, D=0): |amp_abcd/amp_seq| = 1.570796e-3 = z_R/f — output field ~640x too small. All beamlets share Q0 at decomposition, so the error is one constant complex factor: shape/normalized PSF intact, absolute photometry and coherent superposition (the stated purpose of the v4.11.1 axial_opl fix) wrong. Only user is propagate_gbd_through_prescription (gbd.py:767), a public API.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Tested whether the sequential path was the wrong one instead — refuted: it matches analytic Collins exp(ikL)/(A+B*Q0) to 1e-15 and matches the ABCD path for pure free space. (2) Searched tests for an amplitude oracle on apply_abcd_to_beamlets / propagate_gbd_through_prescription — only tests/unit/test_audit_glass.py, which pins axial_opl plumbing (spy + warning absence), never amplitude accuracy; no guard exists. (3) Checked whether the v5.4.6 conj-Q engineering convention could make Q_new/Q_old intentional — no: both paths use the same Q convention and reconstructor, so the internal inconsistency is convention-independent. (4) Read docstring/comments (lines 610-612): the 'q_in/q_out amplitude conservation' comment is itself the misstatement, not documentation of intended alternate behavior; even a bare thin lens via apply_abcd_to_beamlets (B=0) disagrees with apply_thin_lens_to_beamlets. (Finding's reported complex ratio 0.161+0.578j differs in phase from my on-axis probe 0.600-0.0016j — likely different bundle geometry in their repro — but |ratio|=0.5999 and the (C*q_in+D) mechanism match exactly.)

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace line 613 with qratio = 1.0/(A + B*Q_old) (cast to the amplitude dtype), keeping the axial_opl phase multiplication unchanged, so the composite-ABCD path reproduces the Collins/Siegman factor that the sequential legs compose to.

### [P2-31] propagate_hfpi's normalization warning contradicts the v4.10/v4.11.2 code and inline changelog: it claims 1/(j*lambda) and the MC solid-angle weight are NOT applied (they are), while the genuinely-missing 1/r spreading term is what actually breaks the docstring's promised 'relative-intensity ratios'

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/hfpi.py:477`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The .. warning:: block (lines 464-483) states the code does not apply (a) the 1/(j*lambda) prefactor, (b) the 1/r spreading, (c) the solid-angle weight 2*pi*(1-cos)/N. But (a) and (c) ARE applied since v4.10/v4.11.2 (init_paths_from_field lines 222-225, apply_aperture_diffraction lines 353-358, whose comments even claim 'absolute-photometry use is new'). Only (b) 1/r is truly absent (propagate_to_plane line 272 applies exp(ik*ds) with no 1/r). The warning additionally asserts 'relative-intensity ratios within a single experiment' are correct -- false: r differs per path/output pixel, so the missing 1/r (and the missing r^2/cos(theta_out) ray-binning Jacobian) biases the SPATIAL intensity profile, not just a global constant. Users get three mutually-inconsistent stories: warning says fully unnormalized, inline comments say absolutely calibrated since 4.10, actual code is neither.

**Failure scenario.** A user reads the v4.10/4.11.2 inline changelog ('absolute amplitudes were unphysical ... pre-4.10'; 'absolute-photometry use is new') and trusts HFPI absolute coupling efficiencies -> numbers are wrong by missing 1/r, N_pix source-area weighting, and output-pixel-area/cos(theta_out) binning factors. Conversely a user comparing near-axis vs wide-angle PSF wings trusts the warning's 'relative ratios correct' claim -> ratios are wrong because long-r paths are over-weighted by exactly the missing 1/r per path.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace airtight: warning block hfpi.py:464-483 asserts the code does NOT apply 1/(j*lambda), 1/r, or the solid-angle weight 2*pi*(1-cos)/N; but 1/(i*lambda) and solid_angle ARE applied at hfpi.py:222-225 (init_paths_from_field), 353-358 (apply_aperture_diffraction), and 692-695 (init_paths_stratified), with inline v4.10/v4.11.2 comments claiming 'absolute-photometry use is new'. Only 1/r is truly absent: propagate_to_plane (line 272) and _hfpi_segment_trace (line 969) apply only exp(i*k*ds); accumulate_to_grid (374-435) has no r-dependent factor. Numerical probe (single-pixel Huygens source, 4M paths, 60-deg cone, z=200um, lambda=0.5um): binned radial amplitude profile deviates from the physical cos(theta)/r law by a monotonic theta-dependent factor, ratio falling from 1.00 on-axis to 0.48 at cos(theta)=0.985 and 0.07 at cos(theta)=0.52 (~14x relative bias across the field) -> the warning's 'relative-intensity ratios within a single experiment' claim is quantitatively false. Caveat: bias direction is OPPOSITE to the finding's repro prediction -- wings come out systematically LOW, not high, because the implicit MC binning density (~1/r^2) plus intra-pixel phase decoherence outweigh the missing per-path 1/r; the finding's fix direction already names the binning Jacobian, so only its 'wings systematically high' sign is wrong.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Tried reading the warning as intentionally-conservative boilerplate: fails because it affirmatively lists three specific factors as 'not applied' (two are applied) and affirmatively guarantees relative ratios (probe shows up to ~14x spatial bias). Searched the whole file plus dispatch path for any 1/r or r^2 correction applied elsewhere: none exists. Checked whether the inline v4.10/4.11.2 changelog might postdate and supersede the warning: warning cites 'audit #3.1' and was left stale, confirming three mutually inconsistent doc stories (warning=fully unnormalized, inline comments=absolutely calibrated, code=neither).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Apply the per-path 1/r attenuation in propagate_to_plane/_hfpi_segment_trace plus the output-binning Jacobian (pixel-area/cos(theta_out), r^2 density correction), then rewrite the warning block to state exactly which factors are and are not applied so the three doc claims agree.

### [P2-32] HFPI end-to-end helpers pass output_dtype=E_in.dtype to accumulate_to_grid, so a REAL-dtype input field silently discards the imaginary part of every complex path weight

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/hfpi.py:560`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** accumulate_to_grid scatter-adds the complex path weights (which are intrinsically complex: init_paths_from_field multiplies by 1/(i*lambda) and every leg by exp(i*k*OPL)) into an output buffer whose dtype is forced to E_in.dtype by propagate_hfpi_freespace_aperture (line 560) and propagate_hfpi_through_prescription (line 909). For a real-valued E_in (a plain aperture/amplitude mask -- a mainline input; the module's own example-style usage builds boolean/float masks), np.add.at casts complex->float, keeping only the real part. The sibling hf.py fixed exactly this bug class in v4.10 (hf.py:230-239: 'force a complex dtype so a real-valued E_in ... doesn't silently strip the imaginary part'), so hfpi has drifted from its sibling's contract.

**Failure scenario.** E_in = np.ones((32,32), float64) through propagate_hfpi_freespace_aperture: output dtype is float64; each binned pixel is Re(sum of weights) instead of the complex field -- |E|^2 computed from it is Re(E)^2, wrong by the full Im(E)^2 term, and all phase information is destroyed. Only a numpy ComplexWarning is emitted (easily suppressed/missed); no error is raised. JAX path (out.at[].add) has the same cast issue.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (E_in=np.ones((32,32),float64), n_paths=20000, rng=42 through propagate_hfpi_freespace_aperture): output dtype float64; ComplexWarning 'Casting complex values to real discards the imaginary part' raised at hfpi.py:417; output exactly equals Re(complex-input run) (np.allclose True); total |E|^2 = 7.876e-14 vs 1.288e-13 for complex input -> 38.85% of intensity silently lost; max|Im(E)|/max|E| = 0.90. Weights are intrinsically complex regardless of E_in dtype (hfpi.py:224-225 multiplies by complex(1/(1j*wavelength)); probe: weights dtype complex128, mean|Im(w)|/mean|w| = 1.0). output_dtype=E_in.dtype forced at hfpi.py:560 and hfpi.py:909; buffer created at that dtype at hfpi.py:415, cast at np.add.at (hfpi.py:417). Sibling hf.py:230-239 contains the exact v4.10 fix for this bug class ('force a complex dtype so a real-valued E_in (e.g. a pure intensity mask) doesn't silently strip the imaginary part').

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked dispatcher (dispatch.py:579-613): E_in forwarded verbatim, no complex promotion anywhere; dispatcher field validation explicitly accepts real float arrays (dtype.kind=='f', dispatch.py:220), so real input is a supported mainline path. Grepped 11 hfpi test files: none feeds a real-dtype field, so no test pins or covers this. Docstrings nominally say 'complex' E_in, but hf.py's own fix comment treats real E_in as a legitimate use case, refuting an 'unsupported input' defense. JAX branch (out.at[].add into a real-dtype buffer, hfpi.py:406-407) has the same cast.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Mirror hf.py:230-239: promote a real E_in.dtype to the matching complex dtype (float64->complex128, float32->complex64) before passing output_dtype in both end-to-end helpers (or default accumulate_to_grid to paths.weights.dtype).

### [P2-33] ray_to_beamlet silently discards RayBundle.opd, zeroing all inter-beamlet piston phases in the advertised 'GBD coherent-recombination workflow'

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/bundles.py:121`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** BeamletBundle has no opl field; phase must be carried in the complex 'amplitude' (gbd.decompose_field_to_beamlets encodes E_in's phase there). ray_to_beamlet defaults amplitude to all-ones (line 145-146) and never touches ray_bundle.opd, so the accumulated OPL -- the exact quantity a raytrace produces and that coherent recombination interferes on -- is dropped without warning. It also ignores ray_bundle.alive (dead rays become full-amplitude beamlets), unlike ray_to_path which propagates both opl and alive.

**Failure scenario.** rays_from_field(E,...) (which deliberately seeds opd=phi/k0 'so subsequent OPD accumulation continues from the wave-optical state') -> trace(...) -> ray_to_beamlet(image_rays, wavelength=wv, waist0=w) -> reconstruct_field_from_beamlets: every beamlet gets phase 0 and dead/TIR rays contribute amplitude 1, so the reconstructed interference pattern is wrong (e.g. a focused beam's spherical wavefront becomes a flat one).

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/probes_bundles/probe_ray_to_beamlet_opd.py, run from repo root): two co-located rays with opd = [0, lambda/2] (633 nm) should destructively cancel; ray_to_beamlet default gives bb.amplitude = [1+0j, 1+0j, 1+0j] and reconstruct_field_from_beamlets |E|centre = 2.0 (fully constructive) vs 1.22e-16 when amplitude=exp(1j*k0*opd)*alive is hand-fed. Dead ray (alive=False) contributes |E| = 1.0 at its position by default vs 4.56e-60 with alive folded. Code trace: bundles.py:145-146 amplitude=np.ones; lines 148-154 pass no opd/alive; BeamletBundle (propagators/gbd.py:113-123) has only positions/directions/Q/amplitude/waist0 (hasattr(bb,'opl')==False, hasattr(bb,'alive')==False), so amplitude is the only phase carrier and reconstruct_field_from_beamlets sums it coherently (einsum over a_b). Contrast ray_to_path (bundles.py:111-118) which forwards opl and alive (probe: pb.opl=[0, 3.165e-7, 0], pb.alive=[T,T,F]). rays_from_field (raytrace/from_field.py:393-396) indeed seeds opd=phi/k0 as the finding claims, so the advertised chain rays_from_field -> trace -> ray_to_beamlet -> reconstruct drops exactly the interferometric quantity.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for guards/intent: (1) no internal caller of ray_to_beamlet exists outside re-exports and tests, so no upstream code folds opd for the user; (2) the only unit test (tests/unit/test_audit_raytrace.py:369 test_ray_to_beamlet_succeeds) checks shapes and Q sign only — its fixture even sets nonzero opd and a full alive mask but never asserts on amplitude, so nothing covers this; (3) grepped docs/, CHANGELOG.md, release notes for any instruction to fold opd into amplitude — none found; (4) the docstring (bundles.py:128-137) advertises 'switching from a geometric raytrace into a GBD coherent-recombination workflow' with no phase caveat, so silent phase loss is not documented intent; (5) checked whether BeamletBundle could carry opl elsewhere — it cannot (dataclass fields fixed). The amplitude kwarg is a valid escape hatch, but nothing tells the user it is mandatory for correct physics, and the default produces silently wrong interference.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Default amplitude to exp(1j * 2*pi/wavelength * ray_bundle.opd) * ray_bundle.alive (falling back to ones when opd absent), or at minimum add a prominent docstring warning that callers MUST fold opd and alive into the amplitude kwarg for coherent recombination.

### [P2-34] trace_jax_with_params silently traces mirrors / coord-breaks / biconics / freeforms as flat refractives -- the v4.10/v4.13.2 fail-loud guard was only added to trace_jax's builder (probe-confirmed)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/jax_trace.py:1560`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _build_jax_prescription (lines 838-863) raises NotImplementedError for is_mirror / glass_after=='MIRROR' / is_coordbrk / radius_y / conic_y / aspheric_coeffs_y / freeform. trace_jax_with_params -- documented since v3.5.6 as 'the canonical entry point' for differentiable prescriptions -- reads prescription['surfaces'] directly at line 1560 with NO such guard, so unsupported surface kinds are silently treated as refractive (a mirror with air/air becomes a no-op), reproducing exactly the pre-4.10 wrong-answer mode the guard was created to kill.

**Failure scenario.** rx={'surfaces':[{'radius':-0.2,'glass_before':'air','glass_after':'air','is_mirror':True}],'thicknesses':[0.1],'aperture_diameter':10e-3}: trace_jax(state,rx,wv) raises NotImplementedError, but trace_jax_with_params(state,rx,wv) returns rays with N=+1.0 (unreflected) -- an optimizer differentiating through a folded design gets physically meaningless outputs and gradients with no error.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_jax_trace_findings.py, run from repo root): rx with one is_mirror=True air/air surface -> trace_jax raised NotImplementedError('trace_jax does not yet support surface 0 with is_mirror...'); trace_jax_with_params(state, rx, 633e-9) returned N=[+1.0], alive=[True] -- the mirror traced as a refractive no-op exactly as claimed. Code: the validation loop lives only in _build_jax_prescription (jax_trace.py:838-863); trace_jax_with_params (line 1560ff) reads prescription['surfaces'] directly and extracts only radius/conic/aspheric_coeffs/glass strings, so is_mirror/is_coordbrk/radius_y/conic_y/aspheric_coeffs_y/freeform fields are silently dropped. No caller-side guard exists (grep: only validation/raytrace/test_raytrace.py parity tests with supported prescriptions). One nuance narrows the blast radius: the glass_after='MIRROR' variant is NOT silent -- get_glass_index raises ValueError "Glass 'MIRROR' not in registry" (fail-loud, though with a misleading message), so only the is_mirror-flag / coordbrk / biconic / freeform halves reproduce the silent pre-4.10 wrong-answer mode.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a shared validation helper or a caller that pre-validates (grep trace_jax_with_params across repo: only parity tests, __all__, changelogs; AUDIT_V4_12_1 documents the no-JaxPrescription path as intentional for perf, NOT the missing guard). Checked whether glass lookup incidentally fails loud: it does for glass_after='MIRROR' (ValueError) but NOT for is_mirror=True with real glasses, which is the canonical Surface representation. Ran the probe both default-float32 and JAX_ENABLE_X64 -- same result.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Hoist the unsupported-surface validation loop from _build_jax_prescription into a module-level helper and call it at the top of trace_jax_with_params as well.

### [P2-35] Per-surface 'semi_diameter' prescription key is honored by the JAX backend but silently ignored by the NumPy backend -- same prescription vignettes 1/25 vs 25/25 rays (probe-confirmed)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/trace.py:437`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** surfaces_from_prescription derives each Surface's semi_diameter only from prescription['aperture_diameter'] and the optional 'elements' list (trace.py lines 437-446); it never reads ps['semi_diameter']. _build_jax_prescription DOES read s.get('semi_diameter') (jax_trace.py lines 891-897) and never reads 'elements'. The two backends therefore apply different clear apertures to the identical prescription dict, breaking the trace-vs-trace_jax parity contract in both directions (hand-built dicts with per-surface sd: JAX clips, NumPy doesn't; Zemax-loaded dicts whose sd lives in 'elements': NumPy clips, JAX doesn't).

**Failure scenario.** rx with surfaces[0]['semi_diameter']=1e-3 and aperture_diameter=10e-3: NumPy trace keeps 25/25 rays alive (surface[0].semi_diameter resolved to 0.005), JAX trace keeps 1/25. Any cross-validation, JAX-optimize-then-NumPy-verify, or hybrid workflow gets inconsistent vignetting/throughput.

**Verification evidence.** Probe on identical prescription dicts (surfaces[0]['semi_diameter']=1e-3, aperture_diameter=10e-3): NumPy surfaces_from_prescription gives semi_diameters [0.005, 0.005] (per-surface key ignored — trace.py:434-446 reads only aperture_diameter and 'elements'); JAX _build_jax_prescription gives (0.001, 0.005) (jax_trace.py:891-898 reads s.get('semi_diameter')). Reverse direction with sd in 'elements': NumPy [0.001, 0.001] vs JAX (0.005, 0.005) — jax_trace.py never reads prescription['elements'] (grep: zero hits). Divergence confirmed in BOTH claimed directions; the 25-vs-1 alive-ray count follows directly from a 5x aperture difference at the first surface.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Grepped jax_trace.py for any 'elements' consumption (none); searched tests/unit (18 files mentioning semi_diameter, incl. test_v5_4_6_wave2_p1_parity.py) — no test pins numpy-vs-jax semi-diameter parity for per-surface sd, otherwise the probe divergence would have tripped it; checked trace_jax/JaxPrescription docstrings for a documented caveat that per-surface sd is JAX-only or that 'elements' is unsupported (none — docs present trace_jax as accepting the same prescription dict).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Make surfaces_from_prescription honor ps.get('semi_diameter') with the same min() tightening it applies to 'elements', and make _build_jax_prescription consult the 'elements' list the same way, so both backends resolve identical clear apertures.

### [P2-36] _register_fixed_index writes sentinel ('__fixed__','__fixed__','__fixed__') but glass.get_glass_index's user-fixed branch requires ('__user__','__fixed__','__fixed__') -- thin-lens/spherical/aspheric element traces raise ImportError on minimal installs

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/trace.py:1313`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** glass.py line 1384 only short-circuits to the _glass_cache fixed-index object when entry[0]=='__user__'. trace.py's local _register_fixed_index (a drifted duplicate of user_library.register_fixed_glass, which uses the correct sentinel at user_library.py:276) writes entry[0]=='__fixed__', so lookups fall through to the refractiveindex.info tuple branch. With the optional 'refractiveindex' package installed it works only by accident (line 1447 finds the pre-seeded _glass_cache object); without it, glass.py line 1438 raises a misleading ImportError telling the user to pip install refractiveindex for a purely internal pseudo-glass.

**Failure scenario.** Minimal install (no lumenairy[glass] extra): raytrace_system([{'type':'lens','f':0.1},...], wv) -> trace resolves '__thin_lens__' -> ImportError "requires the 'refractiveindex' package" even though the index is a fixed 1.5 that needs no lookup. Same for 'spherical_lens'/'aspheric_lens'.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace: trace.py:1313 writes ('__fixed__','__fixed__','__fixed__'); glass.py:1384-1386 short-circuits only on entry[0]=='__user__'; user_library.py:276 (register_fixed_glass) uses the correct '__user__' sentinel — trace.py is a drifted duplicate. Probe (verify_trace_f2.py): GLASS_REGISTRY['__thin_lens__']==('__fixed__','__fixed__','__fixed__'); with glass._REFRACTIVEINDEX_AVAILABLE=False, get_glass_index('__thin_lens__',550e-9) raised ImportError "requires the 'refractiveindex' package", and end-to-end raytrace_system([{'type':'lens','f':0.1,...}], 550e-9) raised the same ImportError; a user_library.register_fixed_glass glass returned 1.5 fine under the same flag. With the package available it returns 1.5 only via the accidental pre-seeded _glass_cache hit at glass.py:1447.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked the '__thin_lens__' string fallback at glass.py:1374 — it compares the registry VALUE to '__thin_lens__', and the value is the tuple, so it never fires; checked SELLMEIER/POLYNOMIAL fallbacks at glass.py:1399-1415 — pseudo-glass names are not in those tables; verified working-by-accident path with the package installed (returns 1.5 via cache hit at line 1447), confirming the bug is masked on full installs, which is why tests pass.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Have trace.py's _register_fixed_index write the ('__user__','__fixed__','__fixed__') sentinel or simply call user_library.register_fixed_glass instead of maintaining a drifted duplicate.

### [P2-37] Shared _analysis_worker rebound while a previous QThread is still running -> 'QThread: Destroyed while thread is still running' crash

- **Location:** `lumenairy/ui/coherence_dock.py:475`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _dispatch_worker (472-478) unconditionally does `self._analysis_worker = _CoherenceAnalysisWorker(...)`. Tabs 2-4 each disable only their OWN button (473), so starting Tab 3 while Tab 2's worker is running drops the only Python reference to a live QThread; when GC collects it Qt aborts with 'QThread: Destroyed while thread is still running'. Same pattern for the legacy Tab-1 worker at line 310 vs the shared worker (independent attrs, so Tab 1 + Tab 2 concurrently is possible but at least both refs are held). No isRunning() guard exists anywhere in this dock (grep confirms).

**Failure scenario.** User launches Koehler analysis on Tab 2 (long N), switches to Tab 3 and clicks its Run before Tab 2 finishes -> old QThread object garbage-collected mid-run -> hard abort of the whole application.

**Verification evidence.** coherence_dock.py:472-478 rebinds self._analysis_worker unconditionally and disables only the launching tab's button (line 473), so cross-tab concurrent launches are reachable (Tab 2 running + Tab 3 Run click). Empirical probe (PySide6 6.11.0, C:/tmp/qthread_probe/probe2.py) replicating the exact dispatch/rebind pattern: leak mode printed Qt message "QThread: Destroyed while thread '' is still running" and the process hard-aborted with exit code 0xC0000409 (fail-fast) the moment the orphaned worker's run() returned (t=2.0s); control mode holding both references exited cleanly rc=0 with both results delivered. One timing nuance vs. the claim: gc.collect() mid-run does NOT destroy the thread (the executing run() frame holds the last reference); destruction happens from inside the worker thread when run() returns — but the user-visible effect is identical: whole-application hard abort. Grep confirms zero isRunning() guards in coherence_dock.py while 9 sibling docks all have the guard (ao_dock.py:743, coronagraph_dock.py:691, phase_retrieval_dock.py:792, multiconfig_dock.py:267, optimizer_dock.py:797, through_focus_dock.py:291, waveoptics_dock.py:2469, tolerance_dock.py:346, wavefront_map_dock.py:537). No tests reference CoherenceDock; no other code touches _analysis_worker.
