**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Searched entire lumenairy/ui for isRunning guards and any external serialization of _analysis_worker — none in this file, guard present in 9 sibling docks proving it is the intended house pattern this dock is missing. (2) Checked whether other tabs' buttons or the QTabWidget are disabled during a run — only the launching button is (line 473); _on_analysis_finished (589-592) re-enables all three only at completion. (3) Tested whether PySide6/Shiboken keeps a running QThread alive after the last Python reference drops — partially: the run() frame keeps it alive DURING execution (gc.collect() alone is harmless), but on run() return the wrapper is destroyed from the worker thread and Qt fail-fast aborts, so the crash claim survives. (4) Looked for tests covering the dock — none.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Add the sibling-dock guard at the top of _dispatch_worker (`if self._analysis_worker is not None and self._analysis_worker.isRunning(): return` with a status message), and/or disable all three tab run-buttons while the shared worker is active (mirroring how _on_analysis_finished already re-enables all three).

### [P2-38] App close does not stop or join any running worker QThread; the v5.4.2 'C1' close-guard was applied only to coronagraph_dock

- **Location:** `lumenairy/ui/main_window.py:1057`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** MainWindow.closeEvent (1057-1062) only saves workspace preferences. Docks with long-running QThread workers — waveoptics (WaveOpticsWorker), ao_dock, caustic_dock, coherence_dock, tolerance_dock, optimizer_dock, through_focus_dock, phase_retrieval_dock — define no closeEvent and are not interrupted/waited on quit. Only coronagraph_dock.py 684-693 implements the requestInterruption()+wait(2000) close guard (added by the v5.4.2 audit as C1). Grep for closeEvent across lumenairy/ui returns only main_window and coronagraph_dock. Quitting during a run destroys the QMainWindow (and dock C++ objects) while worker threads are alive and about to emit into deleted widgets -> 'QThread: Destroyed while thread is still running' abort or RuntimeError on deleted C++ object.

**Failure scenario.** User starts a 16384x16384 wave-optics run, decides to quit -> app crashes/aborts on exit instead of closing cleanly; if the worker was writing the output file, the file is corrupted too.

**Verification evidence.** main_window.py closeEvent (1057-1062) only calls _save_workspace_preferences() then super().closeEvent(). Grep across lumenairy/ui: closeEvent exists only in main_window.py, coronagraph_dock.py:683-694 (the sole requestInterruption()+wait(2000) guard), layout_3d.py and log_viewer_dock.py (non-thread). 18+ QThread worker classes (WaveOpticsWorker at waveoptics_dock.py:384, created unparented at :2462 with no interruption polling; ao_dock, caustic_dock, coherence_dock x2, tolerance_dock, optimizer_dock x3, through_focus_dock, phase_retrieval_dock, ghost, multiconfig, psf_mtf, richards_wolf, wavefront_map, thin_grating, lg_aberration) have no close handling; package-wide grep shows zero aboutToQuit hooks and requestInterruption/wait only in coronagraph_dock. Probe C:/tmp/probe_qthread_close.py (PySide6 offscreen, dock-owned running QThread, empty closeEvent, close mid-run): stderr = "QThread: Destroyed while thread '' is still running", process exit code 127 (not 0) after the final script line -- abnormal teardown abort reproduced exactly.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a global aboutToQuit/cleanup handler (none in package); searched every ui file for closeEvent (only the 4 listed, only coronagraph guarded); checked whether workers are parented or joined elsewhere (stop buttons use terminate() only on explicit user Stop: waveoptics_dock.py:2470, through_focus_dock.py:292, optimizer_dock.py:803); checked whether dock-level closeEvents would fire at app quit anyway -- they would NOT for docked (non-floating) QDockWidgets, so even the coronagraph C1 guard does not run on app quit, making the gap strictly worse than claimed. Probe attempt to show a clean exit failed: unguarded pattern reliably yields the QThread-destroyed abort.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** In MainWindow.closeEvent, iterate all docks' live workers (or a central worker registry), requestInterruption()+wait(timeout) each before accepting the event (and make heavy workers poll isInterruptionRequested()).

### [P2-39] Every system_changed triggers synchronous full recompute in ALL analysis/layout views on the GUI thread, with no isVisible() guard

- **Location:** `lumenairy/ui/spot_field_dock.py:94`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** spot_field_dock connects system_changed directly to _replot (line 94), which runs a full multi-field spot trace (spot_diagram_vs_field over all field angles x rings x rays, lines 102-140) in the GUI thread. The same unguarded pattern: layout_3d.py:182 (system_changed -> rebuild = full VTK scene teardown self._plotter.clear() + dozens of add_mesh), layout_2d.py:173, rayfan_dock.py:71 (trace per change), analysis.py:211. grep for isVisible/visibleRegion across these files returns ZERO hits — hidden/tabified docks (most are tabified, main_window 551-573, so all but one per tab group are hidden) still recompute on every edit. A slider drag or spin-box edit emits system_changed repeatedly, so each tick pays 4-5 redundant traces + a VTK rebuild, freezing the GUI.

**Failure scenario.** System with 5 field angles, 6 rings x 12 rays; user drags a curvature slider -> each debounce tick runs the 2D rebuild + full 3D VTK rebuild + spot trace + rayfan trace + analysis update serially in the GUI thread even though only the 2D layout tab is visible -> multi-hundred-ms stalls per tick, unresponsive dragging.

**Verification evidence.** Direct connects confirmed: spot_field_dock.py:94 system_changed->_replot (full spot_diagram_vs_field over all fields + full matplotlib figure rebuild, lines 97-243, no guard); layout_3d.py:182->rebuild (plotter.clear() + full VTK scene re-add); layout_2d.py:173->rebuild (scene.clear() + full re-add); rayfan_dock.py:71->_on_system_changed->_replot (full ray-fan trace; its only guard skips field-curvature plot mode, not hidden docks). Finding UNDERcounts: same unguarded pattern also in distortion_dock.py:92, footprint_dock.py:85, glass_map_dock.py:95. Grep 'isVisible|visibleRegion|visibilityChanged|disconnect' across lumenairy/ui: ZERO visibility guards. Tabify confirmed main_window.py:551-574 (spot/summary/rayfan/glassmap/library one tab group; layout2d/3d another) so all-but-one per group are hidden yet still recompute; FigureCanvasAgg.draw() renders regardless of visibility. Drag rate confirmed by repo's own comment slider_dock.py:145-155: 40 ms inner debounce -> '~25 system_changed emits/sec'; the 200 ms auto-retrace timer only coalesces trace_ready, not these slots. Probe (C:/tmp/probe_spotfield_perf.py, singlet, 5 fields, 6 rings x 24 rays = dock defaults): spot_diagram_vs_field min 2.5 ms / mean 2.8 ms, but the matplotlib render half (fig.clear+5 subplots+scatter+tight_layout+draw) min 116.9 ms / mean 143.8 ms — ~130 ms per tick for this ONE dock, 3x the 40 ms debounce window, before adding rayfan/distortion/footprint/glassmap renders + 2D scene rebuild + VTK teardown. Multi-hundred-ms per emit on the GUI thread during a drag is real. Two minor overstatements: analysis.py:211 _update is cheap paraxial text (not a trace), and element_table.py:993-995 is a stale BADGE (recompute on trace_ready), not literally recompute-on-show — neither weakens the core claim.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Searched entire lumenairy/ui for isVisible/visibleRegion/visibilityChanged/disconnect/showEvent-based deferral — only element_table stale badge and main_window showEvent (dock sizing), no visibility gating of any plot slot. (2) Checked whether the heavy work is actually routed through the 200 ms auto-retrace timer — no: that timer only drives run_trace->trace_ready consumers (_draw_rays); the system_changed slots above run per emit. (3) Checked rayfan's _on_system_changed guard — it only skips the explicit-click field-curvature mode. (4) Checked whether hidden matplotlib canvases skip rendering — FigureCanvasQTAgg.draw() renders unconditionally (only paintEvent is visibility-gated). (5) Timed the actual compute to test whether the cost claim is exaggerated — trace itself is only ~3 ms, but the unconditional matplotlib re-render is 117-144 ms/dock, confirming the stall magnitude via a different mechanism than the finding emphasized (render-bound, not trace-bound).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Adopt a stale-flag + recompute-on-showEvent/visibilityChanged pattern in each heavy dock (compute immediately only when self.isVisible(), else set _stale=True and replot on show), optionally sharing one coalescing timer.

### [P2-40] Stop button uses QThread.terminate() on a thread inside FFT/HDF5 C code; through_focus_dock has the same

- **Location:** `lumenairy/ui/waveoptics_dock.py:2470`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _stop (2468-2471) calls self._worker.terminate() and then manually invokes _on_finished({'error': ...}), which sets self._worker = None (2646) — dropping the reference to a thread Qt is still killing (destroyed-while-running risk) on top of terminate()'s documented dangers: the worker may hold the pyFFTW planner lock, be mid-write to the HDF5/Zarr output (971-996 save path), or mid-CuPy call, leaving corrupted output files or deadlocked locks. through_focus_dock.py:292 has the identical terminate()+manual-finish pattern. optimizer_dock (797-803) documents terminate() as an emergency fallback only and uses cooperative interruption first — the mainline Stop of these two docks skipped that. There is also a double-completion race: if the worker emits its own finished right before Stop is clicked, _on_finished runs twice.

**Failure scenario.** User stops a long run that is writing the .h5 output -> truncated/corrupt HDF5 file and possibly a deadlocked FFTW planner lock so the NEXT run hangs; or Qt aborts because the terminated thread object is GC'd while still winding down.

**Verification evidence.** waveoptics_dock.py:2468-2471: `if self._worker and self._worker.isRunning(): self._worker.terminate(); self._on_finished({'error': 'Stopped by user'})`; _on_finished (2641-2646) sets self._worker=None immediately, dropping the Python reference to a QThread whose OS thread terminate() is still asynchronously killing. through_focus_dock.py:290-293 is byte-for-byte the same pattern (`terminate()` + manual `_on_finished(None)`). Contrast confirmed: optimizer_dock.py:793-806 uses cooperative _worker.cancel() with terminate() explicitly documented as a should-not-happen legacy fallback ('v5.4 audit P1-F: cooperative cancellation'), and coronagraph_dock.py:680/692 uses requestInterruption(). WaveOpticsWorker has no isInterruptionRequested() checks (grep: none in waveoptics_dock.py). Double-completion race is real by trace: the worker's queued custom-finished emission (e.g. line 1001) can deliver after the Stop path's manual _on_finished call, running _on_finished twice. Corruption/deadlock consequences (mid-HDF5 write at 971-996, pyFFTW plan lock held at fft_infra.py:1650) are Qt-documented terminate() hazards, not reproduced live.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for cooperative-stop machinery in WaveOpticsWorker (isInterruptionRequested/requestInterruption/cancel flags): none exists in this dock or through_focus_dock. Checked whether the pattern is documented as intentional: optimizer_dock's own v5.4 audit comment condemns terminate() as an emergency-only fallback, so the mainline use here is drift, not intent. Considered whether the race is impossible: it is not — the custom finished signal is emitted from the worker thread and queued, so Stop can interleave between emit and delivery while isRunning() is still true. Did not run a live terminate-mid-HDF5 probe (destructive/flaky by nature; the hazard is documented Qt behavior). Mitigating note: while finding 1 is unfixed the waveoptics Stop path is nearly unreachable (runs die in milliseconds), but through_focus_dock is fully live.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace terminate() in both docks with the optimizer_dock/coronagraph_dock cooperative pattern (requestInterruption/cancel flag polled at stage boundaries in run(), UI re-enabled only from the worker's own finished handler), and guard _on_finished against double invocation.

### [P2-41] load_material catalog branch re-points GLASS_REGISTRY without invalidating _glass_cache/_glass_value_cache, silently serving a stale refractive index

- **Location:** `lumenairy/user_library.py:167`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** register_fixed_glass (user_library.py:276-282) clears _glass_value_cache when it overwrites a name, but the sibling catalog branch of load_material (user_library.py:167) only reassigns GLASS_REGISTRY[mat_name] to the new (shelf, book, page) tuple. If the name previously resolved through _glass_cache (a user-fixed _FixedIndex object, or a previously-loaded RefractiveIndexMaterial for a different catalog page), get_glass_index's tuple branch at glass.py:1447 sees 'glass_name in _glass_cache' and keeps returning the OLD material's index forever; get_glass_index_complex (glass.py:1514) likewise reads the stale cache for kappa.

**Failure scenario.** register_fixed_glass('XTEST', 1.5) followed by save_material('XTEST', shelf='specs', book='SCHOTT-optical', page='N-BK7'); load_material('XTEST'): GLASS_REGISTRY now points at N-BK7 but get_glass_index('XTEST', 587.6e-9) returns 1.5 instead of 1.5168 -- every subsequent prescription using the material silently traces/propagates with the wrong index. Same staleness when re-loading a material whose shelf/book/page was edited.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/probe_stale/probe_stale_glass_cache.py, refractiveindex installed) reproduced the exact scenario: register_fixed_glass('XTEST', 1.5) -> save_material('XTEST', shelf='specs', book='SCHOTT-optical', page='N-BK7') -> load_material('XTEST'). Output: GLASS_REGISTRY['XTEST'] == ('specs','SCHOTT-optical','N-BK7'), _glass_cache['XTEST'] is still _FixedIndex, get_glass_index('XTEST', 587.6e-9) = 1.5 (STALE) and get_glass_index_complex = (1.5+0j), while a fresh control name resolving the identical catalog page returns 1.5167984379050088. Mechanism: user_library.py:167 only reassigns GLASS_REGISTRY[mat_name]; the tuple branch in glass.py:1447 ('if glass_name not in _glass_cache') then keeps serving the pre-existing _glass_cache object forever, and get_glass_index_complex (glass.py:1514) reads the same stale object for kappa. The sibling fixed branch is immune because register_fixed_glass (user_library.py:276-282) overwrites _glass_cache[name] and clears _glass_value_cache; the catalog branch does neither. The same guard at glass.py:1447 also makes re-loading an edited shelf/book/page stale within a session (old RefractiveIndexMaterial retained).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for cache invalidation elsewhere: none in load_material's catalog branch (only the v5.4.6 override UserWarning at user_library.py:161-166, which surfaces the collision but does not fix caches). Checked glass.py resolution order for a sentinel/registry-vs-cache consistency check: the user-fixed sentinel branch (glass.py:1384-1393) is bypassed once the registry entry becomes a real tuple, and the tuple branch trusts _glass_cache unconditionally. Grepped tests: only tests/unit/test_audit_misc.py touches load_material, and only for the v4.11.2 dispersion-drop RuntimeWarning — no test pins catalog-branch cache behavior. Ran the probe end-to-end; the stale value reproduced exactly as claimed.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** In load_material's catalog branch, mirror register_fixed_glass's hygiene: pop _glass_cache[mat_name] and clear _glass_value_cache (imported from .glass) before/after reassigning GLASS_REGISTRY[mat_name].

### [P2-42] Cache-enrollment meta-pin's case-sensitive endswith('_CACHE') filter lets glass.py's two unenrolled, unclearable module caches escape detection

- **Location:** `tests/unit/test_v4_16_1_dispatcher_pin_cache_registry_enrollment.py:253`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The enrollment walker's discovery (_discover_module_level_cache_assignments, lines 253 and 266) only matches assignment targets ending in uppercase '_CACHE'. lumenairy/glass.py declares `_glass_cache = {}` (glass.py:1210) and `_glass_value_cache = {}` (glass.py:1220) in lowercase, so the walker discovers zero caches in glass.py and the main pin (test_every_cache_owning_module_enrolls_with_registry) never evaluates them. glass.py contains no register_cache_clearer call and no clear function at all, so both caches bypass clear_asm_caches()/clear_all_registered_caches() — the exact 'fix N, miss N+1' sibling-gap the v4.16.0 registry and this 10th meta-pin were created to retire. `_glass_value_cache` grows one entry per unique (glass_name, femtometre-rounded wavelength) scalar lookup with no bound and no eviction; `_glass_cache` retains RefractiveIndexMaterial objects (YAML dispersion databases) for the process lifetime. Secondary weakness folded in: the pin's enrollment check is per-MODULE (any single register_cache_clearer call blesses every cache in the file), so even correctly-named new caches added to an already-enrolled module escape per-cache verification; and two exemption entries reference 'lumenairy/propagators/asymptotic.py' paths that no longer host those caches after the v5.x module split (dead entries, currently harmless).

**Failure scenario.** A Monte-Carlo tolerancing or dispersive sweep calls get_glass_index with many distinct scalar wavelengths (e.g. 1e6 random draws): _glass_value_cache accumulates 1e6 entries that survive la.clear_asm_caches() and clear_all_registered_caches(); a long-running pipeline that relies on the documented central cache-clear to bound memory retains this state forever. Meanwhile the CI meta-pin that exists precisely to flag unenrolled caches stays green.

**Verification evidence.** Probe ran clean: _discover_module_level_cache_assignments on lumenairy/glass.py -> [] and _module_has_register_cache_clearer_call -> False (glass.py:1210 `_glass_cache = {}`, :1220 `_glass_value_cache = {}` are lowercase; walker lines 253/266 require case-sensitive endswith('_CACHE'), documented at docstring line 48). Runtime: 1000 _cached_glass_value entries -> still 1000 after BOTH clear_all_registered_caches() and la.clear_asm_caches(); registry holds exactly 11 clearers, none for glass. _cached_glass_value (glass.py:1223-1236) has no eviction bound; _glass_cache is never cleared anywhere in the package (only _glass_value_cache.clear() exists, at user_library.py:282 inside register_fixed_glass). Secondary claims verified: enrollment check is per-module (any one register_cache_clearer call blesses all caches in the file), and both ('lumenairy/propagators/asymptotic.py', '_JAX_IFT_SOLVER_CACHE'/'_HG_MODE_STACK_CACHE') exemption entries are dead post-v5.x split (asymptotic.py discovery -> []; caches now in asymptotic_jax_twin.py as `= None` (filtered by RHS shape) and asymptotic_modes.py:215/224 (module enrolled)) — currently harmless, as claimed.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a glass clearer registered from any other module (grep _glass_cache.clear / clear_glass: only user_library.py:282 and manual test hygiene in tests/unit/test_v5_6_glass_memo.py — not registry enrollment); checked whether the case-sensitive filter constitutes a documented exemption of glass caches (docstring documents the mechanism only; glass caches predate the _FOO_CACHE convention, no exemption entry exists); checked _cached_glass_value for a size cap or LRU eviction (none); checked whether _glass_cache growth is bounded (yes — by distinct glass names, so the unbounded-growth claim rests on _glass_value_cache, which the probe confirmed grows one entry per unique femtometre-rounded scalar wavelength). Practical memory impact needs ~1e6 unique scalar wavelengths (~200 MB), so the leak scenario is Monte-Carlo-shaped rather than everyday, but the meta-pin blindness and the documented central-clear API missing live state are unconditional.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Make the walker's suffix match case-insensitive (accept '_CACHE' and '_cache'), then either enroll a glass clearer via register_cache_clearer or add documented exemptions for glass.py's two caches, and refresh the two stale propagators/asymptotic.py exemption paths.

## P3 findings (minor but worth fixing)

### [P3-01] make_shack_hartmann_wfs with rng_seed set re-seeds default_rng(seed) INSIDE the closure, so every AO frame gets the IDENTICAL noise realization (perfectly frame-correlated noise, not a reproducible sequence)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/analysis/ao.py:1158`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Line 1158: `rng = np.random.default_rng(_rng_seed)` executes on every call to the returned wfs closure. With rng_seed=int, the standard_normal draws are byte-identical on every call, so the 'noisy WFS' delivers a frozen noise map, not i.i.d. per-frame noise. In ao_closed_loop the leaky integrator then integrates this constant bias into the DM command (gain/(leak) amplification) instead of averaging zero-mean noise — a qualitatively different closed-loop noise response than any real WFS. The docstring's 'the closure is fully deterministic' does not disclose that determinism means same-noise-every-frame; the canonical reproducible design seeds the generator ONCE in the factory.

**Failure scenario.** wfs = make_shack_hartmann_wfs(noise_sigma_pixels=1.0, rng_seed=42); ao_closed_loop(..., wfs=wfs, n_iterations=50): the loop converges to a residual equal to the (fixed) noise-induced phase estimate rather than exhibiting the gain-dependent noise-rejection floor a user is simulating; noise-rejection studies of gain/leak values produce systematically wrong conclusions.

**Verification evidence.** ao.py line 1158: rng = np.random.default_rng(_rng_seed) executes on every closure call. Probe (N=64, subap=8, noise_sigma_pixels=1.0, rng_seed=42): (a) same residual twice -> np.array_equal True; (b) noise map isolated by subtracting the noiseless measurement is identical across DIFFERENT residuals, max|n1-n2| = 8.9e-16 (pure fp), noise amplitude ~3.8 rad — so in ao_closed_loop every frame injects the same frozen noise realization and the leaky integrator accumulates a constant bias instead of averaging zero-mean noise. seed=None correctly gives different draws per call. The noise_sigma_pixels docstring line 'Non-zero values inject fresh noise on every call (deterministic when rng_seed is set...)' is false when seeded.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for documentation making it intended: Notes section explicitly pins side-effect freedom ('the closure does not mutate any captured state; when rng_seed is set the same residual always produces the same measured phase') and tests/unit/test_v5_4_make_shack_hartmann_wfs.py:121-132 pins same-input determinism — so statelessness is deliberate, and the finding's proposed one-line hoist would break that pinned test. However nothing documents that seeded determinism means frozen per-frame noise, and the 'fresh noise on every call' docstring line directly contradicts the behavior, so the gap survives. No test covers seeded noise across differing inputs / closed-loop use.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Keep statelessness but decorrelate frames: mix a per-call counter (or a hash of the input) into the seed via np.random.SeedSequence([rng_seed, frame_counter]) — or, minimally, document that a fixed rng_seed freezes the noise realization and is unsuitable for closed-loop noise-rejection studies; note the pinned same-input-determinism test must be consciously updated if semantics change.

### [P3-02] strehl_marechal docstring doctest value is wrong: strehl_marechal(1/14) = 0.81757, not the shown '0.8189...'

- **Location:** `lumenairy/analysis/strehl.py:146`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The Examples block claims `float(la.strehl_marechal(1.0 / 14.0))` -> `0.8189...`. The implemented (correct) formula exp(-(2*pi*sigma)^2) gives exp(-(2*pi/14)^2) = 0.817568..., so the ellipsis doctest would fail if collected, and users sanity-checking the lambda/14 rule of thumb against the printed value see a mismatch in the third decimal.

**Failure scenario.** Running the module's doctests (pytest --doctest-modules, or a user pasting the example) fails: expected 0.8189..., got 0.817568573328328. Anyone calibrating an RMS budget against the documented 0.8189 anchor is off by 0.0014 absolute.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: float(strehl_marechal(1.0/14.0)) == 0.817568573328328; math.exp(-(2*pi/14)**2) == 0.817568573328328 (identical). doctest.testmod(lumenairy.analysis.strehl) fails: line 145 'Expected: 0.8189... Got: 0.817568573328328' (1 of 9 examples failed). Even with ELLIPSIS enabled '0.8189...' cannot match 0.81756... — mismatch is in the 3rd decimal. Implementation at lines 148-149 is correct; only the docstring constant at line 146 is wrong.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Tried to explain 0.8189 as an alternative Marechal convention: (1-(2πσ)²/2)² = 0.8081 and 1-(2πσ)² = 0.7986 — neither matches, so not an intentional variant. Checked whether doctests run in CI: grep for 'doctest' finds only docstring mentions in polychromatic.py/plotting.py, no --doctest-modules config, so the failure is latent (mitigates to doc-only P3 but does not refute the wrong constant).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Change the docstring expected value at line 146 from '0.8189...' to '0.8175...' (or 0.817568573328328) and optionally adjust the '~0.82' comment; no code change needed.

### [P3-03] through_focus_scan_jax materializes all n_z propagated planes at once (device array + full host copy simultaneously) where the NumPy twin streams one plane at a time

- **Location:** `lumenairy/analysis/through_focus.py:1165`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** kernel(E_fft_shifted, z_jax) returns fields of shape (n_z, Ny, Nx) complex (line 1161), and line 1165 immediately makes a full host copy `fields_np = np.asarray(fields)` before the per-plane metric loop, so peak memory is ~2 * 16 * n_z * Ny * Nx bytes held concurrently. The NumPy backend (through_focus_scan, lines 412-433) propagates and reduces one plane at a time (~1 plane resident). Neither the docstring nor the dispatcher warns that backend='jax' changes the memory contract from O(N^2) to O(n_z*N^2).

**Failure scenario.** A dense scan (n_z=201, N=2048 complex128) on backend='jax': 13.5 GB device + 13.5 GB host resident simultaneously -> OOM / device-memory failure, while the identical call with backend='numpy' runs in <100 MB. monte_carlo_tolerancing_jax invokes this per trial (line 1364), so the spike recurs every trial.

**Verification evidence.** Code: kernel = jax.jit(jax.vmap(_asm_one_z, in_axes=(None,0))) (through_focus.py:1076) -> fields (n_z,Ny,Nx) at :1161; fields_np = np.asarray(fields) full host copy at :1165 while `fields` stays referenced through the metric loop (:1172-1214). Probe (Windows peak working set, N=1024 c128): numpy backend 490.8 MiB @ n_z=48 and 490.1 MiB @ n_z=96 (flat, O(N^2)); jax backend 2609.1 MiB @ n_z=48 and 4916.2 MiB @ n_z=96 (linear in n_z, ~3.2x the 768/1536-MiB field stack -- worse than the claimed 2x due to fused-kernel transients). Extrapolated claimed scenario n_z=201/N=2048: ~40 GiB peak vs ~0.5 GiB numpy. Neither docstring (:304-361, :1089-1108) mentions the memory-contract change; monte_carlo_tolerancing_jax calls the scan per trial at :1364. Severity kept at P3: opt-in backend, transient spike, correct results, trivial workaround (backend='numpy'); borders P2 only for very dense large-grid scans.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched the JAX path for z-chunking, per-plane device_get, or any memory guard -- none. Checked both docstrings and the backend-dispatcher notes for a documented memory caveat -- only speedup is advertised. Considered zero-copy np.asarray on CPU JAX as a mitigating factor -- moot: measured peak already shows ~3.2x-stack concurrent residency scaling linearly with n_z while the NumPy backend is flat across n_z. Ran identical calls on both backends (matching peak_I sanity value) to confirm the difference is purely the memory contract, not workload.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Process z in fixed-size chunks (vmap over 8-16 planes at a time, copy each chunk to host and release the device buffer) and document the per-backend memory contract in both docstrings.

### [P3-04] _jax_available defined twice in the same module; second definition silently shadows the first

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_jax.py:301`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Lines 30-33 and 301-306 both define def _jax_available() with identical bodies (different docstrings). The second definition rebinds the name at import time; the first is dead code. Harmless today because the bodies match, but the duplicate is a drift hazard -- an edit to the first definition (e.g. adding a version floor) would be silently ignored for all call sites, since they resolve to the second.

**Failure scenario.** Maintainer patches the line-30 definition to gate on a minimum JAX version; every call site (lines 232, 405, 653) still uses the unpatched line-301 shadow, so the guard never fires and an unsupported JAX version proceeds into the trace.

**Verification evidence.** AST probe on lumenairy/elements/_lens_jax.py: FunctionDef '_jax_available' at lines 30 and 301. Runtime probe: inspect.getsourcelines(m._jax_available)[1] == 301, proving the second definition is the one bound at import and the line-30 definition is dead code. Bodies are identical (both lazily import backend.JAX_AVAILABLE), only docstrings differ, so behavior is unaffected today.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the two definitions differ in behavior (they do not -- identical bodies, so no live bug); checked whether any call site (lines 232, 405, 653) could resolve the first definition via a closure captured before line 301 (no -- all call sites resolve the module global at call time, which is the line-301 binding); considered whether the duplication is intentional (no comment or __all__ export suggests it; it reads as a merge/extraction artifact from the v3.5.5 split).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Delete one of the two identical _jax_available definitions (keep the line-30 one near the top, or the line-301 one with the fuller docstring).

### [P3-05] JAX lens twins did not receive (and do not document the absence of) the v5.17.0 row-band memory mode -- fully monolithic N x N allocation

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_jax.py:545`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** v5.17.0 made the byte-identical row-band (sag_chunk_rows) path the DEFAULT for the NumPy apply_real_lens / apply_real_lens_traced at N >= 4096 (CHANGELOG lines 34-75). apply_real_lens_traced_jax and apply_real_lens_maslov_jax -- documented throughout as the 'JAX-traced twin' of those functions -- build full monolithic N x N wave grids (jnp.meshgrid at lines 545 and 779), full N x N phase screens, plus the amplitude='analytic' callback materializing extra full-grid arrays outside the trace. Neither function accepts sag_chunk_rows nor carries a docstring note that the numpy-side memory mode does not apply, and estimate_lens_memory knows nothing about the JAX path.

**Failure scenario.** A user who just fixed an OOM at N=16384 by upgrading to 5.17.0 moves the same prescription into a jax.grad design loop via the 'twin' and OOMs again (JAX additionally holds forward residuals for reverse-mode): ~2-8 GB per retained full-grid float64/complex128 buffer at 16384^2, times the autodiff tape.

**Verification evidence.** Grep 'chunk' in _lens_jax.py: 0 hits. sag_chunk_rows exists only in _lens_real.py, _lens_traced.py, memory.py; memory.py (estimate_lens_memory) has 0 case-insensitive 'jax' mentions. CHANGELOG.md lines 34-75 confirm sag_chunk_rows=None resolves to AUTO row-band default and estimate_sim_memory/check_sim_memory take sag_chunk_rows. _lens_jax.py builds unconditional (N,N) jnp.meshgrid at lines 545 and 779, and _cheb_eval_value_grad_jax stacks (order+1, N, N) Chebyshev arrays (11 x N^2 float64 at cheb_order=10) inside the Newton fori_loop -- worse than the plain N^2 buffers. The 'not replicated' docstring list (lines 399-403) names multiprocess Newton, CuPy, tilt-aware rays but not the memory mode.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched _lens_jax.py for any chunking/streaming ('chunk': 0 hits); checked whether estimate_lens_memory covers the JAX path (no 'jax' anywhere in memory.py); checked whether the docstring's not-replicated list mentions memory (it does not). Partial mitigation found: the amplitude='analytic' leg calls NumPy apply_real_lens via pure_callback, which DOES inherit the v5.17.0 row-band default -- but the pure-JAX phase-screen legs (default amplitude='input') remain fully monolithic, so the finding stands as a gap.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** At minimum add the row-band absence to the two docstrings' 'not replicated' lists and the Quick decision guide, and teach estimate_lens_memory about the JAX path (including the (order+1, N, N) Chebyshev stacks); optionally implement a lax.map row-band variant of the Newton/eval stage.

### [P3-06] New public kwargs sag_dtype and sag_chunk_rows are undocumented in both public docstrings

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_real.py:439`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** apply_real_lens's docstring (lines 441-680) and apply_real_lens_traced's docstring (lines 1154-1378) document neither sag_dtype nor sag_chunk_rows -- verified by grep over both docstring ranges (zero hits). These are the headline v5.17.0 knobs, one of which (sag_chunk_rows) changed default behaviour for all N >= 4096 grids and the other of which (sag_dtype) is explicitly accuracy-risky and gated by a validator the docstring never points to. help()/IDE users have no way to discover the auto-banding default, the 0-forces-whole-grid escape hatch, or the lens_sag_float32_opd_error sign-off requirement; the only documentation lives in module-level comments and the CHANGELOG.

**Failure scenario.** A user reads help(apply_real_lens), sees no sag_dtype entry, finds the kwarg via autocomplete, and passes np.float32 on a deep-sag singlet without running lens_sag_float32_opd_error -- taking the documented-elsewhere ~2e-3 field error blind; or a user cannot discover why their N=4096 run behaves differently from N=2048 (auto band threshold) because the docstring never mentions chunking.

**Verification evidence.** Runtime check: inspect.getdoc(apply_real_lens) (11367 chars, has a Parameters section) and inspect.getdoc(apply_real_lens_traced) (9628 chars, has a Parameters section) both contain zero occurrences of 'sag_dtype' or 'sag_chunk'. Grep over _lens_real.py: the only hits near the function are the signature lines 438-439, next hit is a body comment at 803; _lens_traced.py: signature 1151-1152, next hit body comment at 1531. The kwargs are documented only in module-level comments (_lens_real.py:80-91 and 126-134), helper docstrings (set_lens_sag_dtype, lens_sag_float32_opd_error), and the CHANGELOG — invisible to help()/IDE users of the two propagators.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the kwargs are documented elsewhere in the same docstrings under different names or in a Notes section (inspect.getdoc substring search over the FULL docstring, not just a line range — zero hits); checked whether the kwargs might be considered private (they are keyword-only params of the two flagship public propagators, and one changes default behaviour at N>=4096, so they are public API); confirmed both docstrings DO have Parameters sections documenting other kwargs, so the omission is a genuine gap rather than a doc-style choice.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Add Parameters entries for sag_dtype (float32 accuracy-risky, validate with lens_sag_float32_opd_error) and sag_chunk_rows (None=auto band at N>=4096, 0=force whole-grid, int>0=band size) to both docstrings, mirroring the module comments at _lens_real.py:80-91 and 126-134.

### [P3-07] apply_spherical_lens docstring thickness formula contradicts its own signed-sag convention (and the code)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_thin.py:276`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Notes lines 276-277 state 't(h) = d - sag1(h) - sag2(h)' while defining the SIGNED sag 'sag(h) = R - sign(R)*sqrt(R^2 - h^2)' on the next line. With signed sags the correct thickness is t(h) = d - sag1(h) + sag2(h) (glass spans z in [sag1, d + sag2]); the '- sag2' form is only right for unsigned per-surface sag magnitudes on a biconvex. The delta_phi formula two lines later, -k(n-1)(sag1 - sag2), and the code at line 324 are both consistent with the signed convention -- i.e. the docstring's t(h) line is internally inconsistent with its own delta_phi and with the implementation.

**Failure scenario.** A user deriving the meniscus (R1>0, R2>0) OPD from the documented t(h) gets the wrong sign for the second surface's contribution and concludes the code has a sign bug (or 'corrects' a downstream model to match the wrong doc formula).

**Verification evidence.** Docstring line 276 states t(h) = d - sag1(h) - sag2(h) while line 277 defines the SIGNED sag R - sign(R)*sqrt(R^2 - h^2). Geometry with that signed sag gives t = d - sag1 + sag2 (front surface z1 = sag1, back surface z2 = d + sag2). The code at line 324 (phase = -k*(n_lens-1)*(sag1 - sag2)) and the docstring's own delta_phi (line 281) / lensmaker form 1/f=(n-1)(1/R1-1/R2) (line 284) match the corrected t, not line 276. Probe (meniscus R1=0.10, R2=0.20, n=1.5, wl=633nm, 64x64, dx=20um): max wrapped phase error vs lensmaker(1/R1-1/R2) form = 3.64e-4 rad (higher-order exact-sag residual only); vs the doc-t-implied (1/R1+1/R2) form = 3.14 rad (f_correct=0.400 m vs f_doc=0.1333 m). So line 276 is inconsistent with both the code and the rest of its own docstring; documentation-only defect.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Tried reading line 276 as using unsigned per-surface sag magnitudes (which would make d - sag1 - sag2 correct for a biconvex) — refuted by line 277 explicitly defining the signed formula applied to both surfaces, and _surface_sag in the code being signed. (2) Checked whether the whole docstring adopts a consistent alternative convention (which would make it self-consistent) — no: the delta_phi and lensmaker lines (281/284) agree with the code and contradict line 276. (3) Checked R1/R2 sign-convention text (lines 244-251) for a convention that rescues line 276 — none. (4) Ran the numeric probe above to confirm the code side follows -k(n-1)(sag1-sag2), i.e., the implementation is correct and only the doc t(h) line is wrong.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** One-character docstring fix at line 276: change 't(h) = d - sag1(h) - sag2(h)' to 't(h) = d - sag1(h) + sag2(h)'.

### [P3-08] ray_subsample undersample floor is silently skipped when the prescription has no aperture_diameter, though docs claim it is 'already enforced'

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_traced.py:1780`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The min_coarse_samples_per_aperture guard runs only 'if min_coarse_samples_per_aperture and aperture is not None'. For a prescription without aperture_diameter (launch_radius falls back to the grid half-extent at line 1769), the guard is skipped entirely, so the default ray_subsample=8 can produce an aliased coarse spline (~350 nm+ RMS OPD error per the in-code benchmark note at lines 1776-1779) with no error or warning. REAL_LENS_CHANGES.md ('Default ray_subsample = 8' section) states unconditionally that 'the internal safety floor ... is already enforced via on_undersample=error -- dropping below the floor raises a clear error', which the apertureless path does not honour.

**Failure scenario.** Hand-built prescription with per-surface clear_aperture keys but no top-level aperture_diameter, run at small N (e.g. N=512, dx sized so the beam fills the grid) with the default ray_subsample=8: fewer than 32 coarse samples span the effective pupil, the spline aliases, and the exit OPD is wrong by ~lambda/4+ with zero diagnostic -- exactly the failure mode the floor was added to prevent.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_lt/verify_lens_traced_f3.py), N=256, dx=1.5e-5, ray_subsample=16, on_undersample='error': WITH aperture_diameter=N*dx it raises 'ray_subsample=16 gives only 16.0 coarse samples across the 3.84-mm aperture (threshold 32)'; the IDENTICAL geometry WITHOUT aperture_diameter completes with no undersample diagnostic (only an unrelated Newton non-convergence RuntimeWarning, which is a different failure mode and not guaranteed to fire when the spline merely aliases). Code: guard at _lens_traced.py:1780 is 'if min_coarse_samples_per_aperture and aperture is not None'; aperture comes solely from lens_prescription.get('aperture_diameter') (line 1482); the apertureless fallback launch_radius = 0.5*N*dx (line 1769) feeds no floor check, and per-surface clear_aperture is never consulted. REAL_LENS_CHANGES.md:481-484 states unconditionally that the floor 'is already enforced via on_undersample=error'.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for any alternative floor using launch_radius, clear_aperture, or a validation step upstream (apply_real_lens at _lens_real.py:791 also just .get()s aperture_diameter — apertureless prescriptions are fully supported, so this is a reachable configuration, not a rejected input). Checked whether the Newton-convergence RuntimeWarning constitutes an equivalent guard: it fired in this probe but detects a different symptom (iteration non-convergence, tol=0.01*dx) and smooth spline aliasing can pass it. Checked docs for a stated aperture_diameter precondition on the floor: none.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Fall back to max per-surface clear_aperture (or the launch diameter) for the coarse-samples floor when aperture_diameter is absent, or emit a warning that the undersample floor cannot be evaluated.

### [P3-09] Chunked assembly retains the full-grid amp array through Newton + band assembly although it is unused after the coarse subsample (preserve_input_phase=True default)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/_lens_traced.py:2433`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** On the sub>1 chunked path, amp (full-grid float, np.abs(E_analytic)) is last consumed at line 2433 (amp_coarse = amp[::sub, ::sub], a view) and in _build_newton_mask; with preserve_input_phase=True (the default) the band assembly loop uses only E_analytic (line 2553), never amp. Because amp_coarse is a view, the full N x N float base stays resident through the ray trace, Newton inversion, and the entire band loop -- ~8.6 GB at N=32768/complex128 (float64) or ~4.3 GB at complex64 -- inside the very path whose purpose is minimum peak RSS. Copying the tiny coarse slice (amp[::sub,::sub].copy()) and del amp when preserve_input_phase is True would drop one full-grid array from the chunked peak with zero fidelity cost, in the same spirit as the v5.16.2 eager frees.

**Failure scenario.** N=32768 complex128 traced run with default chunking on a 128 GB box: the chunked peak carries an extra ~8.6 GB dead float64 array through the ~50% of the call after the amp stage, shrinking the headroom the row-band mode was shipped to reclaim.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace (airtight by Python local-variable semantics): amp = np.abs(E_analytic) (_lens_traced.py:1657/1681/1692) is a full N x N float64 array (8.6 GB at N=32768/complex128); on the sub>1 chunked path its last reads are amp[::sub,::sub] at 2433 (a view) and _build_newton_mask; the band-assembly loop (2531-2561) with preserve_input_phase=True reads only E_analytic (2553) and phase_analytic_lens (2546) — amp[r0:r1] at 2555 is the preserve=False branch. Grep confirms no 'del amp' exists anywhere in the function, so the local name keeps the full array resident through ray trace, Newton inversion, and the entire band loop until the chunked return at 2565.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Grepped every \bamp\b use in the function (2433, 2479, 2555, 2587 — the latter two are preserve=False or sub==1 branches) to find a later consumer on the chunked preserve=True path: none. Looked for an eager free matching the v5.16.2 lifetime fixes (opl_map/delta_phase/phase_exp are del'd at 2567-2588; amp is not). Impact caveat found while trying to refute: the process high-water mark likely occurs earlier during the amp legs (apply_real_lens internals, or the parallel_amp doubled working set ~50 GB at 32k) where amp must exist anyway, so del amp reduces residency for the back ~half of the call rather than necessarily lowering peak RSS — consistent with P3, not higher.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** When preserve_input_phase is True on the sub>1 path, copy the tiny coarse slice (amp[::sub,::sub].copy()) and del amp right after _build_newton_mask, mirroring the v5.16.2 eager-free pattern.

### [P3-10] No input validation anywhere in BORStack: negative/zero Rbig, N, thickness, or non-numeric wavelength are accepted and propagate to garbage or obscure downstream errors

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/bor/bor_stack.py:26`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** __init__ and add_layer perform no range checks: Rbig <= 0 gives a negative/degenerate grid (h<0, r<0 -> 1/r sign flips, m/r wrong sign) deep in _fd_grid_staggered; N=0/1 gives empty or 1x1 operators with cryptic LinAlg errors; a NEGATIVE thickness in add_layer flows into propagation_smatrix exp(1j*q*L) where forward-oriented evanescent modes (Im q > 0) now GROW as exp(+|Im q| |L|), silently destabilizing the Redheffer cascade instead of raising; wavelength<=0 in set_source gives k0=inf/negative. The sibling stacks (PMMStack/RCWAStack) grew such guards during the v5.14.x audits; BORStack, three days graduated, has none.

**Failure scenario.** A sign typo add_layer(-0.5, rings=(...)) — e.g. from a parameter sweep generator — produces exponentially amplified evanescent channels; redheffer_star's inv(I - B11@A22) then either blows up to huge |S| entries or returns a finite but physically meaningless S-matrix, and R/T can exceed 1 or be silently wrong with no exception.

**Verification evidence.** Code: bor_stack.py __init__ (26-33), add_layer (61: float(thickness) unchecked), set_source (65) have zero range checks; zcascade.py propagation_smatrix (181-187) documents the Im(q)>=0 -> exp(iqL) decays assumption which requires L>=0, unenforced. Probe (Rbig=4, m=1, N=80, n_sup=n_sub=1.4142, layer eps=6.0, wl=2pi/2): thickness=-0.5 -> solve returns finite S with max|S11|=407.583; thickness=-2.0 -> energy max=3.46564 (>1, silent); thickness=-4.0 -> energy max=8.13763e7, all finite, NO exception (the claimed silent Redheffer destabilization). Rbig=0 / N=0 / wavelength=0 -> cryptic ZeroDivisionError deep in grid assembly; Rbig=-4 -> solve RETURNS energy=1.0 with 6 'propagating' orders on a negative-radius grid (plausible-looking garbage); N=1 -> returns R=[0.122,0.117] T=[0.878,0.883] from a 1-point grid; wavelength=-1 -> k0=-6.283, solve silently returns EMPTY R/T. Sibling PMMStack (elements/pmm/stack.py) has 30+ raise ValueError validation sites, confirming the claimed asymmetry.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched bor_stack.py, zcascade.py, and coupled_radial_eigensolver for guards protecting these inputs — none exist. Checked whether redheffer_star coincidentally raises on negative-L growth — it returns finite, physically meaningless S instead. Checked test coverage: only tests/unit/test_gate4.py:108 constructs BORStack, valid inputs only, no pytest.raises anywhere for BOR. Checked whether negative thickness could be an intended feature (e.g. back-propagation) — no doc or caller supports that; the propagation_smatrix docstring's decay guarantee explicitly assumes L>=0.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Add cheap guards: raise ValueError in __init__ for Rbig<=0 or N<2, in add_layer for thickness<0 (or non-finite), and in set_source for wavelength<=0 / k0<=0, mirroring PMMStack's validation style.

### [P3-11] solve() docstring says angles are 'far-field polar angle in the substrate' but the code computes them in the superstrate (eps_sup)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/bor/bor_stack.py:78`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The docstring (lines 77-78) documents res['angles'] as 'far-field polar angle in the substrate, rad', but lines 113-115 compute gamma = sqrt(eps_sup.real*k0^2 - qi^2) and angles = arcsin(gamma/(sqrt(eps_sup.real)*k0)) from the SUPERSTRATE permittivity and the incident (superstrate) q's. These are the incident-mode angles in the superstrate; for n_sub != n_sup the substrate angles differ by Snell refraction.

**Failure scenario.** BORStack(n_superstrate=1.0, n_substrate=1.5): a user taking res['angles'] as the transmitted-order polar angles in the substrate (as documented) mispoints a detector / mislabels diffraction orders by the full Snell factor (e.g. 30 deg reported where the substrate angle is 19.5 deg).

**Verification evidence.** bor_stack.py:77-78 documents ``angles`` as 'far-field polar angle in the substrate, rad'; lines 112-115 compute qi = q[inc].real where q = sup['q'] (line 103, the SUPERSTRATE modal basis), gamma = sqrt(eps_sup.real*k0^2 - qi^2), and angles = arcsin(gamma/(sqrt(eps_sup.real)*k0)) — superstrate permittivity in both the gamma and the normalization, applied to incident superstrate modes. For n_sub != n_sup these differ from substrate angles by the full Snell factor (30 deg reported vs arcsin(sin30/1.5)=19.5 deg for n_sub=1.5). The v5.16.0 CHANGELOG's GATE-4 description ('each cylindrical mode's local oblique angle theta = arcsin(gamma/(n k0))' matched against planar incidence) confirms the computed quantity is the INCIDENT-side angle, i.e. the code is intended and the docstring word 'substrate' is the error.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a test pinning the angles semantics — none exists; the only consumer, tests/unit/test_gate4.py, sets n_sub = n_sup (line 102) so the two definitions coincide and the mismatch is unexercised. Checked farfield.py far_field_angles for a substrate-side convention that might justify the docstring — it is medium-agnostic (takes eps as an argument). Checked whether qi could be substrate q's — line 103 unambiguously takes q = sup['q']. No numerical probe needed; the code trace is unambiguous.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Fix the docstring to say 'incident-mode polar angle in the superstrate' (or additionally return substrate-side angles computed from sub['q'] and eps_sub for the transmitted orders).

### [P3-12] BORStack.solve rebuilds every layer's dense 2Nx2N eigendecomposition on every call with no dedup of identical layers and no eig cache (PMMStack has both)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/bor/bor_stack.py:93`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** solve() executes mids = [(thk, self._build(fn)) for thk, fn in self._layers], a full _assemble_staggered + _fast_geig (dense 2N x 2N ~ 600x600 eig at the default N=300) per layer per call. A periodic stack ABABAB... pays one eig per REPETITION instead of one per distinct profile, and repeated solve() calls (e.g. a user probing the same k0, or a sweep harness re-calling solve after set_source) redo everything. The sibling PMMStack keeps _eig_cache/_mats_cache keyed by layer+wavelength (pmm/stack.py:1594-1632); BORStack, documented as its mirror, has no caching at all. Only the sup==sub identity is reused.

**Failure scenario.** A 20-period two-material ring-grating stack at N=300: 40 dense 600x600 geig solves (plus 40 Lei inversions) per solve() where 2 suffice — roughly 20x the eig cost; a 100-point k0 sweep multiplies that by 100 with zero reuse of the layer-profile assembly.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace airtight: bor_stack.py:93 'mids = [(thk, self._build(fn)) for thk, fn in self._layers]' calls layer_modes (_assemble_staggered + _fast_geig on a 2Nx2N dense pencil) once per layer per solve() call; the class defines no cache attribute anywhere (only Rbig, m, N, eps_sub, eps_sup, _layers, k0) and the sole reuse is sup==sub at line 90. Controlled timing (N=80, warmup first, best-of-3): 1 identical rings layer = 0.650 s, 2 = 1.298 s, 4 = 1.456 s, 8 = 2.488 s — cost grows with layer count despite byte-identical profiles, and repeated solve() calls recompute in full. IMPORTANT CORRECTION to the claim's framing: PMMStack does NOT 'have both'. Its plain solve() (pmm/stack.py:678) also runs one _sem_modes_tensor eig per layer with no identical-layer dedup, and the _eig_cache (pmm/stack.py:1594-1633) exists only on the prepare() path with cache key ck=(i, wavelength, angle[, content]) that INCLUDES the layer index i — so even PMM never dedups a repeated ABAB profile; its cache only amortizes repeated solve(materials=...) calls. The genuine parity gap is BORStack's lack of any prepare()/sweep-style reuse path, not a missing dedup that PMM has.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Read the whole BORStack class hunting for any memoization (none). Grepped pmm/stack.py for the claimed dedup ('seen', 'dedup', profile keying) — the only 'seen' dict is in a plotting helper; verified _eig_cache key construction at pmm/stack.py:1612-1623 includes the layer index i, refuting the 'one eig per distinct profile' comparison. First timing attempt was contaminated by machine contention (nrep=1 read 302 s); reran with warmup and best-of-3 to get clean numbers. Considered whether 'prototype' status excuses it — BORStack is released public API (v5.16.0 CHANGELOG, 'Headline API BORStack ... mirrors PMMStack'), so the parity expectation is fair, but the impact is perf-only with correct results.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Give BORStack a per-(profile-key, k0) modal cache — key rings/eps layers by their parameter tuples rather than closure identity — so identical layers share one eigensolve and repeated solve()/sweep calls reuse assemblies, mirroring PMMStack.prepare().

### [P3-13] _fast_geig's promised 'robust QZ fallback at a longitudinal resonance' is unreachable for near-singular B, and Lei = inv() at line 117 is corrupted before either path

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/bor/coupled_radial_eigensolver.py:149`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The docstring claims _fast_geig 'falls back to the robust generalized QZ if B is singular (a longitudinal resonance) -- so the speed is free and the physics is never compromised'. But np.linalg.solve raises LinAlgError only on an EXACT zero pivot; at an actual longitudinal resonance (k0^2*eps equal to an eigenvalue of -Lm) B is near-singular (measured cond(B) ~ 4e16), no exception is raised, and the folded solve proceeds with a corrupted matrix. Moreover Lei = np.linalg.inv(Lm + k0^2*diag(eps_node)) at line 117 (cond ~ 1e16 at resonance) poisons K and B before _fast_geig runs, so even the QZ path would not restore accuracy. No condition check or warning exists anywhere on this path.

**Failure scenario.** Uniform layer eps=2, Rbig=8, N=80, m=1 at the resonant k0=1.9932284651: cond(Lei)=1.0e16, cond(B)=4.0e16, no exception, BORStack energy deviation jumps to 2.8e-4 (vs 6e-15 off-resonance) with no diagnostic; a k0 sweep stepping near such a resonance gets a silently degraded point mid-sweep (worse for structured/lossy layers where energy!=1 cannot flag it).

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_bor_fastgeig_resonance.py, run from repo root): discrete longitudinal resonance found at k0=1.9932284651262135 (matches claimed 1.9932284651) for uniform eps=2, Rbig=8, N=80, m=1. At that k0: cond(Lei)=2.99e15, cond(B)=3.34e15, and np.linalg.solve(Be,Ke) did NOT raise LinAlgError (LAPACK gesv raises only on an exact zero pivot), so the eig(Ke,Be) QZ branch at coupled_radial_eigensolver.py:149 is dead code for near-singular B. Contrast: the sibling pmm _fast_geig (lumenairy/elements/pmm/_core.py:456-459) has an explicit LU pivot-ratio guard (du.min() <= 1e-12*du.max() -> _safe_geig) that the bor copy omits. Lei = np.linalg.inv(Lm + k0**2*diag(eps_node)) at line 117 feeds Phi_r/Phi_p into BOTH K and B (lines 118-124), so the pencil is corrupted before _fast_geig runs and a QZ fallback alone could not restore accuracy — the docstring's 'the physics is never compromised' is wrong at resonance. Behavioral: BORStack (1.0-thick eps=2 layer) max|energy-1| = 4.12e-5 at exact resonance (1.51e-5 in a multithreaded run; magnitude is BLAS/ULP-sensitive at cond~3e15) vs 1.1e-14 at 1e-8 relative detuning — a 9-order-of-magnitude silent jump with no diagnostic. Detuning sweep: err = 4.1e-5 @0, 7.1e-11 @1e-12, 6.3e-13 @1e-11, 1.2e-14 @1e-10. Claimed 2.8e-4 not reproduced exactly (I measured 1.5e-5–4e-5; layer-thickness/BLAS dependent) but the failure class and mechanism are identical.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Searched the whole bor module for a guard: grep cond|rcond|warn|resonan finds only docstrings — no condition check exists on Lei, B, or the solve path. (2) Checked both callers (_radial_coupled_modes_staggered and zcascade._layer_modes_staggered) and both cascade drivers (BORStack.solve, bor_solve.solve): none checks conditioning or energy, so nothing prevents the state. (3) Checked test coverage: no bor test (test_gate4/test_zcascade/test_staggered/test_bor_solve/test_radial_eigensolver/test_coupled_eigensolver/test_farfield) mentions resonance, singular, _fast_geig, or LinAlgError. (4) Tried to refute the 'unreachable fallback' claim directly by running the equilibrated solve at cond(B)=3.3e15: no exception raised. (5) Tried to refute practical impact: detuning sweep shows the degraded window is only ~1e-11 relative in k0 and resonance positions are discretization (N,Rbig)-dependent, so a real sweep almost never lands in it, and the worst error is ~4e-5 — this bounds severity at P3 but does not refute the finding (the docstring promise and the missing guard are factual).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Port the pmm _fast_geig LU pivot-ratio guard (du.min() <= 1e-12*du.max() -> QZ) into the bor version, add the same cheap pivot/rcond check on Lm + k0**2*diag(eps_node) before the inv() at line 117 (warn on near-singularity), and soften the docstring's 'physics is never compromised' claim.

### [P3-14] Staggered layer_modes returns r=r_node and wq=r_node*h although the first N rows of W/V (E_r, h_phi) live on the face grid — the face grid is never returned

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/bor/zcascade.py:89`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _layer_modes_staggered returns dict(W=W, V=V, q=qf, r=r_n, wq=(r_n*h)); but by construction W[:N] is E_r ON FACES (r_f=(i+1)h) and V[N:] is h_phi on faces, while r/wq describe the node grid (i+1/2)h. The correct flux quadrature (used internally at lines 63-65) weights the face half by r_f*h and the node half by r_n*h. Any consumer that combines L['W'][:N] with L['r'] or L['wq'] — e.g. feeding E_r into farfield.fourier_bessel/order_power_fractions (which derives the domain edge as r[-1]+h/2, giving R=Rbig+h/2 for the face grid), or computing flux the way bor_solve._flux does — gets a half-cell-shifted quadrature and a wrong domain edge with no way to know, because r_f is not exposed.

**Failure scenario.** User computes per-order far-field fractions of the transmitted E_r: fourier_bessel(L['W'][:N, j], L['r'], h, m, nmax) evaluates J_m at node radii while the samples sit at face radii — O(h) systematic error in every c_n plus an R inflated by h/2; at N=150 that is a ~0.3% coefficient bias masquerading as physics, and it does not vanish under the Parseval self-check because frac renormalizes.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace airtight: _fd_grid_staggered (coupled_radial_eigensolver.py:53-88) puts E_r/h_phi on faces r_f=(i+1)h and E_phi/h_r on nodes r_n=(i+1/2)h; the internal flux (zcascade.py:63-65) correctly weights the face half by r_f*h and the node half by r_n*h; the return (zcascade.py:89) exposes only r=r_n, wq=r_n*h — r_f is absent. Probe (C:/tmp/verify_zcascade_f2.py): computing flux the bor_solve._flux way with the returned wq on unit-flux staggered modes gives 0.9843-0.9922 instead of 1.0 (rel err 7.8e-3..1.6e-2 at N=80, O(h) systematic). Feeding face-sampled J_1(kt_3 r) into fourier_bessel labeled with the returned r (farfield.py:36 then assumes edge R=r[-1]+h/2=8.0 instead of the face-domain 8.05) leaks 3.1e-3 of power out of the true order vs 3.1e-13 for correct node labeling — and order_power_fractions' frac renormalization hides it, as claimed.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched the whole repo for consumers combining staggered output with r/wq/farfield: none exist today — bor_solve.build_layer and all flux users (bor_solve.py:28, test_zcascade.py:46) use the NON-staggered layer_modes where the single cell-centered grid is self-consistent, and BORStack (the only staggered caller) never touches r/wq or farfield. Mitigations found: the row placement IS documented in _layer_modes_staggered's own docstring, and r_f is trivially derivable as r+h/2 from the uniform grid — so 'no way to know' is overstated. But the public layer_modes docstring ('the shared grid r and r*dr weights wq') is actively wrong for staggered=True, so the trap is real for any external consumer; hence CONFIRMED as a latent consistency gap, kept at P3 (no in-repo failure path today).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Return r_f (and per-half quadrature weights, e.g. wq_face=r_f*h alongside wq_node=r_n*h) from _layer_modes_staggered and document in the layer_modes docstring which W/V rows live on which grid when staggered=True.

### [P3-15] GaussianBSDF normalization makes TIS equal scattered_fraction only at normal incidence; at oblique incidence evaluate() integrates to scattered_fraction*cos(theta_i) while total_integrated_scatter() still returns scattered_fraction

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/bsdf.py:286`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _normalization() returns A = scattered_fraction/(2*pi*sigma^2), derived assuming the specular lobe sits at the surface normal so the cos(theta_s) weight in TIS = integral BSDF*cos(theta_s) dOmega is ~1 over the lobe. For an oblique specular direction the same A yields an integrated scatter of ~scattered_fraction*cos(theta_spec), so evaluate() (used in irradiance integrals) and total_integrated_scatter()/the class docstring ('normalization A is computed so that the TIS equals the caller-specified scattered_fraction') are mutually inconsistent by a factor cos(theta_i). Neither the docstring nor the parameter doc restricts the claim to normal incidence.

**Failure scenario.** Stray-light irradiance computed by integrating GaussianBSDF.evaluate() over the hemisphere for a 45-deg-incidence beam under-reports scattered power by ~29% (factor cos 45) relative to the scattered_fraction the user specified and relative to what sample()-based Monte Carlo (which draws n_samples with implicit weight scattered_fraction) assumes.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_bsdf_tis_oblique.py, 2048x1024 hemisphere quadrature of g.evaluate*cos(theta_s), sigma=0.02, f=0.01): normal incidence -> 0.00999344 (ratio 0.9993); 45 deg -> 0.0070673 (ratio 0.7067 = cos45); 60 deg -> 0.00499733 (ratio 0.4997 = cos60); g.total_integrated_scatter() == 0.01 always. Code trace: _normalization() (bsdf.py:277-286) returns f/(2*pi*sigma^2) with no incidence dependence (its comment approximates cos(theta)~1, valid only for a normal-sitting lobe), while evaluate() (288-303) centers the lobe on the oblique specular direction, so the cos(theta_s) weight over the lobe is ~cos(theta_spec). Docstring (259-261) claims TIS==scattered_fraction with no normal-incidence restriction.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Looked for a pinned convention: base-class numerical TIS (bsdf.py:113-152) hardcodes inc=(0,0,-1), so the framework TIS is normal-incidence-only — makes the closed form internally consistent but is undocumented in both docstrings, so the user-facing claim stays wrong. (2) Searched all in-package consumers: only sample_scatter_rays (513-574) uses the BSDF, via sample() directions only; no package code hemisphere-integrates evaluate() at oblique incidence — limits blast radius to user-side irradiance integrals (keeps this P3, not P2). (3) Checked tests: validation/analysis/test_features.py:541-543,781 and tests/unit/test_v5_4_6_wave5_delegated.py cover only the closed-form TIS value and normal-incidence sampling; no oblique-incidence integral consistency test exists. All three attempts failed to refute; probe reproduces the exact cos(theta_i) factor.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Make _normalization incidence-aware (divide A by |cos(theta_spec)| given incident_dir, i.e. A = f/(2*pi*sigma^2*|N_i|)) or explicitly document that TIS==scattered_fraction holds only at normal incidence and that oblique hemisphere integrals carry a cos(theta_i) factor.

### [P3-16] _FROZEN_CACHE grows one dense (Nx*Ny)^2 complex operator per Bloch k-point with no eviction — band-structure scans (the module's stated niche) accumulate GBs

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/eme/_jax_modes.py:48`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** _FROZEN_CACHE is a plain dict keyed on (Nx, Ny, Lx, Ly, kx0, ky0) (line 67-68). _frozen_helmholtz_L caches a dense (Nx*Ny, Nx*Ny) complex128 Laplacian per key (line 112) and _frozen_yee_dense caches FOUR dense operators per key (line 82). kx0/ky0 vary per point in exactly the band-structure use case the EME module exists for, so every k-point permanently adds a dense-operator set. No LRU bound; enrolled in the registry but only manual clear_asm_caches drains it.

**Failure scenario.** Vector band-structure scan on a 64x64 grid (N=4096): each yee entry is 4 dense 4096^2 complex arrays ~1.07 GB; a 25-point k-path retains ~27 GB after the scan completes -> OOM on typical workstations, or silent multi-GB residency in a notebook session.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (scratchpad/verify_eme_frozen_cache.py, run from repo root): 25-point kx0 scan of _frozen_helmholtz_L(Nx=Ny=12) -> 25 permanent entries, 8.29 MB; repeat scan -> still 25 (cache hits, no growth); _frozen_yee_dense 5-point scan -> 5 entries at 1.327 MB each; lumenairy.clear_asm_caches() -> 0 entries (registered drain works). Per-entry scaling confirmed analytically: yee entry = 4*(Nx*Ny)^2*16 B = 1.0737 GB at 64x64, so a 25-point k-path would retain ~26.8 GB exactly as claimed. Code-trace: _FROZEN_CACHE (line 48) is a plain dict, keys include float(kx0), float(ky0) (line 67-68), inserts at lines 82 and 112 with no eviction anywhere in the file. Severity demotion: (a) the docstring (lines 32-35) explicitly scopes this JAX path to ~12x12 grids because of the O((4NxNy)^3) dense eig + inv(V) VJP — at 64x64 vector the generator alone is a 16384^2 dense matrix (~4.3 GB) and each eig takes hours, so the headline 27 GB scenario is outside the feasible envelope; at documented 12x12 a 25-point scan retains only 8-33 MB; (b) clear_asm_caches is a working, code-documented escape hatch; (c) realistic worst case is intermediate grids, e.g. 32x32 vector = 67 MB/k-point -> ~6.7 GB silently retained over a 100-point k-path in a long notebook session.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for eviction/bounding elsewhere: none — plain dict, only manual _clear_frozen_cache via the cache registry. Checked callers: eme_2d.py:271-277 and eme_2d_vector.py:832 dispatch to the JAX twin only when eps/k0/kx0/ky0 is a jax array (gradient design loops), so plain band scans use the NumPy sparse path (no cache) — this weakens but does not eliminate the exposure, since differentiable band-structure objectives legitimately sweep kx0 through this path. Checked precedent: pmm _jax_twod.py _STATIC_CACHE uses the identical unbounded-dict pattern (intended convention), but pmm keys are geometry-static whereas EME keys include the scan-varying kx0/ky0, so the EME twin is genuinely more exposed. Verified clear_asm_caches actually drains it (it does). Verified repeat scans over the same k-points do NOT grow the cache (design-loop iteration is bounded by the number of distinct k-points).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace _FROZEN_CACHE with a small bounded LRU (OrderedDict, move_to_end on hit, popitem(last=False) beyond ~8 entries) — within a solve only the current k-point's operators are reused, so a handful of slots preserves all hits.

### [P3-17] Scalar strip solver silently discards Im(eps) for lossy media at kx0=0 (eigh on a non-Hermitian matrix), and ref_2d_modes silently drops complex parts

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/eme/eme_2d.py:62`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** strip_x_modes builds A with dtype complex always, so np.isrealobj(A) is always False (the A.real fast path is dead code) and the kx0==0 branch calls np.linalg.eigh(A) unconditionally. LAPACK Hermitian solvers ignore the imaginary part of the diagonal, so a lossy eps (Im(eps)!=0) is silently truncated to its lossless part — layer_modes/dispersion/mode_field then return the lossless spectrum with no error or warning. The scalar oracle ref_2d_modes likewise silently returns eig(...).real (line 302/310) with no return_complex or lossless guard, unlike the vector tier which documents lossless scope and offers return_complex. At kx0!=0 the same lossy input takes scipy eig and keeps complex lam — inconsistent silent behavior between kx0=0 and kx0!=0.

**Failure scenario.** eps_x = 2.0+0.5j (uniform lossy strip), kx0=0: strip_x_modes returns purely real lam (max|Im|=0.0) while the true spectrum of A has max|Im(lam)| = Im(eps)*k0^2 = 19.74. Any layer_modes call on a lossy layer returns the lossless modes as if the loss were not there — silent wrong physics on a mainline API with no documented lossless-only scope in the scalar module.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (py -P C:/tmp/verify_eme_2d_findings.py): strip_x_modes(full(16, 2+0.5j), 1.0, 16, 2*pi, kx0=0.0) -> lam dtype float64, max|Im(lam)| = 0.0, while np.linalg.eigvals of the identically rebuilt A gives max|Im| = 19.7392088 = Im(eps)*k0^2. Same input at kx0=0.1 (scipy eig branch) keeps max|Im| = 19.7392088 — inconsistent silent behavior confirmed. np.isrealobj(A) is False even for real-valued eps (A built dtype=complex at eme_2d.py:55/60), so the 'A.real if np.isrealobj(A)' fast path at line 62 is dead code and np.linalg.eigh always runs on the possibly non-Hermitian A, whose imaginary diagonal LAPACK ignores. ref_2d_modes(full((8,8), 2+0.5j), ...) -> float64, max|Im| = 0.0 (silent .real at lines 302/310). Grep of the whole eme package: no lossless-scope statement for the scalar tier (module docstring line 32 gives scope with no lossless caveat); the vector sibling documents 'lossless' explicitly and offers return_complex + beyn_refine_complex. No test exercises lossy scalar input.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched eme_2d.py docstrings, eme/README.md, and eme/__init__.py for a documented lossless-only scope on the scalar tier (none — only the vector tier documents it); looked for input validation or a warning on complex eps (none); checked whether any caller restricts eps to real (layer_modes and diffraction_eme pass user eps straight through); checked tests (no lossy scalar coverage). Mitigating context found: layer_modes' real-axis sigma_min scan structurally cannot return complex qz^2 anyway, and __init__.py routes lossy work to the vector Beyn refiner, so lossy scalar use is implicitly out of design scope — hence demotion, not refutation.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** In strip_x_modes (and ref_2d_modes), detect Im(eps) != 0 and either route the kx0=0 branch to the general eig path (matching kx0!=0) or raise/warn a documented lossless-only scope, plus a return_complex option on ref_2d_modes for parity with the vector oracle.

### [P3-18] layer_modes crashes with LinAlgError when a qz2 scan sample lands on a strip band edge (ky=0); diffraction_eme's own default window guarantees the hit

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/eme/eme_2d.py:80`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** _interface does np.linalg.solve(Vb, Va) with Vb = Phi@diag(i*ky); at qz2 == lam_n exactly, ky_n = 0 and Vb is exactly singular -> LinAlgError 'Singular matrix' escapes uncaught from layer_modes. For any x-uniform strip at kx0=0 the discrete band top is exactly eps*k0^2 (the constant mode has zero FD Laplacian), and np.linspace includes the range endpoints, so the natural window hi = max(eps)*k0^2 crashes deterministically. diffraction_eme (eme_diffraction.py:181-182) constructs exactly that default window (hi = max Re(eps) * k0^2), so its default crashes whenever the max-eps strip is x-uniform (e.g. the standard [host, pillar, host] cells from the README). The vector path is immune (block-G assembly, no inversion), an unflagged robustness asymmetry between the siblings.

**Failure scenario.** layer_modes([(full(12,2.0),0.5),(full(12,2.0),0.5)], 1, 12, 1, 2*pi, (0.0, 2*(2*pi)**2)) -> LinAlgError. diffraction_eme with strips=[(uniform eps=2, 0.5), (structured, 0.5)] and default qz2_window -> LinAlgError instead of a result or a clear message.

**Verification evidence.** Probe reproduced both crashes exactly as claimed: layer_modes([(full(12,2.0),0.5),(full(12,2.0),0.5)], 1.0, 12, 1.0, 2*pi, (1.0, 2*(2*pi)**2)) raises numpy.linalg.LinAlgError('Singular matrix') from np.linalg.solve(Vb, Va) at eme_2d.py:80 (the x-uniform strip's constant mode has an exactly-zero FD row sum, eigh returns lam = eps*k0^2 exactly, np.linspace includes hi = 2*k0^2 exactly, ky = 0 makes Vb = Phi@diag(i*ky) exactly singular). diffraction_eme([(uniform eps=2, 0.5), (structured, 0.5)], 1.0, 12, 1.0, 2*pi, 1.0, 1.0, 0.3, 1, 1, n_scan=200) with default qz2_window (eme_diffraction.py:181-182 constructs hi = max Re(eps)*k0^2) raises the same LinAlgError. No try/except anywhere in the chain.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a guard in _interface/cell_smatrix/dispersion (none); checked whether shipped tests hit the band edge (all use windows strictly below the band top, e.g. (150,256), (120,256) — so the edge is untested, not protected); considered whether float roundoff would prevent the exact hit (it does not — probe confirms exact singularity for x-uniform strips). Demotion rationale: the failure is loud (an exception, not silent wrong numbers), requires exact float coincidence of a scan sample with a band edge (in practice only via the window endpoint), and the only guaranteed-default trigger is diffraction_eme, which is explicitly documented as an EXPLORATORY dead end; the workaround (shrink hi slightly) is trivial.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Nudge scan samples off exact per-strip band edges (or shrink linspace endpoints by ~1 ulp of the range), or catch the singular _interface solve during the scan and treat that sample's sigma_min as the band-edge limit; also drop diffraction_eme's default hi slightly below max(eps)*k0^2.

### [P3-19] No validation that strip heights sum to Ly -> silently wrong Bloch modes at ky0 != 0 (scalar and vector finders)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/eme/eme_2d.py:136`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** layer_modes (and layer_vector_modes, eme_2d_vector.py:493) document 'heights sum to Ly' but never check it. The cell physics (per-strip propagators exp(+-i ky h), interface chain) uses sum(h) as the cell height, while the Bloch wrap phase uses the separately-passed Ly (t = exp(i ky0 Ly)). If sum(h) != Ly the two are inconsistent: at ky0=0 (t=1) the result quietly corresponds to a cell of height sum(h), and at ky0 != 0 the returned qz^2 are wrong for BOTH interpretations, with no raise. mode_field/mode_field_vec additionally sample yc over [0, Ly) but clamp the strip index to S-1 with eta = y - edges[S-1] possibly >> h_{S-1}, silently extrapolating exp(+-i ky eta) beyond the cell.

**Failure scenario.** Uniform eps=2 cell, ky0 = pi/Ly: heights (0.5,0.5) (correct) gives modes [69.087, 30.503] matching the analytic 2*k0^2-(pi)^2 = 69.087; heights (0.3,0.3) with the same Ly=1 returns [51.541, 12.957] — wrong for any consistent cell, returned silently.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: uniform eps=2, Ly=1, ky0=pi: heights (0.5,0.5) gives modes [69.08723, 30.50255] matching analytic 2*k0^2 - pi^2 = 69.08723; heights (0.3,0.3) with the same Ly=1 silently returns [51.54127, 12.95658] — probe additionally pinned this to exactly 2*k0^2 - (pi/0.6)^2 = 51.54127, i.e. a cell of height sum(h)=0.6 with total wrap phase ky0*Ly=pi, wrong under either documented interpretation of (ky0, Ly). No exception, no warning. Code trace: layer_modes (eme_2d.py:136-184) and layer_vector_modes never compare sum(h) with Ly; cell physics uses sum(h) while the Bloch phase t = exp(i*ky0*Ly) at line 132 uses the separately-passed Ly. mode_field line 248 clamps the strip index to S-1 with eta = y - edges[S-1] unbounded, extrapolating exp(+-i*ky*eta) beyond the cell when sum(h) < Ly.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether any caller enforces the constraint: eps_xy_to_strips (the mainline geometry helper) always produces heights summing to Ly, and diffraction_eme/tests all pass consistent heights — so the mainline generated-strips path cannot hit it; only hand-built strip lists can. Checked the docstring: line 140 explicitly documents 'heights sum to Ly', making the failure a violation of a stated precondition rather than an undocumented trap. That documented contract plus the guarded mainline path is why this demotes to P3 despite the silent-wrong-physics outcome.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Add a one-line tolerance check at layer_modes/layer_vector_modes entry (raise if abs(sum(h) - Ly) > rtol*Ly) and the same in mode_field/mode_field_vec before the y-sampling loop.

### [P3-20] _global_lateral_nullspace computes the full SVD twice (svdvals(G) then np.linalg.svd(G)) — 2x O(n^3) per mode-field reconstruction

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/eme/eme_2d.py:228`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Lines 228-229 run 's = svdvals(G)' and then 'c = np.linalg.svd(G)[2][-1].conj()' on the same (2*Nx*S)^2 matrix; a single np.linalg.svd call provides both the singular values and the null vector. The vector sibling mode_field_vec (eme_2d_vector.py:582-584) already does it in one call — the two implementations have drifted. diffraction_eme calls mode_field once per retained order (Npw times), doubling the dominant reconstruction cost for zero benefit.

**Failure scenario.** diffraction_eme with Mx=My=2 (Npw=25) on an 8-strip, Nx=40 cell: G is 640x640; 25 reconstructions pay 50 SVDs instead of 25 — roughly half the mode_field time wasted, growing as (Nx*S)^3.
