**Verification evidence.** eme_2d.py:228-230 reads `s = svdvals(G)` then `c = np.linalg.svd(G)[2][-1].conj()` then `return c, s[-1]` — two independent SVD factorizations of the same (2*Nx*S)^2 complex matrix. The vector sibling mode_field_vec (eme_2d_vector.py:582-584) already uses one `np.linalg.svd` call for both sigma and the null vector, so the two implementations have drifted exactly as claimed. diffraction_eme calls mode_field once per retained layer mode in a loop (eme_diffraction.py:195: `Psi[:, n] = mode_field(sm, q, ky0, Ly, Ny)[0]`). Probe (C:/tmp/verify_eme2d_findings.py, n=160 near-singular complex G): two-call path 77.44 ms vs one-call 42.80 ms → 1.81x; svdvals[-1]=1.0002103180141574e-12 vs np.linalg.svd s[-1]=1.0002103180141576e-12, |diff|=2.02e-28 (1 ULP — NOT byte-identical as the repro claimed, but exact to machine precision); null-vector residual ||G@c||=1.0e-12 as expected. One accuracy nit in the finding: the loop count is len(retained qz2 modes), not Npw per se.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a reason the two calls could be intentional (different LAPACK driver for the residual, numerical guard, caller relying on scipy-svdvals semantics): none — the returned sigma is only used as a mode-residual diagnostic and a 1-ULP change is immaterial; no test pins the exact sigma value; the vector twin already ships the single-call pattern, proving it is the intended idiom. Confirmed the extra cost is real by timing (1.81x, i.e. ~45% of the pair wasted since svdvals-without-UV is slightly cheaper than full SVD) rather than assuming LAPACK caches anything.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace lines 228-229 with a single `_, s, Vh = np.linalg.svd(G)` and return `Vh[-1].conj(), s[-1]`, mirroring mode_field_vec (note: sigma may shift by ~1 ULP vs scipy svdvals, which no caller depends on).

### [P3-21] Duplicated strips_to_eps_xy: scalar-only copy in eme_2d.py shadowed at package level by the drifted tensor-aware copy in eme_2d_vector.py

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/eme/eme_2d.py:315`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Two same-named public functions exist: eme_2d.strips_to_eps_xy (line 315, scalar-only, raises a shape broadcast error on tensor strips) and eme_2d_vector.strips_to_eps_xy (line 880, tensor-aware). __init__.py imports only the vector one (with the comment 'the general (tensor-aware) rasterizer'), so 'from lumenairy.elements.eme import strips_to_eps_xy' and 'from ...eme.eme_2d import strips_to_eps_xy' give different behaviors for the same call. Neither copy validates that heights sum to Ly, and both leave uncovered cells at eps=0 (which then feeds 1/(k0*eps) -> inf in the oracle generator), and both crash on the magnetic 3-tuple strips (see the magnetic finding).

**Failure scenario.** A user following an older example that imports from eme_2d passes a tensor strip list: shape-broadcast ValueError from the scalar copy, while the identically-named package-level function would have worked; conversely code written against the package facade silently behaves differently if refactored to import from eme_2d.

**Verification evidence.** Two same-named public functions: eme_2d.py:315 (scalar-only) and eme_2d_vector.py:880 (tensor-aware); __init__.py:50 imports only the vector one with the comment 'the general (tensor-aware) rasterizer' (probe: pkg fn `is` vector copy True, `is` scalar copy False). Probe on (Nx=8,3,3) tensor strips: scalar copy raises ValueError 'shape mismatch: value array of shape (8,1,3,3) could not be broadcast...', vector copy returns (8,10,3,3). Heights summing to 0.8 < Ly=1.0: BOTH copies leave 2 of 10 y-columns identically eps=0 (no validation). Magnetic 3-tuple strips (eps_x, h, mu_x) — the documented format per _strip_modes_at (eme_2d_vector.py:360): BOTH copies raise ValueError 'too many values to unpack (expected 2, got 3)' from `[h for _, h in strips]`. All tests (test_eme_2d.py:17-23, test_eme_2d_vector.py:30, test_eme_diffraction.py:20) and the internal caller (eme_2d_vector.py:487) go through the vector copy; the eme_2d scalar copy is shadowed dead code reachable only by direct-module import.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the scalar copy is deliberately kept and tested as a scalar-fast path: no test or internal code imports it from eme_2d; the facade comment explicitly designates the vector copy as 'the general' one, so the duplicate is drift, not design. Checked whether the eps=0 sub-claim of '1/(k0*eps) -> inf in the oracle generator' holds for the scalar oracle: it does NOT — ref_2d_modes uses eps multiplicatively (eme_2d.py:292), so scalar-path zeros give silently wrong physics, not inf; 1/eps appears in the vector strip generator (eme_2d_vector.py:130-139). That detail is overstated for the scalar path but the core duplication/divergence claim is fully reproduced. Impact stays P3: only non-facade imports hit the divergence, and the exception is loud, not silent corruption.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Delete the scalar copy in eme_2d.py (or make it a thin re-export of the tensor-aware one), and in the surviving copy accept 2- or 3-tuple strips (ignore mu) and validate/normalize sum(heights) vs Ly instead of leaving eps=0 cells.

### [P3-22] Magnetic (eps, h, mu) 3-tuple strips — a shipped documented feature — crash mode_field_vec, strips_to_eps_xy, and layer_vector_modes(verify=True)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/eme/eme_2d_vector.py:587`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** The magnetic-media feature accepts strips as (eps_x, h, mu_x) 3-tuples (handled by _strip_modes_at line 383-384), but three sibling consumers still unpack 2-tuples: mode_field_vec line 587 ('[h for _, h in strips]'), strips_to_eps_xy lines 886/891 ('for _, h in strips' / 'for s, (ex, _) in enumerate(strips)'), and hence _fd_eig_dist (line 487) used by verify=True. All raise ValueError('too many values to unpack (expected 2, got 3)'). Worse, even if the unpack were tolerant, _fd_eig_dist builds the FD verify oracle via _build_generator WITHOUT mu (line 488, mu_xy defaults to None -> mu=1), so verify=True would compare magnetic layer modes against a non-magnetic oracle and falsely reject genuine modes.

**Failure scenario.** layer_vector_modes(mag_strips, ..., verify=True) with mag_strips=[(eps,0.5,mu),(eps,0.5)] raises ValueError mid-scan; mode_field_vec(mag_strips, ...) raises the same after already computing the strip modes — the documented magnetic feature is unusable with field reconstruction and with the spurious-verify filter.

**Verification evidence.** Reproduced all three crashes by probe. (1) strips_to_eps_xy([(eps,0.5,mu),(eps,0.5)],1,6,1,12) -> ValueError('too many values to unpack (expected 2, got 3)') at line 886/891 ('for _, h in strips' / 'for s, (ex, _) in ...'). (2) mode_field_vec(same,...) -> identical ValueError at line 587 ('[h for _, h in strips]'). (3) On the test-suite reference cell (k0=8, KY0=pi, Nx=16, grating+uniform, qz2 in (130,256)) with mu=1 supplied as 3-tuples: verify=False returns 6 modes byte-identical to the 2-tuple form ([205.975, 201.887, 169.162, 151.385, 146.421, 140.600]); verify=True raises the ValueError exactly when the first accepted candidate reaches _fd_eig_dist (2-tuple verify=True works, dropping the spurious 169.162). Secondary claim code-trace-airtight: _fd_eig_dist line 488 calls _build_generator without mu_xy -> _build_generator (line 715) defaults mu=1 (lines 736-740), so even a fixed unpack would compare a magnetic layer against a non-magnetic FD oracle. 3-tuple support is intended: _strip_modes_at lines 383-385 handles it and its docstring (line 360) documents '(eps_x, h) or (eps_x, h, mu_x)'.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for an upstream guard or strip-normalizer — none; only _strip_modes_at is 3-tuple-aware. Checked tests: test_vector_magnetic_mu (tests/unit/test_eme_2d_vector.py:364) exercises only strip_vector_modes(mu_x=...) and ref_2d_modes_vector(mu_xy=...), never 3-tuple strips through the crashing consumers, so nothing covers or contradicts the claim. Checked documentation intent: eme/README.md line 162 promises magnetic media via mu_x/mu_xy 'in the strip generator and the oracle' (both work and are tested); the 3-tuple LAYER form is documented only in the private _strip_modes_at docstring, and the public layer_vector_modes/strips_to_eps_xy docstrings mention only (eps_x, h) 2-tuples — so the headline magnetic feature is intact, only the half-plumbed 3-tuple layer extension crashes. Also confirmed the base layer path (verify=False) genuinely works with 3-tuples, so this is not an 'unsupported input' non-issue.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Unpack strips tolerantly (h = s[1], optional mu = s[2]) in mode_field_vec and strips_to_eps_xy, have strips_to_eps_xy also rasterize a mu_xy grid, and thread it into _build_generator inside _fd_eig_dist so the verify oracle is magnetic-consistent.

### [P3-23] diffraction_eme returns an undocumented (dict, qz2) tuple while its sibling driver diffraction_fd returns the bare dict

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/eme/eme_diffraction.py:198`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The two mode-matching drivers share the matcher and are presented as parallel ('the matcher is shared with diffraction_fd'), but diffraction_fd returns mode_match(...)'s dict directly (line 161) while diffraction_eme returns 'mode_match(...), qz2' (line 196-198). Neither docstring mentions the tuple; the diffraction_eme docstring describes no return shape at all.

**Failure scenario.** Code written against diffraction_fd swapped to diffraction_eme (the documented apples-to-apples comparison the module exists for) does res['energy'] and gets TypeError: tuple indices must be integers — or worse, res[0] vs res patterns silently diverge in comparison scripts.

**Verification evidence.** eme_diffraction.py:196-198 ends 'return mode_match(...), qz2' vs diffraction_fd's bare 'return mode_match(...)' at line 161; docstrings (lines 171-178, and mode_match's 'Returns dict') never mention the tuple. Live probe (uniform layer, Mx=My=1, k0=2pi/0.8, qz2_window=(-8*hi, hi)): diffraction_fd -> type dict, energy=1.0000000000000018; diffraction_eme -> type tuple len 2, elem types ['dict', 'ndarray'], and out['energy'] raises TypeError: tuple indices must be integers or slices, not str — exactly the claimed failure when code written against diffraction_fd is swapped to diffraction_eme.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched every in-repo caller: only tests/unit/test_eme_diffraction.py (3 calls, all diffraction_fd) and the export-walker test test_v4_16_0_walker_all_symmetry.py:223-224 (symbol-existence only) — nothing covers or documents the tuple. Checked docstrings/module header for the tuple being intended: absent. Considered the module's 'EXPLORATORY dead-end' status as mitigation — it reduces impact but both drivers are publicly exported side-by-side in lumenairy/elements/eme/__init__.py:83-84 as a parallel pair, so the inconsistency is real. Note: the trivial probe first hit the len(qz2)<Npw guard (found 6 modes < N_pw=9), which shows diffraction_eme is somewhat hard to call casually, but a wider qz2_window reproduces the tuple return.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Return the mode_match dict directly and attach the eigenvalues as res['qz2'] (or at minimum document the (dict, qz2) tuple in the diffraction_eme docstring).

### [P3-24] rytov_tensor / rytov_segments_tensor / BerremanStack.add_effective_grating have no period/wavelength validity guard - order=2 silently accepts period >= wavelength where the (period/lambda)^2 correction is meaningless

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/emt.py:107`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The EMT bridge is exact only as period -> 0 (documented), but nothing in emt.py or add_effective_grating warns or raises when the user supplies period/wavelength outside the homogenization regime. For order=2 the correction pref = (pi^2/3)*(period/wavelength)^2*f^2*(1-f)^2 is an asymptotic series term that grows without bound: at period/lambda >= ~1 it can drive eps_par/eps_perp far outside the constituent bounds (even negative for high contrast), and the function returns it silently. For order=0 the period argument is accepted and ignored, so a caller passing a half-wavelength period gets a resonance-blind tensor with no diagnostic. This is exactly the audit hunt item 'EMT Rytov validity-domain guards (period/lambda warned)' - the guard does not exist.

**Failure scenario.** rytov_tensor(12+0.1j, 1.0, 0.5, period=800e-9, wavelength=633e-9, order=2): (period/lambda)^2=1.6, pref~0.33, eps_par correction ~0.33*(11+0.1j)^2 ~ +40 - eps_par ~ 46 vs constituents 1..12, i.e. non-physical, returned with no warning; a Berreman screen sweep built on it silently ranks designs on garbage dispersion.

**Verification evidence.** lumenairy/elements/emt.py (no warnings import, no ratio check; grep for warn|UserWarning: zero matches) and lumenairy/elements/berreman.py:367-390 (add_effective_grating only raises when order=2 lacks a wavelength; never inspects period/wavelength magnitude). Probe with warnings.simplefilter('error'): rytov_tensor(12+0.1j, 1.0, 0.5, period=800e-9, wavelength=633e-9, order=2) returned with NO warning: eps_par = 46.2357+0.7725j, eps_perp = 13.1329+0.1268j - eps_par ~4x above the max constituent (12), non-physical, at (period/lambda)^2 = 1.598, pref = 0.3286, exactly as claimed. order=0 with period=5e-6 at 633 nm (period/lambda ~ 7.9, deep diffractive regime) also accepted silently. Unit tests only cover the order=2-needs-period ValueError, no validity-domain diagnostic.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a guard at the caller (add_effective_grating) - none beyond the missing-wavelength ValueError. Searched emt.py and berreman.py for any warnings usage - none. Considered the documented-limitation defense: the module and function docstrings do state EMT is exact only as period->0 and that order=2 'can overshoot', and order=0 explicitly documents period as ignored - so the approximation is documented intent, which caps this at a diagnostics gap rather than a physics bug; but nothing at runtime flags eps outside constituent bounds or period >= lambda, so the gap itself stands.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Emit a UserWarning from rytov_tensor/rytov_segments_tensor (and hence add_effective_grating) when order=2 is used with period/wavelength above ~0.3-0.5, and optionally when a supplied period exceeds lambda/max|n| for order=0.

### [P3-25] Module docstring says the JAX Maslov variant lives in lumenairy.elements.lenses; it actually lives in _lens_jax.py

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/lenses_maslov.py:11`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** lenses_maslov.py lines 11-14 state 'the JAX variant apply_real_lens_maslov_jax lives in lumenairy.elements.lenses because it shares a Chebyshev evaluator with apply_real_lens_traced_jax'. Both JAX variants were extracted to _lens_jax.py in v3.5.5 (per _lens_jax.py lines 17-18) and are only RE-EXPORTED from lenses.py (lenses.py line 907-913). The stated rationale (shared Chebyshev evaluator in lenses.py) is also stale -- the shared helpers are _cheb_* functions inside _lens_jax.py.

**Failure scenario.** A maintainer or auditor following the docstring goes hunting for the implementation (and its Chebyshev evaluator) in lenses.py, wasting time or patching the wrong module's re-export.

**Verification evidence.** lenses_maslov.py:11-14 states the JAX variant 'lives in :mod:`lumenairy.elements.lenses` because it shares a Chebyshev evaluator with apply_real_lens_traced_jax'. Grep shows the only definition is _lens_jax.py:614 `def apply_real_lens_maslov_jax(`; lenses.py:911-914 merely re-imports it (`from ._lens_jax import apply_real_lens_maslov_jax, apply_real_lens_traced_jax`) under a comment saying the JAX propagators 'moved to lumenairy.elements._lens_jax in v3.5.5'. The shared Chebyshev evaluator (_cheb_T_recurrence_jax / _cheb_fit_2d_jax / _cheb_eval_value_grad_jax) lives at _lens_jax.py:49-124, not in lenses.py — so both the stated location and the stated rationale are stale.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Tried reading the docstring charitably as 'importable from lenses' (true via re-export) — fails because the sentence gives an implementation-location rationale (shared Chebyshev evaluator in lenses.py) that is false: no _cheb_* helper exists in lenses.py. Grepped the whole elements/ package for other definitions of apply_real_lens_maslov_jax (only _lens_jax.py:614) and for _cheb_ helpers (only _lens_jax.py and _lens_traced.py). _lens_jax.py's own docstring (lines 17-18) independently corroborates the extraction. Doc-only, so no guard/caller/test could refute it.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Update lenses_maslov.py:11-14 to say the JAX variant apply_real_lens_maslov_jax lives in lumenairy.elements._lens_jax (sharing the _cheb_* evaluators there with apply_real_lens_traced_jax) and is re-exported from lenses.py.

### [P3-26] _converged_cluster docstring claims MUTUAL per-order agreement but the code builds a star (all within tol of one anchor), admitting members up to 2x tol apart

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/_core.py:182`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The docstring (:169-178) states the cluster is the largest group of passive solves 'that MUTUALLY agree PER-ORDER ... within tol'. The implementation (line 182: `grp = [b for b in pidx if _aligned_max_diff(records[a], records[b]) <= tol]`) collects everything within tol of a single anchor `a` — no pairwise check between non-anchor members, so two members of the returned 'plateau' can differ per-order by up to 2*_PER_ORDER_TOL = 6e-3. With _MIN_PLATEAU = 2 the returned pick is still within tol of the anchor, so the practical accuracy bound quoted elsewhere ('accurate to the scan's per-order tolerance ~3e-3 worst case', oned.py:347-348) holds only for the anchor-adjacent members; a 3-member star can include a member 6e-3 off that _energy_clean_pick may select on a lossless structure whose energy errors tie. Fix is either a clique check or documenting the star-with-anchor semantics.

**Failure scenario.** Three passive degrees A (anchor), B, C with d(A,B)=d(A,C)=2.9e-3 but d(B,C)=5.8e-3 cluster together; if B has the smallest |tot-1| it is returned as 'the consensus the converged degrees agree on' while disagreeing with C by nearly 2x the documented tolerance — a quiet widening of the stabilize accuracy contract, not a crash.

**Verification evidence.** Direct probe on _core._converged_cluster with three fabricated passive records: d(A,B)=2.9e-3, d(A,C)=2.9e-3, d(B,C)=5.8e-3, tol=3e-3, min_cluster=2 -> returned cluster=[0,1,2]: B and C are co-clustered while disagreeing per-order at 5.8e-3 ~ 2x tol. Code: _core.py:182 `grp = [b for b in pidx if _aligned_max_diff(records[a], records[b]) <= tol]` — anchor-star only, no b-vs-c pairwise test; docstring :170 says 'mutually agree PER-ORDER ... within tol'. _energy_clean_pick (:1427-1429) can then return any cluster member (min |tot-target|), so the returned solution can differ from another 'agreeing' member by up to 2*_PER_ORDER_TOL.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the returned pick is always the anchor (which would keep the accuracy bound): no — _energy_clean_pick selects by energy error over the whole cluster, and in the lossy branch (:1430) picks index 0 or cluster[0], neither guaranteed to be the star's anchor (best is the largest grp, whose anchor `a` need not be its first element). Checked _STABILIZE window size: with _MIN_PLATEAU=2 the common 2-member case IS pairwise-consistent, so real-world exposure needs >=3 scanned passive degrees straddling a slow drift — plausible but marginal, hence P3. Docstring-vs-code mismatch itself is unambiguous.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Either make it a clique (require pairwise _aligned_max_diff <= tol among grp members) or amend the docstring and the oned.py:347 accuracy claim to state star-around-anchor semantics with a 2x-tol worst-case spread.

### [P3-27] JAX stack twin solves the uniform half-spaces with two independent dense 2n x 2n custom-VJP eigs; the NumPy stack shares one n x n geometric eig (the v5.14.2 shared-eig optimization is missing from the twin)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/_jax_stack.py:226`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** Lines 226-229 assemble full multi-region SEM operators for the UNIFORM superstrate and substrate (_jstack_assemble: an O(n_el) python loop of 11 dense (n_glob,n_glob) scatter-adds each) and run _jpmm_sem_modes_tensor on each -- a 2n x 2n jnp.linalg.eig (CPU-only) plus the custom-VJP's extra inv(V) on the backward pass. The NumPy stack instead uses _uniform_geo_eig + _sem_modes_uniform (stack.py:662-666, pmm/_core.py:1012-1041): ONE eps-free n x n geometric eig shared by BOTH half-spaces (spectrum q^2 = eps - mu), documented as ~16x cheaper because half-spaces were the audited 51-64% of 1-D eig time (backlog A2, shipped v5.14.2 at x5.5-6). The twin regressed to the pre-A2 structure, and since eig cost dominates the traced solve, every forward AND gradient evaluation in an optimization loop pays 2 x 8n^3 where n^3 (shared, and analytically eps-shifted, hence trivially differentiable) suffices.

**Failure scenario.** A gradient-descent design loop over a 3-layer PMMStack at degree 12 with several segments (n_glob ~ 100s): per step the twin runs 5 dense 2n-eigs where 3 + one shared n-eig would do -- roughly 40-60% of the eig time spent on half-spaces alone, multiplied over hundreds of optimizer iterations; the VJP of each redundant eig adds an inv and three n^2-matmuls more.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace is airtight: _jax_stack.py:226-229 calls _jstack_assemble (python loop of 11 dense (n_glob,n_glob) scatter-adds per element) on BOTH uniform half-spaces and runs _jpmm_sem_modes_tensor on each — _core.py:2283-2285 builds the full 2n x 2n Mbig and eigs it via the custom-VJP eig (backward pass adds inv(V)). The NumPy stack (stack.py:662-666) instead computes ONE _uniform_geo_eig (_core.py:1012-1038, an eps-free n x n eig of Kx2) shared by both half-spaces via _sem_modes_uniform (q^2 = eps - mu, analytic eps shift, docstring: '~16x cheaper than two independent 2n eigs (the audited 51-64% of 1-D eig time spent on half-spaces)'). So per traced solve the twin pays 2 redundant 2n^3-scale eigs + their VJPs where one shared n^3 eig suffices; eps/n_sup gradients would flow analytically through q2 = eps - mu, and a traced k0/kx0 only requires ONE custom-VJP n x n eig. Two corrections to the claim: (a) 'regressed' is imprecise — the SINGLE-LAYER JAX twin has the identical structure (_core.py:2337-2342 runs _jpmm_sem_modes_tensor on mats_sup and mats_sub too), i.e. the JAX twins never received the NumPy-only A2 optimization, and the multilayer twin deliberately mirrors the shipped single-layer twin ('same building blocks', module docstring); (b) 'eig cost dominates the traced solve' is doubtful in eager (non-jit) execution: my timing probe (C:/tmp/lumjax_audit/audit_jax_probe4_halfspace_eig.py, 2 layers, degree 12, n_glob=216 -> 432x432 eigs that LAPACK solves in ~1 s each) had not completed after >5 min wall because the per-element .at[].add() assembly dispatch dominates — under jit the balance shifts back toward eig, but the eager path users actually hit is assembly-bound.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a shared-geo-eig equivalent anywhere in _jax_stack.py (grep 'geo|shared|uniform' — none); checked whether tracing k0/kx0/n_sup fundamentally blocks the shared-eig approach (it does not — Kx2 depends on k0,kx0 only, and eps enters the spectrum analytically, so at most one custom-VJP n x n eig is needed, or a pure-NumPy eig when angle/wl are concrete); checked whether the omission is documented as a deliberate trade-off (no comment in the twin or docs; docs/pmm_bor_axisymmetric_roadmap.md references _uniform_geo_eig only for BOR); checked the sibling single-layer twin — same structure, which reframes this from 'regression' to 'A2 never ported to any JAX twin'; started an in-process timing probe (monkeypatched _jpmm_sem_modes_tensor) — it confirmed the solve is minutes-long at 432x432 where the 3-4 eigs cost ~seconds, undermining the 'eig dominates' premise for the eager path.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Port A2 to both JAX twins: eig the n x n geometric Kx2 once (NumPy when k0/kx0 concrete, custom-VJP eig when traced), build half-space W2/V2 with the analytic q2 = eps - mu shift as _sem_modes_uniform does, and skip the two _jstack_assemble half-space assemblies entirely.

### [P3-28] _STATIC_CACHE keyed on continuous geometry (x0,x1,y0,y1 / layout bytes) with no eviction: PMM 2-D JAX geometry sweeps/optimizations retain ~3 MB+ per distinct geometry forever

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/_jax_twod.py:47`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** _STATIC_CACHE is a plain unbounded dict. _static_prep keys on the raw floats (period_x, period_y, x0, x1, y0, y1, degree, n_el, grade, n_orders) (line 74) and stores dense Fourier-projected operators (Tp, Tpinv, Gx0F, Gy0F, IprojF); _static_prep_cell (line 112) keys on layout.tobytes(). Every distinct pillar geometry — one per iterate of a width/gap sweep or a finite-difference geometry optimization (the user's metasurface exp10/exp11 workflow class) — inserts a multi-MB entry that is never evicted. Enrolled in the registry but drained only by an explicit clear_asm_caches.

**Failure scenario.** A 1000-point pillar-width x gap sweep through the pmm 2-D JAX twin at production n_orders=7-9 (projected operators scale as (2n+1)^4) retains tens of GB after the sweep; at the probed toy size it is already ~3 GB per 1000 geometries.

**Verification evidence.** _jax_twod.py:47 is a plain dict; _static_prep keys on raw floats (period_x, period_y, x0, x1, y0, y1, degree, n_el, grade, n_orders) at line 74 and _static_prep_cell on layout.tobytes() at line 112; grep confirms no eviction anywhere — only the registry clear hook (_clear_jax_twod_caches). Probe (C:/tmp/audit_verify_jax2d_cache.py): 20 calls with x0 swept (degree=4, n_el=2, n_orders=5) -> 20 entries, 58.8 MB ndarray bytes retained (2.94 MB/entry, exactly matching the claimed probe); one production-ish entry at degree=5, n_orders=7 is 8.92 MB, so a 1000-geometry sweep retains ~9 GB. In-repo precedent for the proposed fix exists: _WRAPPER_MERIT_CACHE bounded LRU, wrapper_merits.py:85-86 (maxsize 32) with popitem eviction at 231-232.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for eviction or size bounds elsewhere (none — grep shows only get/set/clear); checked whether wavelength/angle sweeps grow the cache (they do not — wavelength/theta/phi are not in the key, so the common sweep is safe); checked whether the multilayer stack path uses this cache (it does not — _jax_stack2d rebuilds _layer_static_traced per solve, so only the single-layer twins grow it); checked design intent — the twin's documented scope is TRACED materials on STATIC geometry, so the primary workflow inserts exactly one entry, and clear_asm_caches (v4.16.0 registry contract) is a documented workaround. Demoted P2->P3: unbounded growth is real and measured, but it requires an external geometry sweep through the JAX path (a secondary usage pattern for a twin whose bounds are explicitly non-differentiable), and a global clear escape hatch exists.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Convert _STATIC_CACHE to a lock-guarded bounded OrderedDict LRU following the _WRAPPER_MERIT_CACHE pattern (wrapper_merits.py:85-93, 227-232), with a small maxsize (e.g. 8-32) since each entry is 3-9 MB.

### [P3-29] Incidence angle past grazing (|angle| > pi/2) is silently aliased to the supplementary front-side angle in every 1-D PMM entry point

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/oned.py:392`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** No 1-D PMM public entry validates |angle| < pi/2. The angle enters only as kx0 = Re(n_sup)*sin(angle)*k0 (_core.py:742), so angle = 1.75 rad (100.3 deg, physically back-side/non-existent front-side illumination) produces a result byte-identical to pi - 1.75 (79.7 deg) — a silently wrong answer for the requested geometry rather than a raise. The grazing guard (kz_inc < 1e-9) only catches angle == pi/2 exactly. RCWA shares the hole (its guard is Re(eps_sup) <= kt0^2, and sin^2(100 deg) = 0.97 < 1), so a fix should land in both suites; within this slice the natural anchor is the shared angle resolution at each entry.

**Failure scenario.** pmm_efficiency_1d(..., angle=1.75, stabilize=False) runs without complaint and returns sum R = 0.5655 / sum T = 0.4345 — max|R(1.75) - R(pi-1.75)| == 0.0 exactly. A sweep script that lets angle wander past 90 deg gets plausible, energy-conserving, wrong-geometry efficiencies.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_pmm_angle_alias.py, py -P, repo root): pmm_efficiency_1d(angle=1.75, pol='tm', degree=12, stabilize=False) vs angle=pi-1.75 gives max|dR|=0.0, max|dT|=0.0, R/T arrays bit-identical (tobytes equal); the 1.75 rad (100.27 deg) solve returns sumR=0.24922, sumT=0.75078 (sum=1.0000015) with no warning. angle=3.0 rad (171.9 deg) also runs silently, sumR+T=1.0000000. Code trace: _resolve_incidence (_core.py:78-92) is pure alias resolution with no range check; angle is consumed only via np.sin(float(angle)) (_core.py:718, 742, 1109, 1363, 1396; jnp twins 2125/2541), so supplementary angles are mathematically indistinguishable; the grazing guard abs(kz_inc)<1e-9 (_core.py:636, 681) cannot fire because kz_inc is derived from the supplementary-symmetric kx0 and sin^2(100.3deg)=0.968<1.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a guard: _resolve_incidence has none; grepped _core.py for pi/2 / grazing / abs(angle) checks — only the kz_inc~0 grazing raise, which is supplementary-symmetric and does not trigger. Checked all 11 oned.py entry points calling _resolve_incidence — none adds a domain check. Checked the pmm_efficiency_1d docstring (oned.py:312-320): documents 'Incidence angle (radians)' with no stated fold-back semantics, so the aliasing is not documented intent. Ran the probe expecting a possible raise or divergence — got exact bit-identity instead.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Validate |angle| < pi/2 (or |Re(n_sup) sin(angle)| consistency) at the shared _resolve_incidence / entry-point angle resolution and raise ValueError for back-side angles, mirroring the fix into the RCWA suite's shared angle handling.

### [P3-30] PMMStack.add_layer accepts thickness <= 0 / NaN while sibling PMM2DStack.add_layer validates thickness > 0

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/stack.py:214`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** PMMStack.add_layer stores float(thickness) with no positivity/finiteness check (line 214-215); PMM2DStack.add_layer raises 'thickness must be > 0' (stack2d.py lines 132-137). A negative thickness flips every propagation exponent exp(-lam*k0*t) into growth (for lossy/evanescent modes an unphysical amplifying layer) and solves without any error; NaN propagates to NaN efficiencies silently.

**Failure scenario.** A geometry-builder sign error (t = z_top - z_bottom computed backwards) yields negative thickness; probe: add_layer(-0.3e-6, eps=4.0) accepted, solve returns without raising. For lossy layers the result is silently wrong (gain instead of absorption) and only the R+T>1.01 warning MIGHT trip.

**Verification evidence.** stack.py:214-216 stores float(thickness) with no check; stack2d.py:126-137 raises 'PMM2DStack.add_layer: thickness must be > 0' (probe confirmed the 2-D raise). Probe: PMMStack.add_layer(-0.3e-6, eps=4.0+0.5j) then solve() -> NO exception, max per-pol R+T = 2.5038 (unphysical gain; the physical +0.3um reference gives 0.4779); only the soft energy warning fires and its text misdiagnoses the cause ('reduce n_slices or raise degree'). thickness=NaN -> solve returns R+T = nan with NO warning at all (the NaN never trips 'worst > 1.0+1e-2').

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched solve()/set_source()/add_tapered_* for a downstream thickness guard (none in stack.py); checked whether the energy warning reliably catches the negative case (it did here at 2.5, but it is a warning not a raise, its message points users at the wrong knob, and a mildly negative thin layer could stay under the 1.01 threshold); checked the NaN path (completely silent — no guard fires); considered whether float(thickness) intentionally allows negatives for some phase-conjugate use (no doc or test suggests it; JAX branch in 2-D validates when concrete, so the 1-D omission is inconsistency, not intent).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Mirror the PMM2DStack validation (finite and > 0, with the same concrete-JAX carve-out) in PMMStack.add_layer so builder sign errors fail loudly at add time.

### [P3-31] Covariant (uniform-slant) dispatch returns before the retain_internal / stabilize handling: both kwargs are silently ignored and an invalid stabilize value is accepted

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/stack.py:644`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** solve() dispatches 'return self._solve_covariant(...)' at line 644, BEFORE the retain_internal branch (which for the general slanted path raises NotImplementedError at line 744) and before the stabilize validation at lines 828-833. On a uniform-slant stack, solve(retain_internal=True) silently no-ops (self._internal never set; a later internal_field misleadingly says 'call solve(retain_internal=True) first' -- which the user did), solve(stabilize='slices') silently skips the passive-but-wrong consensus tripwire, and solve(stabilize=<garbage>) does not raise. This is API drift vs the convection/general branch, which raises loudly for the same requests.

**Failure scenario.** st with add_layer(..., slant_angle=0.3); st.solve(retain_internal=True, stabilize='bogus_value') returns normally (probe: no exception, _internal is None). A user relying on stabilize='slices' as a taper-staircase tripwire gets no check at all once the stack is slanted; retain_internal appears accepted but produces nothing.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: uniform-slant stack (add_layer slant_angle=0.3), solve(retain_internal=True, stabilize='bogus_value') returns normally with no exception; _internal never set (attribute missing); subsequent internal_field raises "call solve(retain_internal=True) first" -- which the user did. Contrast probes: vertical stack with stabilize='bogus_value' raises ValueError at :831; mixed-slant (convection general path) with retain_internal=True raises NotImplementedError at :744. Code: 'return self._solve_covariant(wl, angle, k0)' at stack.py:644 precedes both the :743 NotImplementedError and the :828-833 stabilize validation, and _solve_covariant takes neither kwarg.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for kwarg validation inside _solve_covariant or a shared pre-dispatch validator: none. Checked the JAX dispatch (:575-583) which DOES validate both kwargs before its early return, confirming the covariant return is the one dispatch missing the pattern (API drift is real, not intended). Demotion rationale: no silently-wrong physics -- retain_internal no-op produces a loud (if misleading) downstream error rather than stale data on this path, and stabilize='slices' is only meaningful for recorded taper recipes, which the taper builders emit as VERTICAL staircase layers, so a uniform-slant stack routing covariant while carrying a taper recipe is an unlikely combination.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Hoist the stabilize validation and the retain_internal/stabilize NotImplementedError raises above the line-644 covariant return (mirroring the JAX-dispatch guard block at :575-583).

### [P3-32] _PreparedPMMStack._eig_cache / _mats_cache grow without bound across (wavelength, angle, material-content) points

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/stack.py:1594`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Both caches (lines 1594-1595) are plain dicts with no eviction and no size cap; every distinct (layer, wavelength, angle, resolved-eps-bytes) key stores a full modal set (W, V ~ (2*n_glob)^2 complex each) and every (wavelength, angle) stores two SEM operator dicts plus two half-space mode sets. Not enrolled in _cache_registry (instance-scoped, so clear_asm_caches cannot free them either); the only release is deleting the prepared object.

**Failure scenario.** An LC director sweep of 1000 tensors at fixed wavelength on a degree-16 multi-segment stack (n_glob ~ 300): each keyed-layer entry ~ 3*(600x600) complex ~ 8.6 MB -> ~8.6 GB retained by one long-lived prepared object; a 2-D (wavelength x angle x director) scan multiplies further. The documented intent ('re-eigs only the layers whose keys changed') needs only the CURRENT and key-free entries, not the full history.

**Verification evidence.** stack.py:1594-1595 plain dicts, no eviction; _layer_eig (1612-1633) keys by (i, wavelength, angle, resolved-eps-bytes) and inserts unconditionally; grep shows _cache_registry enrollment only in pmm/_core.py:4142 and pmm/_jax_twod.py:60 — the prepared object's instance dicts are NOT registered, and dir(prep) has no clear/evict attribute. Probe: 12 distinct LC tensors at one wavelength -> len(_eig_cache) grows strictly 1:1 (2->13); repeating a tensor is a hit (no growth); _mats_cache holds per-(wl,angle) SEM operators + two half-space mode sets. Each keyed entry stores W,V of shape (2*n_glob)^2 complex (0.009 MB at toy degree=4; ~11.5 MB at the finding's n_glob~300), so a 1000-tensor director sweep retains ~10+ GB in one long-lived prepared object while the documented purpose needs only the current keyed entry per layer.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked for enrollment in the package cache registry / clear_asm_caches (absent — instance-scoped dicts); looked for any size cap, LRU, or prep.clear() API (none); considered whether full-history caching is intended (docstring 1576-1579 promises re-eig avoidance for changed keys and per-(wl,angle) reuse — that contract needs at most the latest keyed entry per layer plus key-free entries, not every historical tensor); considered severity promotion (kept P3: only bites long-lived prepared objects on large distinct-material sweeps, memory is recovered by dropping the object, and the toy per-entry size is small — it scales badly only at high degree/many segments).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Bound the keyed-layer cache (e.g. keep only the most recent content per (layer, wl, angle), or a small LRU) and/or expose prep.clear(); optionally enroll both dicts in the _cache_registry so clear_asm_caches can free them.

### [P3-33] formulation='li' applies the inverse rule to the Ex slot only -- x/y-asymmetric, and for a y-patterned (x-uniform) cell the rules land on the WRONG components

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/twod.py:536`  |  **Category:** physics  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _layer_modes_projected puts EPS_normal (the inverse-rule operator EpnF) only in the Q(2,1) slot, which multiplies Ex, while Q(1,2) (multiplying Ey) always gets Laurent EpsF (lines 536-538). _assemble_2d's own comment (line 341) says Epn is for 'wall-normal Ex'. For an x-patterned cell that is Li-correct, but for a cell patterned along y (nsx==1 separable branch, lines 509-522) Ex is TANGENTIAL to every wall (should be Laurent) and Ey is wall-normal (should be inverse rule) -- both slots get the anti-Li assignment. Consequence: 'li' results are not invariant under a 90-degree rotation of the cell+incidence, and the docstring claim (line 741-743) "'li' = inverse rule for the wall-normal field component; recommended" is only true for x-walls. Both orientations still converge (the nodal grid resolves the wall; the rule only shifts the Fourier-projection floor), so this is a convergence-quality/consistency defect, not a wrong-limit one.

**Failure scenario.** Same physical 1-D lamellar grating fed as a (2,1) cell with TM vs the 90deg-rotated (1,2) cell with TE, formulation='li', degree=9: T0 = 0.08464 vs 0.08289 at n_orders=7 (ref 0.09651), i.e. rotated-by-90 answers differ by ~2e-3 and the y-patterned orientation is consistently the worse one; formulation='laurent' is exactly rotation-symmetric (0.07563 == 0.07563).

**Verification evidence.** Code trace: _layer_modes_projected (twod.py lines 536-541) sets EPS_normal=EpnF only in the Q block row multiplying Ex ([GyF@GyF - EPS_normal]), while the Ey slot (EpsF - GxF@GxF) always gets Laurent, for BOTH separable branches; in the y-patterned branch (lines 509-522) EpnF=kron(Ty@o1['Epn']@Typ, Ix) is the y-axis 1-D INVERSE rule applied to Ex, which is tangential (continuous) across y-normal walls, and the discontinuous Ey gets Laurent -- both slots anti-Li, exactly as claimed; _assemble_2d's comment (line 341) confirms Epn was intended for 'wall-normal Ex'. Probe (C:/tmp/probe_twod/verify_twod_probe.py, repo root, Px=Py=1.0, eps 6.25/1.0 half-half cell, n_sub=1.5, depth=0.5, wl=0.6, degree=9): x-patterned (2,1)-cell TM vs 90-deg-rotated (1,2)-cell TE T0 under 'li': n_orders=2: 0.9550946868 vs 0.9549438061 (diff +1.509e-04); n_orders=3: 0.5136498676 vs 0.5093158226 (diff +4.334e-03); n_orders=4: 0.0879432703 vs 0.0872645433 (diff +6.787e-04). Under 'laurent' the same pairs are bit-exact rotation-symmetric: diffs -2.2e-16, +1.4e-15 -- proving the two calls are the identical physical problem and the asymmetry is the 'li' slot assignment. No test asserts PMM-2D rotation invariance (grep of tests/ for formulation); module docstring and the pmm_efficiency_2d docstring (lines 741-743, "'li' = inverse rule for the wall-normal field component; recommended") contain no orientation caveat.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a guard or per-orientation dispatch: none -- both separable branches and the general factorized branch route EpnF unconditionally to the Ex slot. Checked whether the general nonseparable branch differs: there EpnF and EpsF differ only at shared wall nodes (mass-weighted arithmetic vs harmonic average), so the defect is the same shape. Checked tests for a rotation-symmetry or li-vs-laurent 2-D PMM assertion: none exist (only RCWA fff_nv tests cover rule choices). Checked whether the difference is a wrong limit: the 'laurent' control is bit-exact symmetric and both formulations share the same nodal wall-resolving grid, so the discrepancy is confined to the Fourier-projection floor (gap wanders 1.5e-4 -> 4.3e-3 -> 6.8e-4 over n_orders 2-4 while both branches head toward a common value); consistent with the claimed convergence-quality (not wrong-limit) classification. Could not find documentation making the asymmetry intended.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Build per-axis mixed composite eps operators (Li 1997: inverse rule along the patterned/wall-normal axis composed with Laurent along the tangential axis) and route the wall-normal-rule operator to whichever of the Ex/Ey slots is normal to the walls on each patterned axis (at minimum swap EpnF/EpsF slots in the nsx==1 separable branch).

### [P3-34] pmm_efficiency_2d docstring promises '0 <= x0 < x1 <= period_x' but the validator rejects boundary-touching pillars (strict 0 < lo < hi < period)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/twod.py:714`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The Parameters section (lines 713-714) documents the pillar bounds contract as '0 <= x0 < x1 <= period_x (and likewise in y)', but _validate_pillar_bounds (lines 256-270, applied at lines 798, 1100) enforces strictly interior walls '0.0 < lo < hi < float(per)' and raises ValueError otherwise. A pillar with x0 = 0.0 or x1 = period_x is documented as valid input yet raises. The raise itself is correct policy (a wall coincident with the cell edge degenerates the outer strip); the docstring is what is wrong.

**Failure scenario.** pmm_efficiency_2d(1.0, 1.0, 4.0, 1.0, x_bounds=(0.0, 0.5), y_bounds=(0.25, 0.75), ...) -- valid per the docstring -- raises 'x_bounds must satisfy 0 < lo < hi < period'.

**Verification evidence.** Docstring lines 712-714: "``(x0, x1)`` and ``(y0, y1)`` rectangle edges (metres), ``0 <= x0 < x1 <= period_x`` (and likewise in y)". Validator _validate_pillar_bounds (lines 256-270) enforces `if not (0.0 < lo < hi < float(per)): raise ValueError`, called at line 798 before the solve. Probe: pmm_efficiency_2d(1.0, 1.0, 4.0, 1.0, (0.0, 0.5), (0.25, 0.75), 1.5, 1.0, 0.5, 0.6, degree=9, n_orders=3) raised exactly: "pmm_efficiency_2d: x_bounds must satisfy 0 < lo < hi < period (strictly interior, non-degenerate walls); got (0, 0.5) wi..." -- a docstring-valid input rejected. The validator's own docstring (lines 257-260, v5.14 robustness audit: inverted bounds silently gave geometrically wrong energy-conserving answers) plus the error message's shift-the-origin guidance show the strict raise is intended policy, so the Parameters text is what is wrong.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether any caller normalizes boundary-touching bounds before validation (e.g. wrapping x0=0 to an interior wall): none -- _validate_pillar_bounds is the first geometric check on both the numpy path (line 798) and pmm_jones_2d's use (line ~1100), and the JAX branch dispatches on static bounds without relaxing the contract. Checked whether the '<=' wording appears in prepare_pmm_2d or other docstrings that would also need fixing: it does not (single occurrence). Considered whether the docstring might be right and the validator too strict: a wall at exactly 0 or period degenerates the outer strip (zero-width element -> singular mass matrix), and the validator comment documents that failure mode, so the code is the intended behavior.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Change the docstring bounds sentence to the enforced strict contract `0 < x0 < x1 < period_x` and mention that an edge-touching pillar should be expressed by shifting the unit-cell origin (as the ValueError already advises).

### [P3-35] pmm_efficiency_2d_cell docstring states max_nodal_dof 'default 4000, ~256 MB per dense operator' -- stale; the actual default is 150_000 with no dense N x N operator at all

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/twod.py:906`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The docstring (lines 905-907) still describes the pre-v5.14 dense-kron path: 'max_nodal_dof caps the dense nodal problem size nodes_x * nodes_y (default 4000, ~256 MB per dense operator)'. The actual default is _MAX_NODAL_DOF = 150_000 (line 253), and per the comment there the v5.14 factorized assembly never materializes a dense N x N operator (binding cost is the (Nf, N) projector pair). Users sizing a staircased-cell job from the docstring will wrongly conclude a 32-strip disk (N ~ 83k nodal DOF) is 20x over the cap and route to rcwa unnecessarily.

**Failure scenario.** User reads 'default 4000' and either avoids the cell path for a perfectly affordable N=50k geometry, or passes max_nodal_dof=4000 explicitly 'to match the documented default' and gets a spurious ValueError from _validate_cell_cost for a supported cell.

**Verification evidence.** twod.py lines 905-907 (pmm_efficiency_2d_cell docstring): "``max_nodal_dof`` caps the dense nodal problem size ``nodes_x * nodes_y`` (default 4000, ~256 MB per dense operator)". Actual default: signature line 876 `max_nodal_dof: int = _MAX_NODAL_DOF` with `_MAX_NODAL_DOF = 150_000` at line 253; the comment block at lines 245-252 states the v5.14 factorized assembly never materializes anything dense of size N x N (binding cost is the (Nf, N) projector pair) and explicitly calls 4000 'the old dense-kron path's' cap. max_nodal_dof is enforced via _validate_cell_cost (called at line 953 inside _solve_at), which raises ValueError when nx*ny exceeds it, so passing 4000 'to match the doc' does spuriously reject supported staircased cells (e.g. the 32-strip disk N~83k cited as supported at line 251). Grep for '4000' across lumenairy/elements/pmm: only twod.py:250 (intentional historical reference) and twod.py:906 (the stale doc) -- prepare_pmm_2d_cell / pmm_jones_2d do not repeat it.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether 'default 4000' could describe some other still-live path: the JAX branch (lines 921-938) does not take max_nodal_dof at all, and the only consumer is _validate_cell_cost with the 150_000 default -- no code path retains a 4000 default or a ~256 MB dense operator. Checked whether the docstring was already fixed elsewhere or duplicated: grep confirms the stale number appears exactly once in a docstring. No refutation found; this is a plain doc/code divergence introduced when _MAX_NODAL_DOF was raised in v5.14.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Update the pmm_efficiency_2d_cell docstring sentence to the current contract: default 150_000, cost dominated by the (Nf, N) projector pair under the v5.14 factorized assembly (no dense N x N operator).

### [P3-36] Undocumented Nx == Ny restriction enforced only by a deep cryptic assert; realistic non-square cells (y-uniform gratings) crash with AssertionError

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/twod_staggered.py:384`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** Granet2DTransverseE requires bx.dim == by.dim, i.e. Nx*(M-1) == Ny*(M-1), i.e. Nx == Ny, enforced by 'assert self.bx.dim == self.by.dim, "use square..."' at line 384. The public docstring of pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered (lines 803-808) describes eps_cell as any (Nx, Ny) grid and never states the square restriction; a y-uniform cell (Nx=2, Ny=1 -- the natural way to feed a 1-D lamellar grating, exactly the case the hybrid handles analytically) dies deep in the build with AssertionError 'use square (Nx*(M-1)==Ny*(M-1))'. Under python -O the assert is stripped and the failure becomes an even more cryptic kron/broadcast shape error. An input-validation boundary should raise ValueError at the entry point with guidance (e.g. 'pad the uniform axis to Ny=Nx equal segments').

**Failure scenario.** pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered(1.2, 1.2, np.array([[4.0],[1.0]]), 1.5, 1.0, 0.3, 1.0, degree=6) -> AssertionError with no function-name prefix and no hint that the fix is padding eps_cell to (2,2); any (Nx,Ny) with Nx != Ny fails identically.

**Verification evidence.** Reproduced: eps_cell=(2,1) with degree=6 and eps_cell=(3,2) with degree=5 both raise AssertionError 'use square (Nx*(M-1)==Ny*(M-1))' from twod_staggered.py:384 with no function-name prefix or padding guidance. Under py -O the assert strips and the failure becomes 'ValueError: could not broadcast input array from shape (50,50) into shape (100,100)' — the predicted cryptic kron/broadcast error, exactly as claimed. The docstring (lines 803-808) describes eps_cell as any '(Nx, Ny) array_like' and never states the square restriction; entry-point validation covers only polarization, degree>=3, and ndim==2 (lines 861-879).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether any entry-point validation or docstring note covers Nx==Ny (none; docstring even gives 'Nx = Ny = 2' only as an example, not a constraint). Checked whether tests exercise non-square cells or pin the error (test_validation pins only pol/degree/ndim). Considered whether the assert is an adequate fail-loud contract — it does always raise in normal mode, which is why I demote from P2 to P3: no silent wrong physics is possible, the defect is message quality + documented-contract mismatch (plus the -O cryptic path).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Raise ValueError at the pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered entry point when eps_cell.shape[0] != eps_cell.shape[1], with guidance to pad the uniform axis into equal segments (e.g. tile a (2,1) cell to (2,2)), and document the restriction in the eps_cell docstring.

### [P3-37] Granet2DTransverseE retains ~5 large dead operator matrices (Curl, Kzt, Ktz, G3, Meps33) after assembly, roughly doubling peak memory of a staggered solve

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/pmm/twod_staggered.py:551`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _assemble stores self.Curl (qq x 2qq), self.Kzt (qq x 2qq), self.Ktz (2qq x qq), self.G3 and self.Meps33 (qq x qq) at lines 546-553, but no downstream consumer exists: _region_modes uses only Lmat/Rmat/Et_blocks/Stt/q, and _homog_geom_cache uses Rmat/Stt/Schur/q. pmm_efficiency_2d_staggered additionally keeps TWO solver instances (sol_l line 923 and sol_h line 934) alive simultaneously through the S-matrix and far-field stages (sol_l.bx/by needed at line 953). At convergence-grade settings (e.g. Nx=Ny=4, M=16 -> q=60, 2qq=7200) each (2qq)^2 complex matrix is ~830 MB; the dead attributes plus the duplicated homogeneous solver retain several GB beyond the ~4 matrices the algorithm actually needs.

**Failure scenario.** A user raising the no-floor knob (the module's advertised purpose) to Nx=Ny=4, M=16 sees peak RSS several GB above the eig's intrinsic need and the memory is not released until the function returns; at M~20 this is the difference between fitting and OOM on a 32 GB box.

**Verification evidence.** Dead-code claim airtight: grep across the whole repo finds .Curl/.Kzt/.Ktz/.G3/.Meps33 only at their assignment lines twod_staggered.py:546-553; tests read only Lmat/Rmat (test_v5_12_0_pmm2d_staggered.py:195); the 2026-06-08 audit (docs/audits/PMM_RCWA_AUDIT_2026_06_08.md:355) already flagged self.G3 as 'built, never read... Delete' and it was never removed. Probe: monkeypatching _assemble to delattr all five gives byte-identical R/T (np.array_equal True, max|dR|=|dT|=0.0). Both solver instances (sol_l line 923, sol_h line 934) are alive through the far-field stage (sol_l.bx/by used at line 953; sol_h held by the local). Sizing at Nx=Ny=4, M=16 (q=60, qq=3600): dead attrs = 3x (qq x 2qq) + 2x (qq x qq) = 1.66 GB per solver, 3.32 GB across the two instances; consumed attrs (Lmat/Rmat/Stt/Schur/Et_blocks) = 3.73 GB per solver. QUANTIFIER CORRECTION: dead/needed is ~44% per solver, not 'roughly doubling' — and peak RSS also includes the ~multi-GB LAPACK ggev workspace, further diluting the ratio; 'several GB retained' is accurate, '~2x peak' is not.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Grepped repo-wide (source + tests + docs) for any reader of the five attributes — none; checked whether tests/notebook-style validation introspect them (tests use only Lmat/-Rmat and the module functions); verified deletion cannot change results by running the full solve monkeypatched (byte-identical); recomputed the memory arithmetic independently to check the 'doubling' claim (it overstates: +44% of retained operator memory per solver).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Stop storing Curl/Kzt/Ktz/G3/Meps33 as attributes (keep them as _assemble locals), and drop the sol_h reference (only the geom tuple is needed) once _homog_geom_cache has been called.

### [P3-38] Dispersive (callable) eps_tensor_cell layers re-impose the in-plane-only contract that static tensor layers no longer have, and skip the |e_zz|>0 guard

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/rcwa/stack.py:1379`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** add_layer (line ~1002) accepts a callable eps_tensor_cell with NO validation and its docstring advertises both dispersive tensor layers and out-of-plane tensor support (v5.14.1). But _materialized_layers calls _require_inplane_tensor('solve_vs_wavelength', tcell) on every materialised tensor (line 1379), so a dispersive out-of-plane tensor stack ALWAYS fails at sweep time with the pre-v5.14.1 'in-plane subset' error, even though the identical static tensor solves fine via the generalized cascade. It also never applies the min|e_zz|>1e-12 guard that add_layer enforces for static OOP cells, so if the in-plane check were relaxed the divide-by-ezz guard would be missing too.

**Failure scenario.** s.add_layer(0.2e-6, eps_tensor_cell=lambda wl: tilted_uniaxial(wl)) with exz/ezx != 0; s.solve_vs_wavelength([...]) raises ValueError 'the anisotropic path is the z-decoupled in-plane tensor subset...' while s2.add_layer(0.2e-6, eps_tensor_cell=tilted_uniaxial(0.633e-6)); s2.solve() succeeds (probe confirmed both behaviors). The error is deferred to sweep time and misattributes the limitation.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace: add_layer (stack.py:1001-1005) appends a callable eps_tensor_cell with zero validation; the static branch (stack.py:1006-1022) allows out-of-plane tensors with a min|e_zz|>1e-12 guard; _materialized_layers (stack.py:1376-1380) calls _require_inplane_tensor('solve_vs_wavelength', tcell) with default allow_offplane=False on every materialised dispersive tensor and applies NO ezz guard. Probe (C:/tmp/lum_audit_probes/verify_f2_dispersive_oop.py): identical 30-deg tilted-uniaxial (exz=ezx!=0) tensor cell -- STATIC solve() succeeds (Rsum=0.03389, Tsum=0.96611, sum=1.0 to machine precision); the CALLABLE version is accepted at add_layer then solve_vs_wavelength raises ValueError 'the anisotropic path is the z-decoupled in-plane tensor subset...' at sweep time. A dispersive ezz=0 OOP cell is also accepted at add_layer (the static equivalent raises the |e_zz|>0 guard immediately) and currently only dies on the in-plane check, so the missing ezz guard is latent. _require_inplane_tensor's own docstring (_core.py:2065-2066, 'stack out-of-plane pending') is stale post-v5.14.1.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched CHANGELOG.md and docs/ for any documented restriction that dispersive tensor layers are in-plane-only: the only hit (CHANGELOG line 421) is about the symmetry fast-path fallback, not the sweep; add_layer's docstring advertises callable eps_tensor_cell (GAP5) and OOP support (v5.14.1) with no exclusion. Checked for another dispersive-tensor path that could work: plain solve() raises for any dispersive stack, so solve_vs_wavelength is the ONLY route and it always rejects OOP -- the advertised feature combination is entirely unavailable. Verified the static path really does support OOP (probe: energy-conserving solve). No guard elsewhere; no test covers dispersive OOP tensors.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** In _materialized_layers mirror the static add_layer branch (allow out-of-plane tensors and enforce the min|e_zz|>1e-12 guard on the materialised cell), and update the stale 'stack out-of-plane pending' note in _require_inplane_tensor's docstring.

### [P3-39] line_interface horizontal liner silently no-ops when the carved-side band is thinner than the liner thickness t

- **Location:** `lumenairy/elements/segment_geometry.py:334`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The horizontal-interface carve (lines 332-340) only fires when the post-split band thickness equals t exactly (`abs((z1 - zb[i][0]) - t) < 1e-15`). _hz_split_planes inserts the z1-t / z1+t split plane only when it falls strictly inside a band; if the band on the carved side of an a|b horizontal interface is thinner than t, no split occurs, the equality check fails, and the liner is silently omitted at that interface -- no error, no warning. The vertical-wall liner at the same interface set IS inserted, so the output geometry is an inconsistent partial liner.

**Failure scenario.** A 1 nm Ta liner (the module docstring's motivating use case) carved from side 'a' where the 'a' film cap is only 0.8 nm thick at some horizontal a|b contact: to_pmm_stack/to_rcwa_stack receive a geometry with the liner present on sidewalls but absent on that horizontal interface; solver R/T silently reflect the wrong device.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_line_interface_thin.py) on repo code: g.add_band(0.8e-9,[(1.0,'Cu')]); g.add_band(10e-9,[(1.0,'SiO2')]); g.line_interface('Cu','SiO2',t=1e-9,mat='Ta',side='a') -> bands [(8.000e-10,'Cu'),(1.000e-08,'SiO2')], Ta present: False (silent no-op, no warning). Control with 5 nm Cu -> [(4e-9,'Cu'),(1e-9,'Ta'),(1e-8,'SiO2')], Ta present: True. Mixed probe (0.8 nm band with a vertical Cu|SiO2 wall above a full-width SiO2 band): vertical Ta strips inserted at (0,1e-9) and (4.99e-7,5e-7) but NO Ta at the horizontal Cu-on-SiO2 interface -> the inconsistent partial-liner state exactly as claimed. Trace: _hz_split_planes appends z1-t = -0.2e-9 which _split_z filters (p > 1e-15), then the carve gate at line 332 `abs((z1 - zb[i][0])) >= t - 1e-15` fails (band 0.8e-9 < t) with no else/warning branch, while the vertical insert path (lines 279-295) has no thickness gate.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for guards and callers: line_interface is a public builder with no thickness validation; only test coverage is tests/unit/test_v5_14_1_device_geometry.py::test_geometry_liners which uses band=0.2/t=0.02 and 0.2/0.05 (band >> t), so the thin-band path is untested; docstring and device-geometry roadmap/audit docs do not document the limitation as intended behavior. Ran the exact repro plus a mixed vertical+horizontal case to check whether the vertical path also no-ops (it does not — partial liner confirmed).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** In the horizontal-interface carve, clamp the carved depth to min(band_thickness, t) (splitting only when band > t) or raise/warn when the carved-side band is thinner than t, instead of the silent exact-equality gate at lines 332-340.

### [P3-40] glass.py module caches (_glass_value_cache, _glass_cache, _validity_warned, _kappa_warned) are unbounded and not enrolled in _cache_registry, so clear_asm_caches / lumenairy_context(clear_caches_on_exit=True) never drains them

- **Location:** `lumenairy/glass.py:1220`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _cache_registry.py exists precisely to end the 'fix N, miss N+1' cache-clear gap, and every other cache-owning module calls register_cache_clearer at import (grep shows 14 registration sites); glass.py registers none of its four module-level caches. _glass_value_cache (glass.py:1220) is keyed on (glass_name, round(wavelength*1e12)) with no size cap, so a dense dispersive wavelength sweep inserts one dict entry per distinct femtometre-rounded wavelength per glass; _validity_warned (line 928) and _kappa_warned (line 1526) similarly grow one entry per (glass, 0.1 nm-rounded wavelength) pair; _glass_cache (line 1210) accumulates RefractiveIndexMaterial objects and the per-traced-element '__aspheric_{id}' _FixedIndex entries that trace.py:1314 adds and never removes. None are reachable from clear_all_registered_caches, so the documented full-drain paths (lumenairy_context(clear_caches_on_exit=True), clear_asm_caches) leave them resident.

**Failure scenario.** A long-running notebook/optimizer sweeping wavelength continuously (e.g. 1e6 distinct wavelengths across 20 glasses) grows _glass_value_cache + the two warn-sets to tens of millions of small tuple/float entries (order-GB of dict overhead) with no way to release them short of touching private module state; a user who calls la.clear_asm_caches() expecting the library's caches to drain (its documented purpose) observes no reduction.

**Verification evidence.** Probed: glass.py contains no register_cache_clearer call (14 registration sites elsewhere in the package, none in glass.py); la.list_registered_cache_clearers() = ['asm_local','lg_mode_stack','lg_polynomial_items','phase_retrieval_kernels','pmm_lagrange_dref','propagate_system_jax','rcwa_homogeneous_modes','through_focus_scan_jax','trace_jax','wrapper_merit_meshgrid','zernike_basis'] — no glass entry, and clear_asm_caches (fft_infra.py:1236) is a pure registry walk. Live probe: 1000 out-of-validity lookups -> len(_validity_warned)=1000, unchanged (1000) after la.clear_asm_caches(); N-K5 (__sellmeier__ sentinel) sweep grew _glass_value_cache to 101 entries, unchanged after clear_asm_caches(). trace.py:1303-1314 confirmed to add '__aspheric_{id(elem)}' _FixedIndex entries to _glass_cache/GLASS_REGISTRY with no removal path. TWO CLAIMED DETAILS ARE WRONG, capping impact: (1) the cache key is round(wavelength*1e12) = PICOMETRE resolution, not femtometre (100,000 steps of 1e-15 m produced 101 entries, not 100,000 — the finding's repro output claim of 'both prints show 100000' is off ~1000x; the in-code 'femtometre' comment at glass.py:1214/1225 is itself wrong); (2) mainstream catalogue glasses like N-BK7 dispatch to the refractiveindex-package branch and never touch _glass_value_cache (probe: 100k N-BK7 lookups -> cache len 0); only the 28 '__sellmeier__' sentinel glasses (plus the no-refractiveindex fallback) use it. 100k entries cost only ~4.7 kB of dict slots + ~100 B/entry of key/value objects, so GB-scale growth needs ~10^7+ distinct pm-quantized wavelengths on sentinel glasses — implausible in practice.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for glass-cache registration anywhere else (grep register_cache_clearer across package — none for glass); checked whether clear_asm_caches has a legacy direct fan-out that touches glass (it doesn't — single _clear_all_registered_caches() call, and its docstring cache list omits glass); checked the meta-pin test test_v4_16_0_agent_d_cache_registry.py for a documented intentional exemption of glass caches (the pinned clearer set simply omits glass, no exemption comment); checked whether register_fixed_glass or any glass.py function clears the warn-sets (nothing clears _validity_warned/_kappa_warned); tried to refute the growth claim by running the finding's own repro — this partially succeeded: N-BK7 never populates _glass_value_cache and the femtometre-resolution keying claim is wrong (picometre), so the claimed 100000-entry repro does not reproduce, though the undrained/unregistered core claim does.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Register one 'glass' clearer at glass.py import time that empties _glass_cache (preserving the '__thin_lens__' fixed entry or re-registering it), _glass_value_cache, _validity_warned and _kappa_warned; optionally cap _glass_value_cache and fix the 'femtometre' comment to picometre.

### [P3-41] Malformed/truncated .zmx lines crash with raw IndexError/ValueError instead of a clear parse error

- **Location:** `lumenairy/io/prescriptions_zemax.py:174`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Keyword handlers index tokens without length checks: 'CURV' with no value -> IndexError at line 174 (float(tokens[1])); same pattern for CONI (177), DISZ (180), DIAM (200), and PARM (lines 203-204, float(tokens[2])). A truncated download or a hand-edited file produces a bare 'IndexError: list index out of range' with no filename/line context, violating the parser-robustness contract (the encoding fallback at line 106 shows the module otherwise aims for actionable errors).

**Failure scenario.** A .zmx truncated mid-line at '  CURV' (e.g., partial file copy over the mesh share) raises IndexError from deep inside the token loop; the user cannot tell whether the file is corrupt, mis-encoded, or the loader is buggy. Probe-confirmed: load on 'SURF 0\n TYPE STANDARD\n CURV\n' -> IndexError('list index out of range').

**Verification evidence.** Code trace: lumenairy/io/prescriptions_zemax.py line 174 `current_surf['curvature'] = float(tokens[1])` with no length guard; same unguarded tokens[1]/tokens[2] at CONI (177), DISZ (180), DIAM (200), PARM (203-204). Only MIRR (193-197) wraps in try/except (ValueError, IndexError). Probe (py -P C:/tmp/verify_zmx_f1_f3.py) on a file containing bare '  CURV' -> `IndexError: list index out of range`; on '  PARM 2' with missing value -> same bare IndexError. No filename or line number in the message. The encoding loop at lines 97-106 does raise an actionable IOError, so the module does aim for clear errors elsewhere; nothing upstream (lumenairy callers of load_zemax_zmx: __init__.py re-exports, raytrace/world.py, prescriptions_transforms.py) catches or rewraps it. Note the second loader sharing the token loop pattern (lines ~845+) has the same exposure.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a try/except around the per-line dispatch or in callers (grepped all load_zemax_zmx call sites in the package) — none rewrap. Checked whether the MIRR-style try/except was applied to other keywords — it was not. Checked docstring for a 'file must be well-formed' contract — absent. Ran the probe to confirm it is not masked by the encoding-fallback loop (it is not; the loop only guards decode errors).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Wrap the per-line keyword dispatch in try/except (IndexError, ValueError) and re-raise ValueError with filepath, line number, and the offending line text (apply to both loader copies).

### [P3-42] Auto-detect s_last = last_active_surf + 1 appends the surface after a terminal mirror (often the image plane) as a bogus refractive element

- **Location:** `lumenairy/io/prescriptions_zemax.py:241`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The +1 in auto-detection exists to capture the glass exit surface, but when the last active surface is a MIRROR there is no exit surface: the loader pulls in the next surface (frequently IMA or a dummy) as an air-air 'surface' element. Its DIAM then participates in the aperture computation (line 473) when no STOP is inside the range, and it adds a spurious element/thickness to 'elements'/'all_thicknesses'.

**Failure scenario.** Probe-confirmed: a single-mirror .zmx whose image plane has DIAM 55 loads with elements=[('mirror',1,0.005),('surface',2,0.055)] — a fake 110 mm flat lens surface. Without a STOP flag in range, aperture_diameter would be 0.11 m instead of 0.01 m, and codegen/system replay emits an extra do-nothing surface with a wrong clear aperture.

**Verification evidence.** Line 241 `s_last = active[-1]['surf_num'] + 1` applies the +1 unconditionally (same pattern duplicated at line 857 in the second loader). Probe: 3-surface .zmx with SURF 1 GLAS MIRROR DIAM 5, SURF 2 (image plane) DIAM 55, loaded with surface_range=None -> elements=[('mirror', 1, 0.005), ('surface', 2, 0.055)] and aperture_diameter=0.11 (11x the mirror's 0.01 m clear aperture), plus all_thicknesses=[-0.1] carrying the extra gap. No STOP in range, so line 473's max-semi-diameter fallback picks the fake surface. The docstring (line 51-52) even promises only 'all surfaces between the first and last glass surfaces', which the +1 already exceeds for mirrors. No test in tests/ contains 'GLAS MIRROR' at all, so the terminal-mirror auto-detect path is untested and the current behavior is not pinned as intended.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a mirror-specific guard on the +1 (none; the +1 is correct only for the refractive exit-surface case). Searched tests for any .zmx fixture with a mirror to see if this behavior is blessed — zero matches for 'GLAS MIRROR'. Considered mitigations: a STOP flag inside the range fixes the aperture (line 470-471) but the bogus 'surface' element still lands in elements/all_thicknesses; surface_range is an explicit workaround, and docstring/raytrace.world steer folded-system users toward manual handling — these keep it at P3 rather than P2, but do not refute the concrete failure path, which the probe reproduced exactly as claimed.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Only extend s_last by +1 when active[-1] has non-mirror glass (a refractive entry surface needing its exit surface); when the last active surface is a mirror, set s_last = active[-1]['surf_num'] — apply to both loader copies (lines 241 and 857).

### [P3-43] load_zemax_prescription_data_txt hardwires aspheric_coeffs=None and silently drops all aspheric/freeform data while its docstring claims interchangeability with load_zemax_zmx

- **Location:** `lumenairy/io/prescriptions_zemax.py:926`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The .txt loader captures the Type column ('type': type_str, line 829) but never consumes it: every element is emitted with 'aspheric_coeffs': None (lines 909 and 926) and no EVENASPH/Q-type handling, and no warning is raised when the Type column says EVENASPH/TOROIDAL. The docstring (line ~617) says it 'produces the same output structure as load_zemax_zmx so the two loaders are interchangeable' — for an aspheric design they are not: the txt path silently degrades every asphere to a conic, exactly the class of wavefront corruption the v5.16.1 EVENASPH fix was about.

**Failure scenario.** User exports Prescription Data from OpticStudio for the poc1-19-style design (EVENASPH S9/S38) and loads it via load_zemax_prescription_data_txt because they only have the report, trusting the 'interchangeable' claim; the sub-micron aspheric departure vanishes silently and the traced-lens spot/defocus disagrees with Zemax with no hint why.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_zemax_f3.py): synthetic SURFACE DATA SUMMARY txt with a row 'Type=EVENASPH' loads with elements[0]['aspheric_coeffs'] = None and surfaces[0]['aspheric_coeffs'] = None; recorded warnings list EMPTY. Code trace: type_str captured at line 829 but never consumed; literal 'aspheric_coeffs': None at lines 909 and 926; docstring at lines 617-618 states the parser 'produces the same output structure as load_zemax_zmx so the two loaders are interchangeable'.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the summary-table format even carries coefficients: it does not (columns are Surf/Type/Radius/Thickness/Glass/diams/Conic/Comment), and the SURFACE DATA DETAIL section that would carry EVENASPH parameters is explicitly used as an end-marker and discarded (lines 732-740) — so the loader cannot recover coefficients from what it parses, partially excusing the None. But no warning is emitted for a non-STANDARD Type and the interchangeability docstring claim is factually wrong for aspheric designs, so the finding stands as a documentation+warning gap.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** warnings.warn when a parsed row's Type is not STANDARD/COORDBRK (naming the surface and stating that aspheric/freeform data is dropped) and amend the docstring's interchangeability claim; optionally parse the SURFACE DATA DETAIL section for EVENASPH parameters.

### [P3-44] Stale pre-v5.16.1 comment in export_zemax_zmx ('Zemax PARM index: a_4 -> 1, a_6 -> 2') contradicts the correct code (parm_idx = power // 2 gives a_4 -> PARM 2)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/io/prescriptions_zemax.py:1597`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The code at line 1598 (parm_idx = power // 2) is the correct inverse of the v5.16.1 loader mapping power = 2*parm_num, but the comment directly above it (line 1597) still documents the pre-fix off-by-one convention 'a_4 -> 1, a_6 -> 2, a_8 -> 3'. The mirror-writer copy in _export_zemax_zmx_full (lines 1406-1411) has no comment at all. A future maintainer 'fixing' the code to match the comment would reintroduce the exact off-by-one + 1e6 unit-inflation bug the CHANGELOG 5.16.1 entry root-caused (loader NOTE at line 404 even warns the exporter must mirror parm_idx = power//2).

**Failure scenario.** Maintainer reads line 1597, concludes parm_idx = power//2 - 1 is intended, changes it; round-trip test test_evenasph_full_round_trip_preserves_alpha4 would catch it, but only if run — and the loader-side unit test pins the loader alone, so an exporter-only regression shipped between test runs re-shifts every exported asphere one even power.

**Verification evidence.** Line 1597 comment reads 'Zemax PARM index: a_4 -> 1, a_6 -> 2, a_8 -> 3, ...' directly above line 1598 `parm_idx = power // 2`, which maps a_4 (power 4) -> PARM 2. Loader (lines 386-411) documents the corrected convention `power = 2*parm_num` with the explicit NOTE at line 404: 'export_zemax_zmx must mirror this (parm_idx = power//2)'. CHANGELOG lines 149-156 confirm 5.16.1 corrected loader+exporter to power = 2*parm_num, so the comment is the pre-fix convention left stale. The mirror-writer copy in _export_zemax_zmx_full (lines 1406-1411) has the same correct code with no comment at all. Mitigation confirmed: round-trip tests exist — tests/unit/test_audit_io.py:196 test_evenasph_full_round_trip_preserves_alpha4 and tests/unit/test_audit_misc.py:638 test_evenasph_export_then_load_preserves_coeffs — which would catch a 'fix-to-match-comment' regression when the suite runs.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the comment could be correct under any Zemax indexing: Zemax EVENASPH PARM 1 is the r^2 coefficient, so a_4 is PARM 2 — the comment is unambiguously wrong. Verified against the loader mapping and the 5.16.1 CHANGELOG entry. Checked that existing round-trip tests pin the exporter (they do, weakening the failure scenario to a doc-only issue), consistent with the P3 grade.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** One-line comment fix: change line 1597 to 'a_4 -> PARM 2, a_6 -> PARM 3, ... (PARM n = coefficient on r^(2n))' and add the same note above the copy at lines 1406-1411.

### [P3-45] Guardrail claw-back label 'use complex64 fields' hides that its quoted peak assumes parallel_amp=False

- **Location:** `lumenairy/memory.py:802`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The complex64 rung (lines 801-803) re-estimates with complex_dtype='complex64' AND parallel_amp=False, but its label says only 'use complex64 fields'. The neighboring rungs (lines 796-798, 805, 810) explicitly append '(+parallel_amp=False)' to their labels, showing the disclosure was intended and this rung simply omits it.

**Failure scenario.** Whole-grid c128 run refused with message 'Claw-backs that fit: use complex64 fields -> ~X GB'. User re-launches with complex64 only, keeping the default parallel_amp=True; actual whole-grid peak is ~1.8x the promised X (probe-measured ratio 1.80 at N=16384) -> OOM despite following the recommendation verbatim.

**Verification evidence.** memory.py:802-804: `_try('use complex64 fields', complex_dtype='complex64', parallel_amp=False)` — label omits parallel_amp while neighbors at 797-799, 805-807, 811-812 explicitly disclose '(+parallel_amp=False)' or '(byte-identical)'. Probe: N=16384 c128 traced whole-grid (sag_chunk_rows=0), available=40 GB -> message includes 'use complex64 fields -> ~34 GB'; following it verbatim (complex64, default parallel_amp=True, still whole-grid) estimates 51.52 GB peak -> does NOT fit 40 GB. Measured parallel-on/off ratio for whole-grid c64 N=16384 is 1.505 (claimed 1.80 not reproduced); ratio is 1.000 in the default chunked lens path, so the trap only bites when the caller explicitly forced sag_chunk_rows=0.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Tried to show parallel_amp is irrelevant to the c64 estimate: true in the default chunked path (ratio 1.000), but false in the whole-grid path the scenario cites (ratio 1.505, 51.52 vs 34.24 GB). Tried to show the recommendation wouldn't surface in a realistic budget window: at 40 GB it does surface and the verbatim follow-through fails. Checked for intent: neighboring rungs deliberately disclose the parallel_amp override, so the omission reads as an oversight, not a design choice. Claimed 1.80 ratio did not reproduce (got 1.505 whole-grid, 1.000 chunked) — substance confirmed, magnitude overstated.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Rename the rung label to 'use complex64 fields (+parallel_amp=False)' to match its actual estimate assumptions.
