### [P3-46] set_low_memory captures prior['fft_auto_promote'] as a hardcoded True instead of reading get_fft_auto_promote(), and set_low_memory(False) clobbers user customizations to shipped defaults

- **Location:** `lumenairy/memory.py:894`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Line 894 sets prior['fft_auto_promote'] = True with a '# library default' comment, although get_fft_auto_promote() exists (propagators/fft_infra.py:1495) and the other three knobs ARE captured via their getters. Additionally the enabled=False branch (lines 916-919) hard-restores shipped defaults (8/True/True/True) rather than the captured prior, so a user's pre-existing non-default configuration is destroyed by an enable/disable round-trip. The unit test (test_set_low_memory_roundtrip_and_byte_safe_defaults) only exercises the shipped-default baseline, so it cannot discriminate restore-to-prior from restore-to-shipped.

**Failure scenario.** User had set_fft_auto_promote(False) (e.g. for byte-reproducibility per the v5.17.0 chunked-lens test gotcha) and set_fft_plan_cache_size(16). set_low_memory(True) returns prior claiming auto_promote was True; set_low_memory(False) leaves auto_promote=True and plan_cache_size=8 -- reproducibility pin silently lost and cache resized.

**Verification evidence.** memory.py:895 is literally `prior['fft_auto_promote'] = True   # library default` while the other three knobs use _capture(getter); get_fft_auto_promote() exists (propagators/fft_infra.py:1495, setter at 1461). enabled=False branch (lines 916-920) hard-sets 8/True/True/True. Probe (py -P, repo root): set_fft_auto_promote(False); set_fft_plan_cache_size(16); prior=set_low_memory(True) -> prior=={'plan_cache_size': 16, 'lens_parallel_amp': True, 'fft_double_buffer': True, 'fft_auto_promote': True} (auto_promote WRONG, actual was False); set_low_memory(False) -> get_fft_auto_promote()==True, get_fft_plan_cache_size()==8 (user's False and 16 both lost). Docstring line 858-859 promises 'round-trips via set_low_memory(False)', contradicting the 'Restore shipped library defaults' behavior.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether get_fft_auto_promote() is missing (would justify the hardcode) — it exists at fft_infra.py:1495. Checked whether restore-to-shipped is documented intent — the docstring explicitly says the return value lets the change 'round-trip via set_low_memory(False)', so no. Checked test coverage: tests/unit/test_memory_guardrail.py:135 test_set_low_memory_roundtrip_and_byte_safe_defaults asserts baseline==shipped defaults (cache 8, parallel_amp True) before/after, so it cannot distinguish restore-to-prior from restore-to-shipped, and never checks auto_promote or double_buffer. Ran the probe — reproduced exactly as claimed.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Capture prior['fft_auto_promote'] via get_fft_auto_promote() like the other knobs, and make set_low_memory(False) restore the captured prior (e.g., stash it module-level or accept the prior dict) instead of hard-coding shipped defaults.

### [P3-47] Custom FD gradient paths (jac='auto'+JAX, method='newton') step outside the bounds box when a parameter sits exactly on a bound

- **Location:** `lumenairy/optimize/driver.py:851`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  |  *(finder graded P2, re-graded P3)*

**Claim.** _merit_jac_auto -> _fd_grad_for(..., scheme='forward') (driver.py:851-856) and the Newton Hessian path (driver.py:1133-1166) call _fd_grad_pure, which perturbs x[i] by +step (forward) or +/-step (central) with NO clipping to parameterization.bounds. scipy's internal 2-point numdiff clips FD steps to the bounds; the library's own FD helpers do not. When L-BFGS-B pins a parameter at its bound (extremely common at convergence), the merit is evaluated at an infeasible x — e.g. a glass thickness below its physical minimum or a radius crossing zero — where the ray/wave leg throws and gets replaced by a sentinel penalty, producing a huge spurious gradient component that stalls or misdirects the line search.

**Failure scenario.** design_optimize(jac='auto') with one JaxMeritTerm(build_args=...) plus any non-JAX merit, bounds=[(0.0, ub)] on a thickness, optimizer drives the thickness to 0.0 exactly: for the upper-bound case forward FD evaluates at ub+h (infeasible); for a lower-bound-pinned variable central FD in the Newton path evaluates at lb-h -> apply_real_lens/system_abcd raise -> 1e9 sentinel -> gradient component ~1e9/h dominates the whole Jacobian; the L-BFGS-B/trust-ncg step is corrupted at the very iterate that matters (convergence at the bound).

**Verification evidence.** Code trace airtight: _fd_grad_pure (driver.py:294-299 forward, 270-277 central) has no bounds parameter at all; _fd_grad_for (778-827) and _merit_jac_auto (829-856, forward-FD) never consult parameterization.bounds; the Newton Hessian (1158-1166) perturbs x[i]+/-1e-4-scale steps unclipped. Probe: `_fd_grad_pure(f, [1.0], scheme='forward')` at ub=1.0 evaluated f at x=1.0000001 (outside the box), while raw scipy L-BFGS-B with internal FD and bounds=[(0,1)] never evaluated above 1.0 (max eval exactly 1.0, assertion trap never fired). End-to-end probe with JaxMeritTerm(build_args)+CallableMerit, jac='auto', L-BFGS-B, bounds [(0,1)], objective pulling x to ub=1.0, other-merit returning a 1e9 sentinel for x>1: 20 merit evaluations at x>ub (e.g. 1.0000000303) and 191 total evals vs 11 in the smooth control — a 17x eval blowup from the spurious ~1e16 gradient component at the bound. HOWEVER the claimed convergence corruption did NOT reproduce: x_opt=1.0000000000 (exactly correct) in both runs.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether scipy re-clips a user-supplied jac's internals (it cannot — jac is opaque to scipy); checked whether the pure-FD default path is affected (it is NOT: with final_jac=None scipy's own 2-point numdiff runs and it DOES clip to bounds, so only jac='auto'-with-JAX-terms and method='newton' use the unclipped library FD); noted the Newton-path half of the claim is weaker than stated because trust-ncg receives no bounds at all (driver.py:1174-1179), so the optimizer never 'pins a variable at its bound' there — only a user x0 placed exactly on a physical wall triggers it; tried to reproduce the claimed stall/misdirection — got a 17x evaluation-count blowup but the correct final answer, and note the forward scheme at a LOWER bound steps inward (safe), so the catastrophic direction needs an upper bound with a hard discontinuity immediately above it, plus a merit that degenerates within a ~1e-7-relative step of the bound.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Clip FD steps in _fd_grad_for/_fd_grad_pure/_hess_for_newton to parameterization.bounds (switch to one-sided differences pointing into the box at an active bound, as scipy's _numdiff does).

### [P3-48] MaxFNumberMerit crashes with TypeError when prescription['aperture_diameter'] is None

- **Location:** `lumenairy/optimize/merit_terms.py:1338`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Line 1337 uses dict.get with a default (`ctx.prescription.get('aperture_diameter', 1e-3)`), which returns None when the key EXISTS with value None — the exact shape RawParameterization.build emits ({'_raw_params': x, 'aperture_diameter': None}, parameterizations.py:252-253) and a legal user prescription state that every sibling site handles (driver.py:750 uses `or`, MultiFieldMerit line 722 uses truthiness). `None > 0` then raises TypeError, which is NOT in design_optimize's evaluate() catch lists, so the whole optimization run aborts. Probe confirmed: evaluate raises TypeError: '>' not supported between instances of 'NoneType' and 'int'.

**Failure scenario.** A prescription with a valid EFL (so the ctx_is_valid('efl') early-return at line 1335 does not fire) and aperture_diameter explicitly None, combined with MaxFNumberMerit in the merit list: the first merit_fn call raises TypeError out of scipy.minimize, killing a possibly multi-hour design run instead of returning the ap<=0 fallback penalty one line below.

**Verification evidence.** Reproduced with probe C:/tmp/verify_maxfnum_none.py (run as `py -P` due to stray C:/tmp/inspect.py shadowing stdlib): MaxFNumberMerit(2.0).evaluate(ctx with prescription={'aperture_diameter': None}, efl=0.1) -> TypeError: '>' not supported between instances of 'NoneType' and 'int'; key-absent case returns 9604.0 (default fires); ap=0 case returns 9.99999996e+17 fallback penalty. Code trace: merit_terms.py:1337 uses .get('aperture_diameter', 1e-3) whose default does NOT fire for an existing None value; line 1338 `ap > 0` then raises. Every sibling site None-guards (merit_terms.py:261-263, 522-524, 823-825; context.py:283-285 returns inf on None; driver.py:750 uses `or`), proving None is a codebase-legal 'no aperture' state; parameterizations.py:198-201 documents the None-fallback convention. DesignParameterization.build (parameterizations.py:179-184) deep-copies a user template, so aperture_diameter=None flows through with 'surfaces' present -> ray leg runs, efl valid, line-1335 guard passes, crash at 1338. _sum_merits (driver.py:307-311) has no try/except and the driver's TypeError catches (lines 656, 674) wrap only the ABCD/Seidel legs, so the exception propagates out of merit_fn into scipy and aborts the run. No test covers this (only import references in tests/unit/test_v5_1_0_agent_e_split.py).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Checked the claim's RawParameterization pathway: it is BLOCKED — MaxFNumberMerit inherits needs_ray=True (context.py:231) and driver.py:593-602 raises a clear up-front ValueError for template-free builds lacking 'surfaces', so RawParameterization + MaxFNumberMerit never reaches evaluate (this part of the claim is wrong; note the check is skipped in MultiPrescriptionParameterization mode). (2) Searched for a None-guard upstream (driver evaluate(), EvaluationContext, _sum_merits) — none normalizes prescription aperture before merit evaluation. (3) Searched for exception handling around merit evaluation — _sum_merits and merit_fn have no catch; driver catch lists cover only ABCD/Seidel/opd legs. (4) Searched tests for MaxFNumberMerit coverage — none exercise it with None aperture. (5) Checked whether None is an illegal prescription state users shouldn't produce — refuted by five sibling None-guarding sites and the documented None convention.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace the dict.get default with a None-aware read, e.g. `ap = ctx.prescription.get('aperture_diameter') or 0.0`, so None routes to the existing ap<=0 fallback (fnum=1e9 penalty) one line below.

### [P3-49] beam_expander_prescription / keplerian_telescope docstrings claim 'wavelength : float, default 550e-9' but the parameter is keyword-only with NO default; prose also still says the solver zeros the ABCD 'B element' while the code zeros C

- **Location:** `lumenairy/optimize/multiconfig.py:316`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Both builders declare `*, wavelength: float` (lines 294-297, 374-377) — omitting wavelength raises TypeError — yet both docstrings document 'wavelength : float, default 550e-9' (lines 316-317, 393-394). Additionally the narrative in both docstrings says the function 'solves numerically for the air-gap that drives the system ABCD matrix's B element to zero' (lines 305-307, 383-386), but the implementation calls _zero_C_air_gap, whose own docstring (lines 234-244) explains at length that the afocal condition is C=0, not B=0 — the prose was never updated after the rename (the `_zero_B_air_gap` alias at line 291 memorialises the correction).

**Failure scenario.** A user following the docstring calls beam_expander_prescription(10, 0.1) expecting the documented 550 nm default and gets TypeError: missing required keyword argument 'wavelength'; a reader trusting the B-element prose mis-derives the afocal condition when writing their own variant.

**Verification evidence.** Probe: py -c calling beam_expander_prescription(10.0, 0.1) and keplerian_telescope(0.2, 0.02) both raise "TypeError: ... missing 1 required keyword-only argument: 'wavelength'", yet both docstrings (multiconfig.py lines 316-317, 393-394) say 'wavelength : float, default 550e-9'. inspect.signature confirms '*, wavelength: float' with no default on the top-level exports (same objects, no shim). Prose at lines 304-306 and 383-385 says the solver drives the 'B element' to zero, but the code calls _zero_C_air_gap (line 364) which reads system_abcd(...)[0][1,0] (the C element) and whose own docstring (lines 231-243) derives the afocal condition as C=0; line 290-291 aliases _zero_B_air_gap = _zero_C_air_gap under 'the old (misnamed) name'. _deprecation.py:281-304 shows the historical pattern (wavelength default 550e-9 deprecated v4.7, required v5.0) — the docstring text is a leftover from before removal.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched the whole package for a wrapper/deprecation shim restoring the 550e-9 default (only a docstring EXAMPLE in _deprecation.py:297; no live shim — verified top-level lumenairy exports are the identical unwrapped functions). Checked whether 'B element' could be correct under some ABCD convention (no: code indexes [1,0]=C and the file itself documents the B->C rename). Grepped all callers (tests/validation) — every one passes wavelength explicitly, so no test covers the documented default; nothing refutes the claim.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Update both docstrings: change 'wavelength : float, default 550e-9' to a required-parameter entry (moved next to the other params), and reword the 'B element to zero' prose in both functions to 'C element to zero' to match _zero_C_air_gap.

### [P3-50] DesignParameterization lacks the duplicate-free_vars guard that v4.14 (audit P3 #19) added to MultiPrescriptionParameterization

- **Location:** `lumenairy/optimize/parameterizations.py:137`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** MultiPrescriptionParameterization.__post_init__ (lines 369-387) rejects duplicate (index, *path) entries with a clear ValueError because duplicates silently over-parameterise the design (two x slots write the same prescription field; last write wins; the FD gradient is split arbitrarily). DesignParameterization.__post_init__ (lines 137-147) performs no such check — the sibling classes have drifted. Probe confirmed: DesignParameterization(tmpl, free_vars=[('thicknesses',0),('thicknesses',0)]) is accepted with n_params=2.

**Failure scenario.** User builds free_vars programmatically and accidentally lists ('surfaces', 0, 'radius') twice: build(x) writes x[0] then overwrites with x[1]; the optimizer sees two identical FD gradient columns, x[0] becomes a free-floating dead variable, and the reported optimum x is ambiguous/misleading — exactly the quietly-wrong-result mode the multi-prescription fix was written to prevent.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (repo root, py launcher): DesignParameterization({'surfaces':[{'radius':0.05}],'thicknesses':[1e-3]}, free_vars=[('thicknesses',0),('thicknesses',0)]) accepted with n_params=2; build([2e-3, 7e-3]) -> thicknesses=[0.007] (last write wins, x[0] dead); identical duplicate on MultiPrescriptionParameterization raises 'ValueError: ... duplicate (prescription_index, *path) entries in free_vars'. Code trace: DesignParameterization.__post_init__ (lumenairy/optimize/parameterizations.py:137-147) only checks bounds length and resolves scale_floor — no dedup; MultiPrescriptionParameterization carries the explicit v4.14 guard at lines 362-387 whose comment states the identical quietly-wrong-optimisation rationale.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a guard elsewhere: grep 'duplicate' across lumenairy/optimize hits only the MultiPrescription block; grep 'free_vars' in core.py/multi_objective.py shows only docstring examples and pass-through, no caller-side validation before build(). Checked tests: tests/unit/test_audit_misc.py:5062 covers only MultiPrescriptionParameterization; nothing asserts DesignParameterization's silent accept is intended, and no documentation says duplicates are allowed. Ran the minimal probe; the claimed behaviour reproduced exactly.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Add the same normalized-tuple seen-keys duplicate check to DesignParameterization.__post_init__, ideally factored into a helper shared by both classes.

### [P3-51] apply_fresnel_curvature silently promotes complex64 -> complex128, contradicting its own docstring and its R=0 branch

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/asm.py:505`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Line 505 returns E * np.exp(sign*1j*k*r2/(2R)) where r2 is float64, so the phase factor is complex128 and a complex64 E is promoted to complex128. The docstring (line 476-478) promises 'Same shape and dtype as E', and the R==0/non-finite short-circuit at line 491 returns E.copy() which DOES preserve dtype -- so the function's output dtype depends on R. This is exactly the c64->c128 dtype-follows-input violation class the v5.17.1 tilted-ASM fix (71f9715) closed elsewhere; it also doubles the working memory of the OPDPy/Zemax convention-conversion path this utility exists for.

**Failure scenario.** E = np.ones((64,64), np.complex64); apply_fresnel_curvature(E, 1e-6, 1.31e-6, R=0.05).dtype == complex128 while R=0.0 gives complex64 (probe-confirmed). A c64 pipeline that round-trips OPDPy comparisons through this helper silently becomes c128 (2x memory, and downstream H-cache entries get keyed under the wrong dtype family).

**Verification evidence.** Probe: E=np.ones((64,64),np.complex64); apply_fresnel_curvature(E,1e-6,1.31e-6,R=0.05).dtype == complex128 while R=0.0 returns complex64 (E.copy() at asm.py:491). Code trace: asm.py:505 multiplies E by np.exp(sign*1j*k*r2/(2R)) where r2 is float64 meshgrid product, so the phase factor is complex128 and numpy promotes. Docstring at asm.py:476-478 promises 'Same shape and dtype as E'. Output dtype therefore depends on the value of R.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked the R==0/non-finite short-circuit (preserves dtype, proving the inconsistency rather than refuting it). Searched for callers that pre-cast or post-cast (none in-library; the function is a leaf conversion utility). Checked whether the docstring might mean only 'complex' generically — it explicitly says 'Same shape and dtype as E'. complex128 input is unaffected (probe: c128 in -> c128 out), so impact is limited to c64 pipelines: dtype/memory consistency, no numerical wrongness — P3 stands.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Compute the phase in float64 then cast the phase factor to E's complex dtype before the multiply (mirroring the v5.17.1 tilted-ASM carrier recipe), keeping the complex128 path bit-identical.

### [P3-52] x64 policy drift across JAX twins: fit_canonical_polynomials_jax mutates the global jax_enable_x64 mid-call (the exact pattern rcwa's _require_jax_x64 docstring rejects as unsafe), while the other asymptotic twins have no x64 handling at all

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/asymptotic_jax_twin.py:788`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Every PMM/RCWA/Berreman twin entry raises via _require_jax_x64, whose own docstring (rcwa/_core.py:326-329) states that jax.config.update mid-call 'is unsafe mid-trace when the caller jits the whole solve'. fit_canonical_polynomials_jax (line 772-788) does precisely that global mutation with only a RuntimeWarning, silently changing dtype behavior of everything else in the process for the rest of the session (including user code that intentionally runs float32). Meanwhile aberration_tensor_lg00_jax, propagate_modal_asymptotic_lg00_jax and solve_envelope_stationary_jax_ift check nothing: with x64 disabled they run the M-matrix inv/det/exp chain in complex64 and the Newton-IFT 2x2 solves in float32 with no warning, in a module whose own comment says single precision gives ~5% coefficient error and NaN gradients.

**Failure scenario.** A user runs fit_canonical_polynomials_jax inside jax.jit with x64 off: the global config flips mid-trace (JAX documents this as undefined behavior; at best it silently re-traces everything). Separately, calling aberration_tensor_lg00_jax with x64 off returns a complex64 Strehl coefficient with degraded precision and no diagnostic, unlike every element-module twin which raises.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/probes/probe_asym_jax_x64.py, x64 OFF): aberration_tensor_lg00_jax returned dtype=complex64, warnings emitted: [], jax_enable_x64 still False after call; with x64 ON returned complex128; relative difference 2.532e-06 on a toy order-2 fit. Grep confirms aberration_tensor_lg00_jax (line 284), propagate_modal_asymptotic_lg00_jax (416), and solve_envelope_stationary_jax_ift (650) have zero x64 handling. asymptotic_jax_twin.py:788 does jax.config.update('jax_enable_x64', True) after only a RuntimeWarning, flipping the global for the rest of the session.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for guards/intent: (a) the auto-promote is a deliberate, commented design choice ('3.5.6: auto-enable ... rather than raising', lines 773-780) and matches the propagators-family convention — fft_infra.py:487-499/531-539 does the identical auto-promote and rcwa/_core.py:326-329 explicitly acknowledges 'the rest of the library auto-promotes'; so the elements-raise vs propagators-promote split is intentional policy, not accident. (b) The mid-trace-jit failure scenario is contrived: CanonicalPolyFit is a plain non-pytree dataclass (asymptotic_canonical_fit.py:65), so jax.jit(fit_canonical_polynomials_jax) fails at output flattening; only an outer jit that unpacks arrays hits the trace-time config flip. (c) Typical workflow self-heals: the JAX fit runs first and enables x64 session-wide before the downstream twins, so the silent-complex64 path needs a NumPy-built fit passed to the JAX evaluator (my probe's path — it does occur and is fully silent). The core factual claims (global session mutation, zero diagnostics in three twins, silent complex64) all reproduced, so CONFIRMED at the claimed P3; the '~5% error / NaN gradients' comment applies to the guarded lstsq fit, and the unguarded evaluator error measured only ~2.5e-6 on a benign fit.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Give the three unguarded asymptotic twins the same x64 policy as the rest of the propagators family (call an fft_infra-style auto-promote-with-warning helper, or at minimum warn on complex64), and note in fit_canonical_polynomials_jax's docstring that the config.update makes it unsafe to call inside jax.jit.

### [P3-53] decompose_lg/decompose_hg 2-D-meshgrid path silently returns all-zero coefficients for indexing='ij' meshgrids (dx computed as 0 along the wrong axis)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/asymptotic_modes.py:709`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** For 2-D coordinate inputs, dx = mean(diff(x, axis=1)[:,0]) and dy = mean(diff(y, axis=0)[0,:]) (lines 709-710) assume indexing='xy' meshgrids. If the caller passes X,Y from np.meshgrid(x, y, indexing='ij') (shape-compatible, passes the shape check at 706), x is constant along axis 1 so dx=0, da=abs(dx*dy)=0, and every returned overlap is exactly 0 -- with no raise or warning. The docstring only says 'typically from meshgrid' without pinning the indexing convention. The degenerate dx=0 also lands in the mode-stack cache key. decompose_hg (753-754) is identical.

**Failure scenario.** User builds X, Y = np.meshgrid(x, y, indexing='ij') (the convention used by much scientific code and by parts of this library's own FEM/PMM siblings) and calls decompose_lg(field, X, Y, w, 3, 3): returns {(p,ell): 0j for all modes} silently; downstream mode-filtering (e.g. hf.py's source_lg_amp_threshold path) then falls back to a plane-wave LG00, silently discarding the input field structure.

**Verification evidence.** asymptotic_modes.py:709-710 (and 753-754) compute dx=mean(diff(x,axis=1)[:,0]); for X,Y=np.meshgrid(x,y,indexing='ij') x is constant along axis 1 so dx=0, da=abs(dx*dy)=0, and overlaps=einsum(...)*0. Probe: decompose_lg on an 'ij' meshgrid of a unit-power LG00 returns {(p,ell): 0j} for all 6 modes; decompose_hg likewise all-zero; a rectangular 64x48 'ij' grid also passes the shape check at line 706 and returns {(0,0): 0j}. No raise, no warning.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a da/dx degenerate-value guard in both functions: none. Checked the docstring for a pinned indexing convention: only 'typically from meshgrid'. Checked whether internal callers could hit it: hf.py:470 and asymptotic_aberration_tensor.py:575 both build indexing='xy' grids, so the library itself is safe — user-facing only. Verified the trap is realistic: 25+ sites in this same library use indexing='ij' (asm.py:502, coronagraph.py:159, _lens_traced.py:542, eme_diffraction.py:80, ...), so users reasonably pass 'ij' grids.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** After computing dx/dy in the 2-D branch, raise ValueError when dx==0 or dy==0 naming the required indexing='xy' convention (or auto-detect the variation axis and handle 'ij' by transposing).

### [P3-54] snapshot_fft_state/_FFT_STATE_KEYS omits later-added setter-backed globals (_PYFFTW_DOUBLE_BUFFER, PYFFTW_FALLBACK_ON_ERROR, USE_SCIPY_FFT, plan-cache size, ASM cache bounds), so spawned workers silently lose them

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/fft_infra.py:794`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _FFT_STATE_KEYS (lines 794-798, frozen at v5.4.6) lists 8 globals, but the docstring of snapshot_fft_state promises to capture 'the FFT / precision dispatch globals this process has set (via the set_default_* / set_fft_* / set_pyfftw_* setters)'. set_fft_double_buffer (v5.16.2, _PYFFTW_DOUBLE_BUFFER), set_fft_fallback (PYFFTW_FALLBACK_ON_ERROR), set_fft_plan_cache_size (_PYFFTW_PLAN_CACHE_SIZE) and set_asm_cache_size (H-cache byte caps) are set_fft_*/cache setters whose globals are not in the tuple, so restore_fft_state in a spawn-based worker resets them to library defaults with no warning.

**Failure scenario.** A memory-tight N=32768 workflow calls set_fft_double_buffer(False) (the v5.16.2 knob added precisely to halve the 16 GiB/key aligned-workspace footprint) plus set_asm_cache_size(h_max_total_bytes=...), then farms shards to a ProcessPoolExecutor using the documented initializer=restore_fft_state recipe: each worker silently reverts to double-buffered plans and default 8 GiB H-cache budget -> ~2x resident FFT workspace per worker -> OOM on exactly the configuration the knobs were set to prevent.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/verify_fftinfra/probe_fft_state_keys.py): snapshot keys = only the 9 v5.4.6 entries (DEFAULT_*, FFTW_THREADS, SCIPY_FFT_WORKERS, USE_PYFFTW, _PYFFTW_PLAN_FLAGS, _PYFFTW_AUTO_PROMOTE). After parent set_fft_double_buffer(False)/set_fft_fallback(False)/set_fft_plan_cache_size(3)/set_asm_cache_size(h_max_total_bytes=1GiB), a real spawn ProcessPoolExecutor worker initialized with the documented initializer=restore_fft_state recipe reported _PYFFTW_DOUBLE_BUFFER=True, PYFFTW_FALLBACK_ON_ERROR=True, _PYFFTW_PLAN_CACHE_SIZE=8, _H_CACHE_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES=8589934592 (all reverted to library defaults) while control key FFTW_THREADS=2 carried correctly. Docstring at line 802 promises capture of globals set 'via the set_default_* / set_fft_* / set_pyfftw_* setters', but set_fft_double_buffer (line 710), set_fft_fallback (1412), set_fft_plan_cache_size (679) and set_asm_cache_size (1239) back globals absent from _FFT_STATE_KEYS (794-798).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched for a guard or extended key list elsewhere: restore_fft_state (830) iterates only _FFT_STATE_KEYS, so nothing else can carry the missing keys. Checked tests (tests/unit/test_v5_4_6_wave9_concurrency.py) — only pins the original 9 keys, no coverage of the newer setters. Checked __all__ comment (lines 1923-1933) for documented intent to exclude — it only explains why raw globals are out of __all__, not out of the snapshot; the snapshot docstring affirmatively promises set_fft_* coverage. One sub-claim is weak: USE_SCIPY_FFT has no setter, so its omission is arguably outside the docstring's promise; the setter-backed omissions stand regardless.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Append '_PYFFTW_DOUBLE_BUFFER', 'PYFFTW_FALLBACK_ON_ERROR', '_PYFFTW_PLAN_CACHE_SIZE', '_H_CACHE_MAX_BYTES_PER_ENTRY', '_H_CACHE_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES' (and optionally 'USE_SCIPY_FFT') to _FFT_STATE_KEYS — restore_fft_state already ignores unknown/missing keys so the change is forward/backward compatible.

### [P3-55] _clear_local_asm_caches mutates _PYFFTW_PLAN_CACHE and _PYFFTW_BAD_SHAPES under _ASM_CACHE_LOCK instead of _PYFFTW_PLAN_LOCK

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/fft_infra.py:1116`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Every other mutation of _PYFFTW_PLAN_CACHE / _PYFFTW_BAD_SHAPES (insert/evict in _get_or_make_plan lines 968-1041, blacklist add in _handle_pyfftw_failure lines 1559-1561, reset_fft_backend lines 650-652 -- the latter fixed to use the plan lock in v5.4.6 audit P3-14) is serialised by _PYFFTW_PLAN_LOCK, but the registry-driven clearer (lines 1112-1117) clears both under the unrelated _ASM_CACHE_LOCK. A clear_asm_caches() racing a concurrent _fft2 can interleave: the plan-lookup thread holds _PYFFTW_PLAN_LOCK, re-inserts or move_to_end's an entry the clearer is about to (or just did) drop, so plan entries (each pinning up to 2 full-grid aligned buffers) can survive a 'drain everything' call, and a bad-shape entry added mid-clear can be silently wiped (the inverse of the race v5.4.6 explicitly fixed in reset_fft_backend).

**Failure scenario.** Multi-threaded sweep calls clear_asm_caches() between stages to release the ~2x full-grid pyFFTW workspaces while another thread is mid-_fft2 at N=8192: the just-used 2 GiB plan entry is re-inserted after the clear and stays resident, defeating the memory release the caller asked for.

**Verification evidence.** Lock mismatch is textually airtight: lines 1112-1117 clear _PYFFTW_PLAN_CACHE/_PYFFTW_BAD_SHAPES under _ASM_CACHE_LOCK, while set_pyfftw_planner (627), reset_fft_backend (650-652, the v5.4.6 P3-14 fix), set_fft_plan_cache_size (684), _get_or_make_plan (968/1035) and _handle_pyfftw_failure (1559) all use _PYFFTW_PLAN_LOCK. Stress probe (C:/tmp/verify_fftinfra/probe_clear_race.py: 4 threads of _fft2 at 256x256 vs one thread looping _clear_local_asm_caches, sys.setswitchinterval(1e-6), 20 s) reproduced "KeyError: ('fwd', (256, 256), '<c16', 1)" propagating UNCAUGHT out of _fft2 — the clearer emptied the OrderedDict between the membership check (line 969) and the indexing (970) that _get_or_make_plan performs while holding the plan lock; KeyError is not in the _fft2 fallback except tuple (1657-1658) so PYFFTW_FALLBACK_ON_ERROR cannot rescue it. Note reset_fft_backend itself calls clear_asm_caches() (661), so the registry path re-clears the same structures under the wrong lock, partially undoing the v5.4.6 P3-14 fix.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Looked for a guard: checked whether the clearer is only reachable single-threaded (no — clear_asm_caches is public API and reset_fft_backend calls it), whether _fft2's except tuple would absorb the race (no — KeyError/LookupError not caught), and whether GIL atomicity of dict ops protects the compound check-then-index in _get_or_make_plan (no — thread switch between bytecodes reproduced the crash). Also tried to refute the claimed memory-retention scenario: that specific claim is weak — a concurrently-active FFT thread would legitimately re-insert its plan immediately after even a correctly-locked clear, so 'defeating the memory release' happens with or without the fix; the real consequence of the lock mismatch is the uncaught KeyError crash (reproduced) plus blacklist add/clear ordering ambiguity. Kept P3 (matches the audit's own grading of the sibling v5.4.6 P3-14 window), though the reproduced uncaught crash makes this borderline P2 for workflows that clear caches while worker threads are mid-FFT.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** In _clear_local_asm_caches, clear the two pyFFTW structures under _PYFFTW_PLAN_LOCK (separate acquisition alongside the _ASM_CACHE_LOCK block), matching reset_fft_backend's v5.4.6 pattern.

### [P3-56] fresnel_propagate makes an avoidable full-grid copy of E_in (astype without copy=False) plus a redundant same-dtype astype of the phase screen

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/fresnel.py:147`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** L147 'E_mod = E_in.astype(target_cdtype) * phase_in': when E_in is already complex, target_cdtype == E_in.dtype (L129-130), so astype (default copy=True) duplicates the entire input grid only to be consumed by the multiply -- one extra full-grid temporary at peak. L146 similarly does '.astype(target_cdtype)' on phase_in, which under NEP50 scalar promotion is usually already at target dtype (complex-scalar x fdtype grid), making it a second same-dtype full copy. The sibling tilted-ASM path uses the correct idiom 'E_in.astype(target_cdtype, copy=False)' (asm.py L752), so this is drift, not a convention.

**Failure scenario.** fresnel_propagate on a 16384^2 complex128 field: two avoidable ~4 GB transient allocations and copy passes in a function whose v5.17.0 changelog note (L148-152) explicitly performs lifetime hygiene (del X1/Y1/phase_in) to control peak memory at exactly this scale -- the astype copies partially undo that work.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace: fresnel.py L129-130 sets target_cdtype = E_in.dtype for complex input, so L147 'E_in.astype(target_cdtype)' is a same-dtype astype; probe confirms x.astype(same_dtype).base is None and result is not x -> fresh full copy (numpy default copy=True). L146's phase_in astype is also same-dtype: probe shows xp.exp((1j*k/(2*z))*(X1**2+Y1**2)) already yields complex64 for float32 grids (and complex128 for float64 grids), so .astype(target_cdtype) is a second full-grid copy; L165's prefactor.astype is a third. At 16384^2 complex128 each copy is ~4.3 GB transient, in a function whose L148-152 v5.17.0 comment explicitly manages peak memory via del. Sibling idiom confirmed: asm.py:752 uses astype(target_cdtype, copy=False); fraunhofer L281 guards with 'if E_in.dtype != target_cdtype'.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether the copy is required for aliasing safety: no -- the multiply at L147 allocates a fresh E_mod, and E_in is never written, so copy=False cannot corrupt caller data. Checked the real-input path: astype still needed there (dtype differs) and copy=False handles it identically, so the fix has no downside. Checked whether exp could return a dtype other than target (making the astype load-bearing): under NEP-50 the weak complex scalar times fdtype grid gives exactly the target complex dtype on both paths, probe-confirmed; on JAX the astype is likewise a no-op-dtype cast. Checked JAX semantics: jnp astype(copy=False) is also valid. No test or comment pins the copying behaviour.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Use astype(target_cdtype, copy=False) at L146, L147, and L165 (and optionally mirror fraunhofer's dtype-check idiom), matching asm.py:752.

### [P3-57] propagate_huygens_fresnel_with_opl_callable: chunk_output parameter and chunking loop are dead code -- evaluation is strictly per-pixel regardless, with 13 full-grid opl_fn evaluations per output pixel

- **Location:** `lumenairy/propagators/hf.py:250`  |  **Category:** perf  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The outer 'for start in range(0, n_out, chunk_output)' loop (line 250) immediately contains 'for k in range(start, end)' (line 252) that processes one output pixel at a time; no work is batched per chunk, so chunk_output has zero effect on behavior or speed while the signature advertises a vectorization knob. Each pixel evaluates opl_fn on the full (Ny_in, Nx_in) grid 13 times (1 phase + 12 Hessian stencil calls), i.e. O(13 * N_out^2) full-grid callable evaluations; the four Hessian cross-derivative stencils re-evaluate opl_fn at overlapping arguments that could be shared, and s2 could be batched into the callable per chunk (the stated purpose of chunk_output).

**Failure scenario.** 64x64 output grid with Van Vleck enabled = 53,248 full-grid opl_fn evaluations; a user raising chunk_output expecting the documented chunked evaluation sees identical (slow) runtime -- misleading API plus real, avoidable cost on the 'direct' HF path.

**Verification evidence.** hf.py:250-252: outer 'for start in range(0, n_out, chunk_output)' wraps scalar 'for k in range(start, end)'; start/end feed nothing else. Probe (16x16 out, 32x32 in, Van Vleck): chunk_output=1 vs 4096 gives byte-identical outputs (max|diff|=0.0), identical opl_fn call count (4352 = exactly 17 full-grid evals/pixel), and identical post-warm-up runtime (0.21-0.25 s at chunk 1/64/4096; initial 18.8s-vs-0.12s gap was first-call warm-up only). The repo itself documents this exact defect as fixed in the sibling propagate_hf_chebyshev_quadrature (asymptotic_canonical_fit.py:1052-1058: 'the chunking did nothing' pre-4.12.0) -- the v4.12.0 broadcast vectorization was never ported to the opl-callable variant. Detail corrections: 17 evals/pixel (1 phase + 16 stencil), not 13 -- a 64x64 VV run is 69,632 full-grid evals, not 53,248; and the 'overlapping stencil arguments' sub-claim is WRONG (probe: all 17 arg tuples per pixel unique). Docstring never documents chunk_output here, so only the parameter name/sibling contract advertise it.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Grepped all chunk_output uses: benchmarks/tests pass it but tests only assert equality across chunk values, which passes trivially for a no-op -- no test would catch removal. (2) Checked the sibling propagate_hf_chebyshev_quadrature for a shared vectorized helper this function might route through -- none; hf.py has its own scalar loop. (3) Checked the docstring for language declaring per-pixel evaluation intended -- silent on chunk_output. (4) Ran timing probe with warm-up and alternating chunk values to refute any hidden speed effect -- none found. (5) Tested the 'overlapping stencil args' sub-claim -- refuted that detail (17 unique tuples/pixel). One mitigating nuance found: vectorizing s2 per chunk would change the user callable's implied contract (opl_fn currently receives scalar s2x/s2y), so the fix is less free than in the polynomial-fit sibling.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Port the v4.12.0 pattern from propagate_hf_chebyshev_quadrature: make the chunk axis a true broadcast dim by passing (chunk,1,1)-shaped s2 arrays to opl_fn (documenting the required broadcast contract, with a scalar-fallback probe for non-broadcasting callables), or else remove/deprecate the chunk_output parameter.

### [P3-58] JAX intersect kills tangent rays (disc==0) that the NumPy path deliberately accepts since v5.4.6, and DOE-kick propagating boundary differs (< 1.0 vs <= 1.0)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/jax_trace.py:246`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** intersection.py v5.4.6 (audit P3-3) changed the sphere-intersection acceptance to disc >= 0 so exact-tangency rays (e.g. a chief ray on the stop edge) survive (lines 149, 207). The JAX twins still use disc_pos = disc > 0 and mark ~disc_pos as missed (jax_trace.py line 246 in _intersect_jax; line 1403 in _intersect_jax_param) -- the double-where gradient guard needs disc>0 for sqrt safety, but the MISS mask could still accept disc==0 with t=-b/2. Similarly _apply_doe_kick_jax uses propagating = sumsq < 1.0 (line 463) vs the NumPy trace loop's _evan = _sumsq > 1.0 (trace.py line 184), so a grazing order with L^2+M^2 exactly 1.0 lives in NumPy but dies in JAX.

**Failure scenario.** A ray constructed to graze a sphere exactly (disc==0) or a diffraction order landing exactly at the propagation cone: alive-count and downstream OPD/spot statistics differ between trace() and trace_jax() on otherwise-identical inputs, breaking bitwise/parity regression tests that pin cross-backend agreement.

**Verification evidence.** Stage-level probe (C:/tmp/verify_jax_trace_x64.py, JAX_ENABLE_X64): exact-tangency ray (R=0.5, x0=0.5, dir +z; disc computes to exactly 0.0 in float64) -> NumPy _intersect_surface: alive=[True], err=[0], lands z=0.5; JAX _intersect_jax: alive=[False] (miss |= ~(disc>0) at jax_trace.py:246/265; same pattern in _intersect_jax_param:1403/1425/1415 -- t_sphere zeroed at disc==0 too). Code cross-check: intersection.py:149 'disc_pos = disc >= 0' with the v5.4.6 comment explicitly accepting tangency. DOE half, end-to-end: order-1 grating with period_x == wavelength (dL=1.0 exactly, sumsq==1.0) -> NumPy trace(): alive=[True], L=[1.0], N=[0.0], err=[0]; trace_jax AND trace_jax_with_params: alive=[False] (jax_trace.py:463 'propagating = sumsq < 1.0' vs trace.py:184 '_evan = _sumsq > 1.0'). Both halves identical under float32 and float64. Caveat that keeps this P3: in the rim-tangency repro the end-to-end NumPy trace ALSO kills the ray one stage later (refract normal degenerates at r==R -> RAY_NAN via intersection.py:336-339), so final alive-counts coincidentally agree there and differ only in error_code/stage; mid-surface exact tangency is essentially unconstructible with unit direction cosines in binary floats. The DOE sumsq==1.0 case, however, is a clean end-to-end alive divergence.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Tried to refute by (a) checking whether the JAX MISS mask elsewhere re-admits disc==0 -- it does not (line 265 and 1425 both feed ~disc_pos into miss); (b) running end-to-end trace() vs trace_jax() on the tangency case hoping parity holds -- final alive agreed but only via an unrelated RAY_NAN kill (err=4, degenerate hemisphere-rim normal), i.e. mechanism differs and any per-stage or error-code parity check still diverges; (c) checking the DOE boundary can only trigger at exact sumsq==1.0 -- constructed it exactly (period==wavelength) and reproduced the alive divergence in both trace_jax and trace_jax_with_params, in both dtypes.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** In both JAX intersect kernels set miss from disc < 0 while keeping the sqrt double-where on disc > 0 (accepting t = -b/2 at disc==0), and change _apply_doe_kick_jax to propagating = sumsq <= 1.0 to match trace.py's strict > 1.0 evanescent test.

### [P3-59] JAX aspheric Newton (fixed 8 iterations) silently accepts unconverged intersections; NumPy path kills the same rays as RAY_MISSED_SURFACE

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/jax_trace.py:285`  |  **Category:** gap  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The NumPy Newton loop (intersection.py:216-252) tracks per-ray convergence (|dt|<1e-15 with a stuck-with-residual check) and marks never-converged rays dead. The JAX kernels run jax.lax.fori_loop for exactly _ASPHERIC_NEWTON_ITERS=8 iterations (lines 285 and 1449) and afterwards filter only NaN/Inf t (miss | ~isfinite(t)); a finite-but-unconverged t (steep asphere, near-grazing incidence, bad initial guess after the DOE kick) is accepted and the ray lands off-surface, then refracts with a normal evaluated at the wrong point. The docstring says '8 is plenty for typical optical aspheres' but does not document the accept-vs-kill asymmetry with the NumPy reference.

**Failure scenario.** A high-departure asphere (large A4/A6 at the aperture edge) where Newton needs >8 iterations for edge rays: trace() flags those rays RAY_MISSED_SURFACE while trace_jax() keeps them alive with a residual sag error, contaminating jax.grad-driven merit functions with silently wrong edge-ray OPD instead of a detectable dead-ray count.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (steep quartic asphere, oblique fan of 40 rays h=1-20 mm, float64 JAX): A4=1e8 m^-3, L=0.9: NumPy trace() kills 40/40 as RAY_MISSED_SURFACE (err=3); trace_jax() keeps 40/40 alive, example landing residual |z - sag| = 8.42e-1 m off-surface -- a physically meaningless point silently fed to refraction. A4=1e6, tilt 0.9: 34/40 rays NumPy-dead & JAX-alive, residual ~9.0e-4 m. Divergence goes BOTH directions: A4=-1e8, tilt 0.9: 20/40 NumPy-dead & JAX-alive but JAX residual ~1.0e-8 m, i.e. JAX genuinely converged on rays the NumPy |dt|<1e-15 criterion killed as unconverged. Code: jax.lax.fori_loop(0, _ASPHERIC_NEWTON_ITERS=8, ...) at jax_trace.py:285 and 1449 followed only by miss |= ~isfinite(t) (292, 1452) -- no residual/convergence mask; intersection.py:216-252 tracks per-ray converged and kills ~converged as missed_final.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Tried to refute by (a) checking whether the post-loop isfinite filter catches divergent Newton -- it only catches NaN/Inf, and the probe produced finite t with 0.84 m residual; (b) checking whether NumPy's kills were actually disc<0 misses rather than non-convergence -- surfaces were flat+asph (missed_init identically zero), so all kills came from the converged mask; (c) re-running under JAX_ENABLE_X64 to rule out a float32 artifact -- identical mismatch counts and residuals; (d) checking whether the rays genuinely miss the surface -- for A4>0 the quartic sag must eventually cross any upward oblique ray, so an intersection exists and NumPy's kill is a Newton failure, JAX's accept is off-surface. Severity kept at P3: triggering requires an extreme asphere (A4>=1e6 m^-3) with ~64-degree incidence; typical prescriptions converge in well under 8 iterations on both backends.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** After the fori_loop, evaluate the residual F(t) once and fold miss |= (|F| > tol) into the alive mask (mirroring the NumPy stuck-with-residual kill), and/or document the accept-vs-kill asymmetry; separately consider relaxing NumPy's |dt| < 1e-15 criterion (e.g. ulp-scaled), since it kills rays the JAX kernel demonstrably converges on.

### [P3-60] _surface_copy_with drops is_coordbrk/tilt/decenter/coordbrk_order/world_origin/world_R despite docstring claiming it propagates all optional fields

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/surface.py:440`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The clone helper enumerates fields by hand and omits the coord-break block (is_coordbrk, tilt_*_deg, decenter_*_m, coordbrk_order) and the world-frame fields (world_origin, world_R). A cloned coord-break Surface silently becomes a regular flat refracting surface; a cloned world-frame surface loses its frame and makes trace_world raise. Current callers (trace_prescription image-distance clone at trace.py:1076, seidel.py:408/700/742 paraxial sub-system clones) are mostly paraxial where the loss is benign, but seidel.py:700/742 clone arbitrary user surface lists, so a folded (coord-break-bearing) list passed to compute_pupils turns the break into a refracting flat in the cloned ABCD walk instead of being skipped as a coordbrk.

**Failure scenario.** surfaces containing a coordbrk before the stop -> compute_pupils clones them via _surface_copy_with -> the clone has is_coordbrk=False; any code path that later traces or kind-dispatches on the clone treats a pure frame transform as an optical surface. Also a latent tripwire for any future caller cloning coordbrk/world surfaces (the docstring promises safety it doesn't deliver).

**Verification evidence.** Probe (py -P, repo root): Surface(is_coordbrk=True, tilt_x_deg=5.0, decenter_x_m=1e-3, coordbrk_order=1, world_origin=[0,0,1], world_R=eye(3)) -> _surface_copy_with(s) returns is_coordbrk=False, tilt_x_deg=0.0, decenter_x_m=0.0, coordbrk_order=0, world_origin=None, world_R=None. Code trace: surface.py:450-472 Surface(...) call enumerates only radius/conic/aspheric/semi_diameter/glass/is_mirror/is_stop/thickness/label/surf_num/radius_y/conic_y/aspheric_coeffs_y/freeform/bsdf; the coord-break block (surface.py:183-191) and world fields (surface.py:227-228) are absent, while the docstring at line 443 claims 'Propagates all optional fields'. No test pins coordbrk/world survival through the clone (tests/unit/test_audit_misc.py:5404/5632 cover only the thickness-clone behavior). IMPORTANT DEMOTION OF THE FAILURE SCENARIO: the seidel.py:408/700/742 clones feed ONLY system_abcd, where a de-flagged coordbrk (radius=inf, non-mirror) takes the R_mat=eye(2) branch + transfer t/index(glass_after) (seidel.py:202-213) — byte-identical to the coordbrk skip branch (seidel.py:169-178, also t/index(glass_after)). So compute_pupils/lens_abcd/Seidel outputs are numerically unchanged. The trace.py:1076 clone cannot see a coordbrk or world-frame surface because trace_prescription's list comes from surfaces_from_prescription (trace.py:498-516), which never emits is_coordbrk/world fields (coordbrk producers are io/prescriptions_zemax.py:165 dict-level and ui/model.py:2099, which calls trace() directly).

**Refutation attempts (survived).** (1) Checked whether the docstring only promises the enumerated fields — the parenthetical lists five fields, but the lead sentence 'Propagates all optional fields' plus 'lightweight drop-in for dataclasses.replace' overstates; mismatch stands. (2) Tried to show the compute_pupils failure is real: traced both system_abcd branches for a coordbrk vs a de-flagged flat clone — identical matrices (zero power, same t/n transfer), so the claimed 'refracting flat in the cloned ABCD walk' has zero numerical effect; that escalation is refuted. (3) Tried to reach the trace.py:1076 clone with a coordbrk/world surface — impossible via trace_prescription since surfaces_from_prescription never sets those fields. (4) Searched tests for a guard pinning clone fidelity — none exists. Net: the field drop and docstring violation are airtight; every currently-reachable caller is numerically benign, leaving it a latent tripwire.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Replace the hand-rolled constructor call with dataclasses.replace(surf, **overrides) (or add the six missing fields with getattr fallbacks) and correct the docstring's field list.

### [P3-61] _register_fixed_index permanently mutates the global GLASS_REGISTRY/_glass_cache per raytrace_system call using id(elem)-derived names — 'temporary' entries are never removed and id() reuse can alias a retained Surface to the wrong index

- **Location:** `lumenairy/raytrace/trace.py:1313`  |  **Category:** memory  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** _register_fixed_index (trace.py:1303-1314) writes GLASS_REGISTRY[name] and _glass_cache[name] for names like f'__spherical_{id(elem)}' (lines 1243-1245, 1270-1271) on every spherical/aspheric element conversion. The docstring says 'temporary' but there is no unregister path and no cache-registry enrollment, so a long-running loop over prescriptions accumulates registry entries bounded only by CPython address reuse. Because CPython recycles id() values, a Surface retained from an earlier conversion can silently resolve its glass_after to a LATER element's index if a new element dict lands at the same address and overwrites the shared key.

**Failure scenario.** GUI/optimizer loop: call raytrace_system on system A (n=1.5), keep its Surface list; the elem dicts are freed; build system B whose elem dict reuses the same id() with n=1.9 -> re-tracing the retained system-A surfaces now looks up n=1.9. Also steady global-dict growth across thousands of conversions.

**Verification evidence.** Probe C:/tmp/verify_fixed_index_leak.py (run `py -P` from repo root): (1) GROWTH: 2000 spherical_lens conversions grew GLASS_REGISTRY 78->2078 and _glass_cache 1->2001; all 2000 '__spherical_*' keys still registered afterwards — no cleanup path exists (repo-wide grep: the keys appear ONLY at registration sites trace.py:1243/1270/1313-1314). (2) ALIASING: after `del elemA`, a new elem dict reused the same id() on ATTEMPT 0 (CPython dict free-list makes reuse essentially deterministic); the retained system-A Surface's glass_after '__spherical_<id>' then resolved via get_glass_index to n=1.9 instead of the original n=1.5. (3) WRONG PHYSICS: re-tracing the retained system-A surfaces vs a fresh n=1.5 conversion gave max|dL|=max|dM|=7.881e-2 direction-cosine error and 6.4e-5 m image-height error, silently. Bonus code-trace: trace.py's sentinel ('__fixed__','__fixed__','__fixed__') does NOT match glass.py:1384-1386's user-fixed branch (which requires entry[0]=='__user__'), so resolution succeeds only accidentally via the pre-seeded _glass_cache hit at glass.py:1447-1452 — on an install without the refractiveindex package the tuple branch would raise ImportError (glass.py:1438) despite the cache entry existing.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched whole repo for any unregister/cleanup of '__spherical_'/'__aspheric_'/'_register_fixed_index' keys — none; only registration sites and a v5.1 release note listing the function. Checked user_library.py's register_fixed_glass cleanup machinery and glass.py's _glass_value_cache staleness protections (glass.py:1212-1220) — they cover the '__user__' sentinel path only, not trace.py's '__fixed__' sentinel, so no guard applies. Checked whether get_glass_index re-resolution could avoid the stale cache — no: trace() resolves names at trace time (trace.py:125-126) through the overwritten _glass_cache entry. Checked whether retaining surfaces is an unsupported workflow — no: raytrace_system's docstring explicitly returns the converted Surface list as 'useful for further analysis'. Ran the probe expecting id() reuse to be rare — it succeeded on attempt 0.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Key the pseudo-glass by a module-level monotonic counter instead of id(elem) (eliminating aliasing), and either resolve the fixed index locally without touching the global registry or remove the temporary entries after surface conversion/tracing.

### [P3-62] WaveOpticsWorker shadows QThread's built-in finished signal with a custom Signal(object)

- **Location:** `lumenairy/ui/waveoptics_dock.py:388`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** Line 388 declares `finished = Signal(object)` on a QThread subclass, shadowing QThread.finished(). Any standard cleanup idiom (worker.finished.connect(worker.deleteLater), or Qt-internal users of the no-arg finished) now binds to the custom signal, which is emitted only by the explicit emit at line 1001 — i.e. NOT emitted if run() raises (see finding 1) whereas the real QThread.finished always fires. All sibling docks avoid this by naming their result signal finished_result (caustic_dock.py:248, coherence_dock, ao_dock, coronagraph_dock) — this dock drifted.

**Failure scenario.** Future maintenance adds the canonical `self._worker.finished.connect(self._worker.deleteLater)` -> deleteLater is invoked with the results object silently discarded when run succeeds, and never invoked when run raises; also masks the reliable thread-done notification that could have prevented the hang in finding 1.

**Verification evidence.** waveoptics_dock.py:388 declares `finished = Signal(object)` on a QThread subclass, emitted only explicitly inside run(). Behavioral probe (C:/tmp/probe_wo_thread.py): with run() raising, thread reaches isFinished()=True but the slot connected via worker.finished.connect never fires ('slot fired: []') — i.e. the shadow masks the always-fires built-in QThread.finished, which is exactly the mechanism that turns finding 1's exception into a permanent UI hang. Naming drift confirmed: 14 sibling worker classes use finished_result (caustic_dock.py:35, coherence_dock.py:30/57, ao_dock.py:86, coronagraph_dock.py:158, ghost_dock.py:43, through_focus_dock.py:40, richards_wolf_dock.py:67, psf_mtf_dock.py:38, phase_retrieval_dock.py:180, multiconfig_dock.py:33, wavefront_map_dock.py:63, optimizer_dock.py:1138). One inaccuracy in the claim: tolerance_dock.py:41 ALSO declares `finished = Signal(object)`, so waveoptics is not the only drifter.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Checked whether PySide6 treats the redeclaration as a harmless overload where the no-arg built-in still delivers to a connected 1-arg slot: ran the live probe — it does not deliver; the class-level Signal(object) fully masks the built-in for `self.finished` connect/emit. Checked whether the claim 'all sibling docks avoid this' holds: mostly, but tolerance_dock.py:41 shares the same shadow (weakens the wording, not the finding). Confirmed no existing connect to deleteLater relies on the built-in today, so current harm is latent (maintenance trap + it enabled the finding-1 hang), consistent with P3.

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Rename the signal to finished_result (matching all other docks) and update the emit sites plus the single connect at waveoptics_dock.py:2465; optionally also connect the now-unmasked built-in QThread.finished to a UI-reset handler as a safety net (and apply the same rename to tolerance_dock).

### [P3-63] WaveOpticsWorker.run reads the live GUI-thread SystemModel (self.model) throughout the background run instead of a snapshot

- **Location:** `lumenairy/ui/waveoptics_dock.py:471`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** The worker stores the SystemModel itself (392) and, on the background thread, calls self.model.build_trace_surfaces() (471), reads self.model.wavelength_m (427), self.model.source (522), self.model.to_prescription() (584) and self.model.lens_options (589) at different times during a run. The GUI thread remains fully interactive (only btn_run is disabled), so the user can edit surfaces/wavelength mid-run: the worker then mixes pre-edit and post-edit state (e.g. surfaces built from the old design but prescription exported from the new one), or races a list mutation and dies with an exception. Sibling docks avoid this: coronagraph_dock snapshots all widget state into a plain dict before starting (423, 'no reference into the GUI beyond signal connections' comment at 163), and coherence/caustic pass copied params.

**Failure scenario.** User starts a real_lens_traced run, then deletes an element while it runs -> to_prescription() at line 584 exports the mutated system while trace_surfs (line 471) reflects the old one -> silently inconsistent propagation result, or IndexError mid-run.

**Verification evidence.** Code trace is airtight. waveoptics_dock.py:2462 passes the live model (`WaveOpticsWorker(self.sm, config)`); worker stores it uncopied (392). Background-thread reads span the WHOLE run: wavelength_m (427), len(self.model.elements) (453), build_trace_surfaces() (471), epd_m (509), source (522), to_prescription() (584, 653), and — after the long propagation loop — bfl_mm (802), efl_mm (871), to_prescription() (922, embedded into the saved HDF5/Zarr metadata). model.py:1941-1946 shows build_trace_surfaces() serves a cache that any GUI edit invalidates (model.py:1936 _invalidate()), so a mid-run edit makes the late calls rebuild from the NEW design while the worker's local trace_surfs reflects the OLD one — the exact mixed-state failure claimed; the worker also writes _flat_surfaces_cache from the background thread while the GUI thread can clear it (cross-thread race). Only btn_run/btn_stop are toggled (2454-2455); system_changed is wired only to _refresh_planes (1667); main_window.py has no run-time edit freeze. Bonus: lines 424-486 including the 471 build call sit OUTSIDE the try at 493 / except at 998, so a racing-mutation exception there never emits `finished` and leaves btn_run permanently disabled. Sibling pattern confirmed: coronagraph_dock.py:160-164 worker takes a snapshot params dict ('no Qt objects survive into the worker beyond the signal connections'), built by _collect_params() (770-800). No runtime probe run — a Qt mid-run-edit race repro would be flaky, and every load-bearing fact (which reads are live, when they execute, absence of guards) is statically decidable.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched main_window.py for any run-in-progress edit lock (none — only undo/redo enable toggles); checked whether system_changed stops or guards the worker (it only triggers _refresh_planes at 1667); checked whether build_trace_surfaces()/to_prescription() internally deep-copy or pin state (opposite: cached list is rebuilt from current elements after any invalidation, worsening mixing); grepped for tests referencing WaveOpticsWorker (zero); checked the prior GUI audit doc AUDIT_V5_3_2_GUI_VS_LIBRARY for this being documented-as-intended (it isn't — it flags general worker-hygiene gaps).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Adopt the coronagraph_dock pattern: in _run(), snapshot everything the worker needs into plain data (wavelength, epd, source copy, trace_surfs list from build_trace_surfaces(), pres = to_prescription(), lens_options deep-copy, bfl/efl) and pass only that dict into WaveOpticsWorker, removing all self.model reads from run().

### [P3-64] GATE 4a asserts min(|S11|-rTM, |S11|-rTE) without classifying mode polarization, so a TE/TM-swap bug passes the gate its docstring calls 'rigorous' and 'load-bearing'

- **Location:** `tests/unit/test_bor_solve.py:72`  |  **Category:** consistency  |  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Claim.** test_gate4a_planar_fresnel_correspondence (test_bor_solve.py:72) checks each propagating cylindrical mode's |S11[j,j]| against BOTH the planar TM and TE Fresnel coefficients and passes if EITHER matches within 1e-3. Because no polarization classification is performed, an interface_smatrix bug that maps TM-like modes with the TE admittance (or any systematic TE<->TM swap) satisfies the assert for every mode. The module docstring overstates this as 'rigorous ... the load-bearing per-mode physics', unlike the sibling min()-pattern in test_zcascade.py:98-99/166-167 which is explicitly documented as 'circular, necessary-not-sufficient'. The pol-classifying helper (_pol_fraction via <|E_phi|^2>/<|E|^2>) already exists in test_gate4.py and works on the same half-space basis, so a per-pol assert is cheap. Mitigation: the slower grating-level test_gate4 does classify polarization and compares per-order against the matching planar oracle, so a swap is not entirely uncaught by the suite — hence P3 not P2.

**Failure scenario.** A refactor of interface_smatrix (or of the mode admittance V) that exchanges the TE and TM impedance mapping for m!=0 modes: every mode still reflects with A Fresnel coefficient, just the wrong pol's one. GATE 4a stays green (min over both candidates), energy stays 1, and only the ~2-minute slow test_gate4 grating comparison would flag it — reducing the intended fast per-mode anchor to a non-discriminating check.

**Verification evidence.** Probe (C:/tmp/probe_bor/probe_gate4a_swap.py, N=150 and N=200, same e1=4.0/e2=2.25/m=1/R=5/k0=2 as the test): all 9 checked modes have |rTM-rTE| = 0.0029..0.233, i.e. 3x-230x the 1e-3 tolerance, and a simulated swapped solver (wrong pol's Fresnel coefficient, same per-mode match error 2e-6..2.9e-4) passes every assert at line 72 plus n_checked>=5 ('SWAPPED solver: all min() < 1e-3 -> True'). Second probe (probe_polmix.py) shows the gate is WEAKER than claimed: the Er-dominant TM-like candidates carry reldiv 2.0-10.5 and are all dropped by _physical_propagating's reldiv<0.5 filter (bor_solve.py:57-59); every surviving checked mode is Ephi-dominant (frac_phi 0.64-0.98) and matches rTE, so the rTM branch of the min() never fires — GATE 4a validates only the TE coefficient in practice. Docstring cross-refs verified: test_bor_solve.py:6-11 says 'rigorous ... the load-bearing per-mode physics'; test_zcascade.py:10-11 labels the identical min() pattern (lines 98-99, 166-167) 'circular, necessary-not-sufficient'; _pol_fraction exists at test_gate4.py:52 on the same half-space basis.

**Refutation attempts (survived).** Searched the whole test for any polarization guard or classification — none exists. Checked whether rTE~rTM at the sampled angles (which would make min() harmless) — refuted by probe: differences up to 0.233 at the 46-deg mode, and test_gate4a_oblique_angles_span guarantees a >20-deg spread, so the coefficients genuinely diverge. Verified the claimed suite-level mitigation: test_gate4.py classifies each mode via _pol_fraction and compares per-pol per-order efficiencies against the matching planar pmm_efficiency_1d oracle at 1.5-2.5% tolerance, so a systematic TE/TM swap would still be caught by the slow grating test — mitigation confirmed, which is why this stays P3 (test-discrimination gap with docstring overstatement, not an uncaught physics bug).

**Fix direction (NOT implemented).** Classify each checked mode with the _pol_fraction technique from test_gate4.py and assert |S11[j,j]| against the single matching Fresnel coefficient (note the TM-like modes are reldiv-filtered on this nodal basis, so either lower the filter for uniform half-spaces or state that only TE is anchored), and soften the module docstring's 'rigorous/load-bearing' claim to match test_zcascade's 'necessary-not-sufficient' wording.

## Appendix: refuted candidates

### [REFUTED] Mainline wave-leg OPD aperture uses falsy `or`, shadowing a deliberate aperture_diameter=0 — the exact bug class the v4.14.1 _ZERO_APERTURE_MASK sentinel machinery was built to prevent

- **Location:** `lumenairy/optimize/driver.py:750`  |  **Category:** consistency

**Original claim.** `ap = pres.get('aperture_diameter') or (0.4 * N * dx)` treats a deliberate 0 the same as None and substitutes the 0.4*N*dx grid fallback for wave_opd_2d. The wrapper merits went through a documented three-release fix cycle (wrapper_merits.py:192-201, 396-399: 'explicit is-None check ... so a deliberate 0 isn't shadowed') establishing that aperture_diameter<=0 means 'block all light', with a dedicated sentinel; the driver's own mainline evaluate() missed that sweep. With ap=0 the field out of apply_real_lens is fully blocked, so wave_opd_2d is fed a zero field with a fictitious 0.4*N*dx aperture — its output OPD map is fit noise rather than the None that OPD-consuming merits are documented to treat as 'contribute 0'.

**Why refuted.** The falsy-`or` at driver.py:750 exists as described, but the claimed failure state is unreachable — line 750 can never see aperture_diameter=0. Line 750 only executes when ctx.bfl is finite and |bfl|<10 (gate at driver.py:720), which requires the ray leg to have succeeded; the ray leg calls `_core.surfaces_from_prescription(pres)` at driver.py:653 OUTSIDE the exception-swallowing try, and surfaces_from_prescription (trace.py:424) runs validate_prescription(strict=True), which hard-rejects any non-positive/non-finite aperture_diameter (trace.py:377-387). Probe A (singlet, aperture_diameter=0.0, needs_wave merit): design_optimize raises loudly — traceback frames driver.py:653 -> trace.py:424 -> trace.py:391 'aperture_diameter: must be positive and finite; got 0.0' — before ever reaching line 750. Probe B (needs_ray=False so validation is skipped): bfl stays at the 1e9 sentinel, evaluate() early-returns at the line-720 gate, and ctx.opd_map remains None (the documented 'contribute 0'), line 750 again never executed. Probe C (aperture_diameter=None control): opd_map populated normally via the intended 0.4*N*dx fallback. So ctx.opd_map is never populated from a zero field with a fictitious aperture; the claimed 'fit-noise OPD map injecting gradient noise near lb=0' cannot occur — an aperture free variable hitting exactly 0 produces a hard, well-labelled ValueError instead.

### [REFUTED] Module-scope 'import scipy.linalg' + 'import scipy.special' in backend/scipy.py accounts for the majority of 'import lumenairy' time, contradicting the backend package's documented lazy-import design

- **Location:** `lumenairy/backend/scipy.py:21`  |  **Category:** perf

**Original claim.** backend/array.py (lines 41-45) documents that heavy dependencies are loaded lazily via _get_*() accessors, and backend/scipy.py follows that pattern for JAX (lines 34-47) -- but eagerly imports scipy.linalg and scipy.special at module scope (lines 21-22). lumenairy/__init__.py eagerly imports elements -> rcwa._core -> lumenairy.backend, so every 'import lumenairy' pays the full scipy.linalg chain (BLAS/LAPACK extension modules plus scipy._lib._util -> scipy._lib.array_api_compat.numpy, which drags numpy.testing (~62 ms) and the entire numpy.f2py package (~80 ms incl. 8+ ms crackfortran)). Measured with py -X importtime: lumenairy.backend.scipy cumulative = 296 ms of the 497 ms total package import (~60%); scipy.fft (~40 ms) would still load via propagators._bluestein, so deferring linalg/special saves roughly 250 ms per process. All six public functions in the module already dispatch per-call, so moving the two imports behind the existing _get_*() accessor pattern is behavior-preserving.

**Why refuted.** Imports exist as cited (backend/scipy.py:21-22) and importtime attribution reproduces (cold run: lumenairy.backend.scipy cumulative 711 ms of 1240 ms total, with scipy._lib._util -> array_api_compat.numpy 478 ms -> numpy.f2py 286 ms nested under scipy.linalg). BUT the attribution is a first-importer artifact: 'import lumenairy' ALSO eagerly imports scipy.linalg via elements/__init__.py:106 'from .pmm import' -> pmm/__init__.py:12 'from ._core import *' -> pmm/_core.py:12 'import scipy.linalg as sla' (plus pmm/twod_staggered.py:72, eagerly imported at elements/__init__.py:123). Counterfactual probe (median of 7 warm subprocess runs): 'import lumenairy' as-is 560 ms; 'import scipy.linalg' preloaded then lumenairy 543 ms; linalg+special preloaded 539 ms => making backend/scipy.py lazy saves only ~17-41 ms (scipy.special marginal), NOT ~250 ms, because scipy.linalg loads anyway via pmm. Additionally, the 'documented lazy-import design' (backend/array.py:41-45) explicitly scopes laziness to 'Heavy OPTIONAL dependencies (CuPy, JAX)' — backend/scipy.py already lazies JAX; scipy is a hard dependency (pyproject.toml:51 'scipy>=1.7'), so no design contradiction exists.
